# Eduportal NG — Remediation PRD
**Companion document to:** `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md` (61-table schema, §1–§13)
**Purpose:** This PRD does not replace the master plan. It is the execution-order layer on top of it: it takes every problem reported by the owner after live use, traces each one to its actual root cause inside the existing architecture/codebase, and sequences the fixes by **technical dependency**, not by how the bugs were noticed or how annoying they are.
**How to use this document:** Work top to bottom. Do not start a Tier until every item in the previous Tier is closed AND on-device verified (see §0.2 — this is the rule the master plan's own session log shows being violated 31 times in a row). Each problem item states: root cause, master-plan sections to keep open while working, exact files touched, and an explicit Definition of Done. A problem is not done when the code is written — it is done when the Definition of Done is observed on a real device by a real second role.

---

## 0. How this PRD is organized

### 0.1 Triage logic
Problems are grouped into **Tiers**. A Tier is a set of problems that share a common technical prerequisite. Lower tiers must be structurally sound before higher tiers are attempted, because higher-tier fixes either (a) read data that doesn't exist until the lower tier writes it, (b) render inside a shell that doesn't route correctly until the lower tier fixes routing, or (c) get silently re-broken every time the lower tier's bug reoccurs.

| Tier | Theme | Why it must go first |
|---|---|---|
| **0** | Verification discipline | Without this, every later tier risks being marked "done" without ever being tested — exactly what produced the current state |
| **1** | Identity & routing foundation | Every role-specific view, every restriction, every dashboard depends on knowing *who is logged in and what they're allowed to see* — nothing above this layer can be trusted until it's solid |
| **2** | Onboarding completion & app-shell stability | The wizard-loop-on-reload bug sits between login and every other screen; nothing downstream can be tested by a real user until this is fixed |
| **3** | Permission enforcement (the `permissions` catalog → UI) | Role-based dashboards, restrictions, and "different views per actor type" are all the same underlying gap: the UI never reads `role_permissions`. Fix the mechanism once, then every dashboard tier below just *consumes* it |
| **4** | Role-specific dashboards & views (Admin, Teacher, Guardian, Student) | Depends entirely on Tier 1 (identity) + Tier 3 (permission reads) |
| **5** | Module-level data correctness (Attendance save bug, school-calendar awareness, Students photo/analytics) | Depends on Tier 4 existing as a real screen to test inside |
| **6** | Design-system port & visual pass (token system, icon set, live-period widget, popups, settings/alarm, term-progress — see §0.4) | Deliberately after Tier 5 among "must-fix" items — re-skinning or adding widgets to a screen before its logic is correct means re-doing the work once the logic changes. Absorbs and supersedes the original "visual/design system pass" scope. |
| **7** | Public-facing & platform-level additions (pre-login homepage, i18n system) | Net-new scope, not bug fixes — correctly sequenced last because they are additive surfaces that don't block anything else, and i18n in particular should wrap finished, stable UI text — not text that's still being rewritten |
| **8** | Launch-blocking issues (first real school's enrollment) | Added 2026-07-11 per the owner's launch-focus pivot: Rows 28/33/35–43 are owner-deferred to *after* the first real school's launch (see the note above the tracker table), and this tier is opened to receive whatever issues the owner and I identify together as genuinely blocking that first real enrollment. Sequenced last in this table only because it was opened last — in practice it now takes priority over the deferred rows above it, since those are explicitly on hold until this tier's scope is live and stable. **Re-sequenced internally, 2026-07-12:** the owner confirmed the actual first enrolling school is a higher institution, not a secondary school. Problems 8.1/8.2 (identity bugs, school-type-agnostic) remain unaffected and proceed as scoped. Problems 8.3/8.4/8.6 (Assessment Window, Scheme of Work) were designed against the secondary-school academic model and are **paused, fully designed, not cancelled** — same "owner-deferred, not dropped" status already used for Rows 28/33/35–43 — resumed once higher-institution support is live and stable. **Problem 8.7 (higher-institution support) now leads the tier's remaining build work**, since it is what the real enrolling school actually needs. |

### 0.2 The verification rule (read this before touching any tier)
The master plan's own progress log (§11) shows the same failure repeated at least 15 times: a file is generated, reviewed against the schema, the roadmap checkbox in §10 is ticked `[x]`, and the file is *never opened by a real device, real second role, or real data* before the next session builds on top of it. This is the actual root cause of most of the owner's 15 reported problems — not any single line of broken code.

**Every Definition of Done in this PRD requires one or more of:**
- On-device test in the actual mobile browser (not just `npm run dev` succeeding)
- A second/third real role logged in (not just admin — Tier 1 exists specifically because no non-admin role has ever been tested end-to-end)
- `npm run build` (not just `npm run dev`) run clean before any git push — the master plan's own §11 entry for 2026-06-26 names this exact gap: *"`npm run dev` will never catch `noUnusedLocals`, union type mismatches, or missing namespace errors."*

A checkbox in §10 of the master plan must never be ticked from this PRD's work unless the Definition of Done below it was actually observed, not assumed.

**Clarification, added 2026-07-01 after a prior session over-applied this rule:** the line above restricts *premature or unverified* completion claims — it is not a statement that this PRD document itself is read-only or externally maintained. §10 (Session Task Breakdown) belongs to this file, including its per-session instruction blocks and the summary execution-order table. Once a session's Definition of Done has genuinely been observed (on-device test passed, or the specific criteria that Done Check names), the corresponding row in §10's summary table should be updated to ✅ Complete with a dated, detailed entry — matching the standard already set by rows like Session 6, 7b, 8, and 9. A prior session incorrectly concluded from this rule that the PRD "does not track completion status itself" and left a genuinely-completed Session 10 row sitting at ⬜ Not started — that was wrong; do not repeat it. The one instruction still worth honoring literally: don't rewrite a session's "Exactly what to do" instruction block as if it were a narrated history — that stays as forward-looking instructions. The summary table row for that same session, and any status prose immediately around it, is fair game to update once the work is real.

### 0.3 Master-plan sections every tier must keep in view
Regardless of which problem is being worked, these are never re-litigated and must be re-read before writing code that touches them:
- §3 (Architecture decisions, all 16) — especially §3.2 (RLS is the single point of failure), §3.7 (custom roles/permissions), §3.15 (two tiers of admin), §3.16 (`school_type`/`node_type` are inert — never branch logic on them)
- §4 (UX principles) — the non-negotiable list the owner's complaints map onto almost one-for-one
- §5 (Native app strategy) — no browser-only APIs in business logic, business logic stays out of components, Zustand only
- §7 (Schema) — never invent a column or table without flagging it; this PRD flags every net-new table/column explicitly so nothing is invented silently
- §12 (Key Q&A) — locked decisions; do not re-ask answered questions while fixing bugs

### 0.4 Tier 6 source: the reference app (`index__17_.html`)
On 2026-07-01 the owner supplied a working, previously-shipped single-file app (the live Dilimi madrasah app referenced in master-plan §1/§2 as "old, live, untouched") and initially named seven specific pieces of it to be ported into this project's design language. On a second pass, the owner clarified this list was illustrative, not exhaustive: the actual ask is for "the design format and concepts... the UI... the flows, everything UI" — i.e. the reference app's entire structural/visual language, not just the seven named widgets. This is a **source of proven UI/UX patterns to port, not a codebase to merge** — the target app is React/TypeScript/Zustand per §5, the source is vanilla JS/HTML. Every item below is a *reimplementation against the same visual and behavioral spec*, not a copy-paste.

Each of the original seven asks is a real, self-contained, working piece of that file — verified by direct inspection, not assumed from the ask's wording:

| # | Owner's ask | Confirmed source in `index__17_.html` |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Overall design language (colors, timetable, login page) | `:root` token block (emerald/parchment/gold palette + dark-theme overrides), `.att-login-card` login pattern, `.tbl-wrap`/`.cell` timetable grid |
| 2 | "Current periods across all classes" live summary above the timetable | `.live-banner` widget + `getState()`/`updateWidget()`/`gridHtml()`/`tick()` — ticks every second, shows what's running right now in every class simultaneously |
| 3 | Icon set instead of multicolored emoji | `const ICONS = {...}` — ~45 hand-authored monochrome inline SVGs, colored via `currentColor` (no external icon library) |
| 4 | Same coloring, but admin-customizable primary color | The source hardcodes its palette to one school. The target app already has the correct mechanism for this (`--color-brand-600` design token + live `document.documentElement.style.setProperty(...)` on save, confirmed working per master-plan §11 session 24) — this is **not a port**, it's making the ported components consume the token that already exists, per Tier 6's original Problem 6.1 |
| 5 | Popups | `.modal-ov`/`.modal` overlay pattern, reused for period-detail popups, class/teacher pickers, and logout confirmation |
| 6 | Settings, including the alarm | `settingsModal` (dark mode, language, clock format, default day/view, highlight-current-column) + a genuine Web Audio API alarm system: 4 synthesized tone types (chime/bell/buzzer/digital), volume, repeat count, early-warning lead time, scope-by-teacher, browser notifications — zero audio files, all generated in-browser |
| 7 | Visible session/semester display that auto-adjusts with holidays | `.trim-card` progress bar (`buildTrimCardHtml()`) + `attAutoExtendTrimester()`, which extends a term's end date by one school day for every school-day holiday inserted into it |

**One asymmetry flagged, not silently copied:** the source only *extends* a term when a holiday is added — it never *shrinks* the term back when a holiday is deleted (confirmed by reading `attDelHoliday()`/`attDelHolidayGroup()`: they delete the row and reload, with no reverse-extension call). The owner's ask explicitly includes "auto changes with holiday inserted/**removed**" — this is new behavior beyond what the source actually does, not a porting gap. Problem 6.7 below designs the symmetric version deliberately, as a reviewed decision rather than an inherited bug.

**Second-pass inventory (2026-07-01, same day): the reference app is a whole application, not seven widgets.** Re-inspection at the owner's prompting found an entire staff-facing app inside the file that the first pass never catalogued — an app shell everything else renders inside, plus direct or partial equivalents for most of this app's own modules:

| Reference-app pattern family | What it covers | Maps to this app's... | New problem |
|---|---|---|---|
| App shell | Collapsible sidebar (desktop), header (brand/logo/user chip/status badge/logout), bottom nav (mobile), section-switching | The navigation chrome every screen sits inside — `BottomNav.tsx`, header, `AdminDashboard.tsx` | Problem 6.8 |
| Attendance flow | `.att-*` family (~300 class occurrences, the largest in the file): registration grid, mark-all-present/absent, pending-changes save bar, 30-day history strip | `AttendanceModule.tsx` — direct, detailed analog | Problem 6.9 |
| Student management + Announcements | `.stud-popup-*` detail view, printable ID card generator (`.card-*`, dedicated print stylesheet); `.ann-card` compose/list | `StudentsModule.tsx`, `AnnouncementsModule.tsx` — direct analogs; ID cards are a feature this app is missing outright, not just styled differently | Problem 6.10 |
| Results, Finance, HR, Library, Guardian/Student Portal | *No equivalent* — the reference app is a single-purpose Qur'an-memorization madrasah tool with no grading, fees, payroll, or book catalogue | `ResultsModule.tsx`, `FinanceModule.tsx`, `HRModule.tsx`, `LibraryModule.tsx`, `GuardianPortal.tsx` — apply the *pattern language* the other ports establish, since there's nothing module-specific to point at | Problem 6.11 |

This second-pass inventory does not replace the seven-item table above — it adds the structural/flow layer the owner's second clarification asked for. Problems 6.8–6.11 (below, after 6.1–6.7) cover it.

**Master plan sections every Tier 6 session must keep in view**, in addition to §0.3's standing list: §3.16 (do not branch on `school_type`/`node_type`), §4.5 (i18n/RTL — Tier 6 ships components with translatable strings in mind even though the i18n engine itself is Tier 7; do not hardcode Arabic-only strings the way the source app does), §4.7 (consistency), §4.10 (accessibility — the source's `prefers-reduced-motion`, focus-return-on-modal-close, and keyboard-operable switches are worth keeping, not just the visuals).

## TIER 0 — Verification discipline (process fix, not code — do this first, literally today)

### Problem 0.1 — No file has ever been verified the way the master plan claims
**Root cause:** §10 roadmap checkboxes and §13 manifest status columns disagree throughout the document. Example: §10 line marks "Admin settings panel" `[x]` and states "Phase 6 is now fully complete," while §13's own manifest rows for `StudentsModule.tsx`, `attendanceService.ts`, `financeService.ts`, `HRModule.tsx`, `LibraryModule.tsx`, `AnnouncementsModule.tsx`, `GuardianPortal.tsx` all read "Not yet on-device tested" as of the same date.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §0 (how to use the document — step 8: "update §11 with what changed and tick off completed steps in §10"), §11, §13.
**Files touched:** `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md` only (no app code).
**Goals before moving to Tier 1:**
1. Re-audit §10 against §13 row by row. Un-tick any §10 box whose corresponding §13 manifest row does not say "confirmed on-device" with a date.
2. Add one new column to every §13 manifest row: **"Tested as which role"** (admin / teacher / guardian / student / not tested). Today every populated entry will read "admin" or "not tested" — leave it that way; it is supposed to look sparse right now. This column is what Tier 1–4 will fill in.
3. Adopt, in writing inside §0 of the master plan, the rule from PRD §0.2 above (verification rule) as a permanent addition to "How to use this document."

**Definition of Done:** §10 contains zero `[x]` checkboxes that are contradicted by their own §13 row. This is a documentation-only task — no app behavior changes — and should take under an hour, but it is the foundation everything else is measured against.

## TIER 1 — Identity & routing foundation

These problems are grouped first because **every** role-specific complaint (#6, #7, #8) and every restriction complaint (#10) is downstream of the same fact: only the `staff`/admin identity path has ever been exercised. `App.tsx` branches on `actorType`, but `actorType ∈ {guardian, student}` has literally never been reached by a real login, per §11's own session log ("Guardian/student login tabs were not tested — no students enrolled, no guardians registered").

### Problem 1.1 — Teacher account is indistinguishable from Admin account *(user's #6)*
**Root cause:** `App.tsx`'s `isAdmin` check is computed as `actorType === 'staff' || actorType === 'platform_admin'`. This is a **binary** check. It does not look at *which* `role` a staff member holds (`roles.is_admin_role`, `staff_roles`), only whether they are staff at all. A teacher and a school admin are both `actor_type = 'staff'` in `app_identities`, so they are currently the same person to every screen in the app. This is not a bug in any single screen — it is the absence of a second condition everywhere `isAdmin` is read.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §3.7 (roles are data, fully custom per school — there is no hardcoded "teacher" role to special-case), §7.3 (`roles.is_admin_role`, `staff_roles`, `role_permissions`), §3.16 (do not branch on `school_type`/`node_type` — this does not apply here but is a reminder of the same discipline: don't hardcode role *names* either, since roles are custom per school).
**Files touched:**
- `src/stores/authStore.ts` — extend identity resolution to also fetch the staff member's `staff_roles → roles.is_admin_role` (or, correctly per §3.7, their resolved permission set — see Tier 3) at login, not just `actor_type`.
- `src/App.tsx` — every `isAdmin` boolean currently threaded as a prop must become a real permission/role read, not a re-derivation of `actor_type === 'staff'`.
- Every module file currently receiving an `isAdmin` prop (`AdminDashboard.tsx`, `TimetableModule.tsx`, `AttendanceModule.tsx`, `ResultsModule.tsx`, `StudentsModule.tsx`, `FinanceModule.tsx`, `HRModule.tsx`, `LibraryModule.tsx`, `AnnouncementsModule.tsx`) — each currently treats "is staff" as "is admin." This file list is large on purpose: this single root cause is wired into nine separate files.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Decide, and record in master plan §12, the exact resolved-permission shape `authStore` exposes (this is finished properly in Tier 3 — for Tier 1, the minimum bar is: `authStore` must expose `isAdminRole: boolean` derived from `staff_roles.role_id → roles.is_admin_role`, separate from `actorType === 'staff'`).
2. Seed one real non-admin teacher account (a real `staff` row + `staff_roles` row pointing at a role with `is_admin_role = false` + `app_identities` row) on the real school — not the `dilimi` test school.
3. Log in as that teacher on-device. Confirm the UI is visibly different from the admin login at least at the dashboard level (full dashboard vs. restricted view) before any further work proceeds — this is the first time in the project's history this comparison will have been made.
**Definition of Done:** Two side-by-side on-device screenshots — one admin login, one teacher login, same school — showing a different `home` screen. Until this exists, do not proceed to Tier 4's teacher dashboard build, because there is nothing yet to tell the dashboard apart from the admin one.

### Problem 1.2 — No dedicated Guardian or Student dashboard ever actually seen *(user's #8, partially #7)*
**Root cause:** `GuardianPortal.tsx` exists in the codebase (§13 manifest, session 23) and is routed in `App.tsx` for `actorType === 'guardian' || actorType === 'student'`. But per §11's own log, **no guardian or student `app_identities` row has ever existed** for the owner to log in as, on any school, in any session. The component may be correct or may not — it is unverified code, not a missing feature.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.4 ("Guardian/Parent: ... First screen after login = their child's summary, not a menu" and "Student: needs simplicity and a sense of progress"), §4.9 (exact list of what guardians/students may see: results, attendance, fees, announcements, calendar), §7.2 (`app_identities`, `actor_type` check constraint), §7.8 (`student_guardians`, the one-platform-access-guardian-per-student partial unique index).
**Files touched:**
- `src/routes/GuardianPortal.tsx` — read, do not rewrite from memory; per `additional_skills_reminder`-equivalent discipline for this project (master plan §0 step 9), this file must be opened and read before any new portal code is generated.
- `src/services/portalService.ts` — same.
- `src/routes/StudentsModule.tsx` (Guardians tab) — this is the *only* existing UI path that provisions guardian portal access (`provision-guardian-access` Edge Function). Use it to create the first real guardian identity rather than hand-seeding rows, so the actual provisioning flow gets tested at the same time as the portal.
- `supabase/functions/provision-guardian-access/index.ts`, `supabase/functions/provision-student-access/index.ts`, `supabase/functions/login-with-code/index.ts` — confirm these are actually deployed (§13 Edge Functions manifest shows `login-with-code` as "corrected code delivered, redeploy pending" — verify redeploy actually happened, since this exact pattern (claimed-done-but-not-deployed) already happened once with `create-staff-account`, per §11's 2026-06-25 entry).
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Confirm all three Edge Functions above are deployed (check Supabase Dashboard → Edge Functions → invocation logs are non-empty, not just that the deploy button was clicked — this is the exact diagnostic method §11 used to catch the `create-staff-account` false-positive).
2. Provision one real guardian (linked to the real student from Problem 1.1's class, or any real enrolled student) via `StudentsModule.tsx`'s Guardians tab.
3. Provision one real student login (Brief tab → "Set up login," per §11 session 31).
4. Log in as the guardian on-device. Walk all five tabs (Summary, Results, Attendance, Fees, News) and note every error, blank state, or crash — do not fix yet, just produce the list. This list becomes Tier 4's guardian-dashboard backlog.
5. Repeat step 4 as the student.
**Definition of Done:** A written list (can live in master plan §11 as a new dated session entry) of every concrete defect found in `GuardianPortal.tsx` when used for real, by both a guardian and a student identity, on-device. Tier 4 below consumes this list — it does not re-derive it.

### Problem 1.3 — No link/button anywhere to reach the Admin Dashboard *(user's #5)*
**Root cause:** Per §13's `App.tsx` manifest row, `home` is the default tab and routes to `AdminDashboard` only when `isAdmin`. If the owner is experiencing "no link to the dashboard," one of two things is true: (a) `currentTab` state is not resetting to `'home'` after actions elsewhere in the app, so the user gets stranded on a different tab with no way back, or (b) the bottom nav (`BottomNav.tsx`) does not render a `home` icon at all for the admin actor type. Per §13, `BottomNav.tsx` is flagged **"Not yet reached (requires onboarding completion first)"** — meaning this component has *never once been confirmed rendering on a real device*, for any role. This is very likely the actual cause of complaint #5: the nav bar itself may be missing the home tab, mis-rendering, or never reached because of the Tier 2 onboarding-loop bug.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.2 ("Bottom navigation bar for primary routes — the most common actions are thumb-reachable"), §4.4 (admin needs to feel in control, never lost).
**Files touched:** `src/components/BottomNav.tsx` (read first — never confirmed rendering, per §13), `src/App.tsx` (tab-state management).
**Goals before moving on:** This problem cannot be properly diagnosed until Tier 2 (onboarding loop) is fixed, because the owner may never be reaching the main app shell at all on reload — see Problem 2.1. Treat this as **blocked on Tier 2**, but keep `BottomNav.tsx` open and reviewed now so the fix is ready the moment Tier 2 closes.
**Definition of Done:** Deferred — re-opened as the first item once Tier 2 is closed.

## TIER 2 — Onboarding completion & app-shell stability

### Problem 2.1 — Admin lands on the last onboarding step, not the dashboard, including after every reload *(user's #3 and #13's reload half)*
**Root cause:** `App.tsx` routes to `OnboardingWizard.tsx` whenever `schools.onboarding_complete = false`. Per §10 Phase 5, the wizard's own checklist items — "progress saved automatically after every step," "Onboarding wizard... renders before app if false" — are **unchecked** `[ ]` at the top level, and §13's manifest entry for `OnboardingWizard.tsx` reads: *"Wizard launched and navigated. Individual step saves partially confirmed. `completeOnboarding()` + redirect not yet confirmed."* This is the precise mechanism: if `completeOnboarding()` (the function that should set `onboarding_complete = true` in the `schools` row) never successfully runs or never successfully commits, the database still says onboarding is incomplete — so every reload, by design, correctly sends the admin back into the wizard. The wizard is not broken; it is functioning exactly as specified against a flag that was never actually flipped.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.6 (onboarding UX principles — in particular "progress saved automatically after every step... never lose work"), §7.1 (`schools.onboarding_complete boolean not null default false`), §10 Phase 5 exit condition ("app reads subdomain → loads school → shows public homepage → routes to onboarding wizard for new admin" — note this exit condition itself assumes Tier 7's public homepage exists, which it currently does not; flagging this dependency for Tier 7).
**Files touched:**
- `src/services/onboardingService.ts` — locate `completeOnboarding()` (or equivalent final-step handler). Confirm it issues an `update schools set onboarding_complete = true where id = ...` and that the call is awaited and its result checked, not fired-and-forgotten.
- `src/routes/OnboardingWizard.tsx` — confirm the final step's "Finish" action calls this function and only then changes app-level routing state (do not let the UI optimistically advance past the wizard before the DB write is confirmed — this would be the inverse failure mode).
- `src/App.tsx` — confirm the subdomain → school lookup query (per §7.1's note: *"App.tsx needs id and onboarding_complete from this query too"*) is actually selecting `onboarding_complete` and not a stale/cached value from before the wizard ran.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Reproduce live: log in as the real admin, finish the wizard, then immediately query `schools.onboarding_complete` directly in Supabase SQL Editor (bypasses RLS, gives ground truth) — confirm whether it's `true` or `false` right after finishing.
2. If `false`: the bug is in `completeOnboarding()` not running/erroring silently — add explicit error surfacing (per §4.3, "Errors explain and guide... never show a technical error message," but for this diagnostic step, a console log is acceptable since this is a dev-only repro).
3. If `true` but the wizard still reappears on reload: the bug is in `App.tsx`'s routing read, not the write — likely a caching issue (note §4.1 mandates aggressive caching of rarely-changing data; `schools` row may be getting served from a stale cache layer that was never invalidated when `onboarding_complete` flipped).
4. Fix whichever side is confirmed broken. Do not fix both speculatively — confirm with the SQL Editor query first.
**Definition of Done:** Admin completes the wizard once, then **force-reloads the browser tab three separate times** (not just navigates within the app) and lands on the main app shell's `home` tab every time, with `BottomNav.tsx` visible. This also closes Problem 1.3 above — re-test it here.

### Problem 2.2 — Admin dashboard is static; nothing is clickable *(user's #4)*
**Root cause:** §13's manifest entry for `AdminDashboard.tsx` is explicit: the quick actions ("Add student, Record payment, Mark attendance, View timetable") are **"all `// TODO` stubs until those modules exist."** Those modules (`StudentsModule`, `FinanceModule`, `AttendanceModule`, `TimetableModule`) now exist and are wired into `App.tsx`'s tab router — but nothing in the session log shows anyone returning to `AdminDashboard.tsx` after those modules were built to replace the stub `// TODO`s with real navigation calls. This is a stale cross-reference, not a rendering bug: the cards likely render correctly (per §13, confirmed rendering on-device 2026-06-25); they just don't *do* anything when tapped, exactly as documented.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.3 ("the most common action is prominent... progressive disclosure"), §4.11 (admin dashboard is the analytics surface, not a separate module).
**Files touched:** `src/routes/AdminDashboard.tsx` (replace each `// TODO` quick-action stub with a real `onNavigate('students' | 'finance' | 'attendance' | 'timetable')` call — `onNavigate` is already threaded into `TabContent` per the `App.tsx` manifest row, session 13 addition, so this is wiring, not new plumbing).
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Grep the live `AdminDashboard.tsx` for every `// TODO` and enumerate them before touching anything — there may be more than the four quick actions listed in §13's summary.
2. Wire each to `onNavigate(...)`.
3. Confirm the stat cards themselves (attendance %, fee collection, pending approvals) are reading **real** data and not placeholder zeros — §13 doesn't flag this as broken, but it was also never tested with real attendance/fee data in the system (none existed at time of writing per §11), so it must be re-verified now that Tier 5 will have produced real attendance/fee rows.
**Definition of Done:** Every visible element on the admin dashboard that looks tappable (has a hover/pressed state, a card border, an icon) actually navigates somewhere or performs an action, verified by tapping each one on-device.

### Problem 2.2a — Person icon and "More" button lead to the same destination *(flagged 2026-06-30, post-Session-7 walkthrough)*
**Root cause:** `App.tsx`'s header renders a 👤 button labeled `aria-label="Account"` that calls `setActiveTab('more')` — the exact same state transition the bottom nav's "More" tab triggers. There is no separate account surface; the label is the only thing distinguishing the two entry points, and it is currently inaccurate (it does not open anything account-specific). Inspecting the `'more'` tab's render branch in `App.tsx` confirms the underlying information-architecture problem this surfaces: the full-screen More view already mixes two different *kinds* of content in one list — school modules (Results, Library, Announcements, HR, Guardians, and admin-only Settings/Setup guide/Audit log) sitting in the same scroll alongside `SignOutButton`, which is a personal/account action, not a module. The duplication the owner noticed is a symptom of this deeper mixing, not just two buttons with one job.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.7 (one product, everywhere — navigation must work the same, predictable way; two icons doing one job violates this), §4.4 (admin should feel in control, never lost — an inaccurate "Account" label that opens a school-modules menu undermines this), §4.1 (every screen must feel instant — a full-screen route swap for what should be a 2-second action like sign-out is the wrong weight of transition), §4.2 (swipe gestures supplement tap targets — relevant to the dismiss gesture specified below).
**Files touched:**
- `src/App.tsx` — split the single `'more'` tab branch into two distinct UI surfaces:
  1. A new lightweight **account popover**, anchored to the header's person icon. Not a routed tab — local open/closed state, dismissible by tap-outside or tap-elsewhere. Contents: signed-in user's name + their actual role label (per §3.7, roles are custom per school — read the resolved role name, do not hardcode "Teacher"/"Admin"), the language-preference control (this is the orphaned Session 3 toggle — its home was never decided; this is that decision), and `SignOutButton` (moved here from the More list). For admin actors, also relocate **Audit log** here — "what changed and who changed it, including me" reads as account/activity, not a school module, and §4.12 already describes it as something an admin checks about their own oversight, not a thing they navigate to alongside Library or HR.
  2. The existing **More** destination keeps its `'more'` tab/route exactly as-is for everything else: Results, Library, Announcements, HR, Guardians, and (admin-only) Settings and Setup guide. Re-styled per the presentation change below.
- Header markup — fix the person icon's `onClick` to open the new popover instead of `setActiveTab('more')`; correct `aria-label` to reflect what it now actually does (e.g. `"Account"` is accurate again once it genuinely opens account content).
- CSS — new popover styles (small anchored card, not a full route); update the More destination's presentation per Problem 2.2b below since both touch the same render branch in the same session.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Enumerate every item currently inside the `'more'` tab's two sections (`Modules`, `Administration`) plus `SignOutButton` — confirm the split above is exhaustive (nothing currently in that list should disappear; every item moves to exactly one of the two surfaces, none duplicated, none dropped).
2. Build the account popover as local component state in `App.tsx` (per §5, business logic stays out of components only where it's genuinely business logic — open/closed UI state for a popover is presentation state and is fine to keep local).
3. Confirm the popover is reachable and dismissible by both tap-outside and an explicit close affordance (do not rely on tap-outside alone — §4.10 accessibility baseline expects a visible, operable control, not just an implicit gesture).
**Definition of Done:** On-device, as admin: tapping the person icon opens a small popover showing name/role/language/Settings-adjacent items/sign-out — not the modules list. Tapping "More" on the bottom nav opens the modules list — not sign-out or account content. The two are visually and functionally distinct, confirmed by a second real role (teacher) showing the same split with their own correct role label.

### Problem 2.2b — "More" reads as a full app-section swap instead of a menu *(flagged 2026-06-30, same walkthrough)*
**Root cause:** Once Problem 2.2a's split is in place, the More destination is unambiguously a navigation menu (a list of other modules to jump into) — but it currently renders as an opaque, full-screen view that replaces the entire app shell, the same visual weight as navigating into Students or Finance. A menu and a module are different *kinds* of thing and should not currently feel identical to open.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.1 (perceived speed — a sheet transition reads as faster and lighter than a full route swap), §4.2 (mobile-first — bottom sheets are the standard mobile pattern for "more options," and swipe-to-dismiss supplements the existing tap-to-dismiss), §4.7 (consistency — this is a presentation change to an existing destination, not a new one; the five module destinations underneath More are unaffected).
**Files touched:**
- `src/App.tsx` — change the `'more'` tab's render from a full-screen route replacement to a bottom sheet: slides up from the bottom edge over a dimmed/blurred backdrop, current screen still visible-but-dimmed behind it (preserves the user's place per §4.4's "never lost" principle — dismissing the sheet should not feel like leaving and re-entering the app).
- CSS — backdrop (dimmed, optionally blurred per platform capability — fall back to plain dim if `backdrop-filter` is unsupported, do not block on it), sheet container (slide-up transition, respecting `prefers-reduced-motion` per §4.10 — no slide animation when that flag is set, sheet should simply appear), drag handle affordance at the top of the sheet per standard convention.
- Dismiss handling — backdrop tap and swipe-down both close the sheet, in addition to selecting any item inside it (which should also close the sheet before navigating, not navigate underneath it).
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Confirm the sheet reuses the existing item list/markup from the current More view unchanged (Results, Library, Announcements, HR, Guardians, Settings, Setup guide) — this is a presentation/container change only, not a content change; content already moved correctly per Problem 2.2a.
2. Verify `prefers-reduced-motion` is respected (per §4.10) — sheet appears without a slide animation when the device has this set.
3. On-device check: confirm the underlying screen (whatever tab was active before More was opened) is still visibly present/dimmed behind the sheet, and that dismissing returns to it instantly with no flash or reload.
**Definition of Done:** On-device, tapping More slides up a sheet over the dimmed current screen rather than swapping the whole view; swipe-down or backdrop-tap dismisses it back to exactly where the user was; tapping a module item inside it navigates correctly. Confirmed with `prefers-reduced-motion` both on and off.

### Problem 2.2c — Dashboard quick actions duplicate bottom-nav taps instead of skipping a step *(flagged 2026-06-30, same walkthrough)*
**Root cause:** Session 6 (§11) wired `AdminDashboard.tsx`'s four quick actions to `onNavigate('students' | 'finance' | 'attendance' | 'timetable')` — closing Problem 2.2's literal Definition of Done ("actually navigates somewhere") but not its underlying intent. A bottom-nav tap and a dashboard quick-action tap on "Add student" / "Record payment" / "Mark attendance" currently land on the exact same screen (the module's default list/browse view), making one of the two entry points pointless rather than complementary. Per §4.3's own progressive-disclosure principle ("the most common action is prominent... advanced options are one tap deeper"), a quick action is supposed to *skip* the browse step for the single most common task in that module, not just be a second door into the same room. "View timetable" is the one exception — there is no form to skip to, so identical behavior to the nav tab is already correct for that one and needs no change.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.3 in full (progressive disclosure, zero-training daily tasks), §4.4 (teacher needs raw speed for daily tasks — "score entry shows the class list pre-populated," same standard applies here to admin quick actions), §4.11 (admin dashboard is the actionable surface, not a static summary — this is the literal mechanism that makes it actionable rather than a second nav).
**Files touched:**
- `src/routes/AdminDashboard.tsx` — extend each quick-action's `onNavigate` call to optionally carry an **intent** alongside the tab, e.g. `onNavigate('students', { intent: 'add' })`, `onNavigate('finance', { intent: 'recordPayment' })`, `onNavigate('attendance', { intent: 'markToday' })`. "View timetable" continues calling `onNavigate('timetable')` with no intent — confirmed no change needed there.
- `src/App.tsx` — extend the `onNavigate` signature/type threaded through `TabContent` to accept the optional intent payload and forward it to the relevant module as a prop; this is additive to the existing signature, not a breaking change to the four other callers (bottom nav, More sheet, header) which simply pass no intent and get current behavior unchanged.
- `src/routes/StudentsModule.tsx` — read an incoming `intent === 'add'` prop on mount; if present, set the existing `formOpen` state (already present in this file, per its own internal state) to `true` immediately, same as if the user had tapped the existing in-module "Add student" button. No new form needs building — this triggers the form that already exists.
- `src/routes/FinanceModule.tsx` — read an incoming `intent === 'recordPayment'` prop on mount; open the existing payment-recording form state, pre-filtered to outstanding balances if the module's existing balances view supports a filter (reuse `balances`/`balancesState` already loaded — no new query). No new form needs building.
- `src/routes/AttendanceModule.tsx` — read an incoming `intent === 'markToday'` prop on mount; ensure `selectedDate` defaults to today (already the existing default per `todayDateString()`) and, if the admin has exactly one class in scope, skip straight to that class's register; otherwise focus the existing class-picker. No new UI needs building.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Confirm each of the three target modules already has the local state this relies on (`formOpen`-equivalent) before writing any new state — per the file list above, all three already do; this should be wiring, not new component architecture.
2. Implement the optional intent payload as a non-breaking addition to the existing `onNavigate` type so the three other call sites (bottom nav, More sheet, header popover) require no changes.
3. On-device, confirm tapping each quick action lands inside the already-open form/state, not the browse view; confirm tapping the equivalent bottom-nav tab (no intent) still lands on the plain browse view exactly as before — the two entry points must now behave differently, by design.
**Definition of Done:** As admin, on-device: tapping "Add student" from the dashboard opens Students with the add-student form already on screen; tapping "Record payment" opens Finance with the payment form already on screen; tapping "Mark attendance" opens Attendance on today's date ready to mark; tapping "View timetable" behaves exactly as before. Tapping the corresponding bottom-nav tab directly (Students/Finance/Attendance) continues to land on each module's plain browse view with no form pre-opened.

## TIER 3 — Permission enforcement (`permissions` catalog → UI)

This tier is the single highest-leverage fix in this entire PRD. It is listed once and referenced by every dashboard/restriction problem above and below it, because they are all the same defect appearing in different screens.

### Problem 3.1 — Admin restrictions configured in Settings have no effect anywhere *(user's #10)*
**Root cause:** Confirmed directly in the master plan's own text (§11, session 20 entry, library module): *"write access to library tables is gated by `isAdmin`... not the finer-grained `library.manage` permission key that exists in the `permissions` catalog... the permissions catalog is not yet consumed by any frontend component."* This is not a per-module bug. It is a project-wide architectural gap: the database has a fully built, RLS-enforced permission system (§7.3: `permissions`, `role_permissions`, `staff_roles`), but **every single frontend file** (`TimetableModule`, `AttendanceModule`, `ResultsModule`, `StudentsModule`, `FinanceModule`, `HRModule`, `LibraryModule`, `AnnouncementsModule`) fails to gate its write UI on the actual permission the action requires — most via a blunt `isAdmin &&` from Problem 1.1, but confirmed 2026-07-01 that this isn't uniform: `AttendanceModule.tsx` has **no gate at all** on its write actions (only `disabled={!markedBy}` — "signed in," not "authorized"), so Session 9 adds a gate rather than converting one. Don't assume the `isAdmin &&` pattern holds for a given module until that file is actually read.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §3.7 (the full locked architecture for this — re-read in full before writing any code here), §4.12 ("Role changes take effect immediately — if an admin removes a permission from a teacher, that teacher's next action using that permission should fail gracefully with a clear message" — this is a *non-negotiable UX principle*, not an enhancement), §7.3 schema exactly as written (do not invent a different shape).
**Files touched:**
- **New file:** `src/services/permissionsService.ts` — one function, `loadEffectivePermissions(staffId): Promise<Set<string>>`, resolving `staff_roles → role_permissions → permissions.key` for the current staff member, unioned across all roles they hold (per §3.7: "effective permissions are the union").
- `src/stores/authStore.ts` — call this once at boot, store the resulting `Set<string>` alongside the existing actor-type resolution from Tier 1. Expose a single store selector, e.g. `useAuthStore(s => s.can('attendance.mark'))`.
- Every module file listed above — replace every `isAdmin &&` write-gate with the specific permission key that action actually requires, per the §7.3 catalog (the 44 seeded keys across 5 modules — pull the exact key names from the live `permissions` table via SQL Editor, do not guess them from memory).
- `src/routes/AdminDashboard.tsx`, `BottomNav.tsx` — any module-level navigation item that should disappear entirely for a staff member with zero permissions in that module (per §4.3 progressive disclosure — don't show a door to a room someone can't enter) should be conditioned on `can('<any-key-in-that-module>')` rather than `isAdmin`.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Pull the live `permissions` table contents via SQL Editor — confirm the exact 44 keys and their `module` groupings exist as documented. This becomes the canonical reference for every gate rewritten in this tier.
2. Build `permissionsService.ts` and wire it into `authStore.ts`.
3. Pick **one** module (recommend Attendance, since it's already broken and going to Tier 5 next) and convert its gates first, end to end, as a proof of pattern before repeating across the other seven modules.
4. Live-test the §4.12 exact scenario: as admin, revoke a teacher's `attendance.mark` permission in Settings; have that teacher's already-open session attempt to mark attendance; confirm it fails with a plain-English message (per §4.3 — not a raw RLS error) rather than silently succeeding or silently doing nothing.
5. Repeat the conversion for the remaining seven modules.
**Definition of Done:** For at least three different permission keys spanning at least two different modules, an admin can revoke the permission in Settings and watch — on a second device logged in as that teacher — the corresponding UI control disappear or gracefully refuse on next use, without a page reload being required to pick up the change (or, if a reload genuinely is required given the current architecture, that limitation is explicitly documented in §11 rather than silently accepted).

---

## TIER 4 — Role-specific dashboards & views

Everything in this tier was technically pre-built in earlier sessions (§13 manifest shows files existing for all four roles) but is unusable as a *differentiated experience* until Tier 1 (real identity) and Tier 3 (real permission reads) exist underneath it. This tier is mostly wiring and visual differentiation, not new architecture — the hard part already happened above.

### Problem 4.1 — Teacher dashboard does not exist *(user's #7)*
**Root cause:** There is currently exactly one staff-facing dashboard component, `AdminDashboard.tsx`, shown to anyone with `isAdmin = true` under the old (Tier-1-fixed) definition. A teacher, post-Tier-1, now resolves to `isAdminRole = false` — which currently means they fall into `App.tsx`'s `PlaceholderModule` "coming soon" branch (the same stub guardians/students saw before Tier 1/4). There has never been a third branch for "staff, but not admin."
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.4 ("Teacher: needs raw speed for daily tasks... Timetable is always one tap from anywhere in the app. Show the current lesson in the header"), §4.11 (analytics principles — a teacher's dashboard should surface *their* pending items, e.g. their own unsubmitted scores, not school-wide approval queues).
**Files touched:**
- **New file:** `src/routes/TeacherDashboard.tsx` — modeled directly on `AdminDashboard.tsx`'s structure (stat cards, action items, quick actions, announcements) per §4.7's consistency principle ("same card layout... across all modules"), but scoped to the signed-in teacher: their assigned classes (`teaching_assignments` where `staff_id = self`), their pending score submissions, today's timetable entry for them specifically (reusing `getCurrentPeriod()` from `timetableService.ts`), not school-wide totals.
- `src/App.tsx` — add the third `home`-tab branch: `isAdminRole ? <AdminDashboard /> : (actorType === 'staff' ? <TeacherDashboard /> : <GuardianPortal />)`.
- `src/services/timetableService.ts` — reuse `getCurrentPeriod()`; do not duplicate this logic (per §4.7 consistency and §5.1 "business logic in service files... portable").
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Build `TeacherDashboard.tsx` using only data the teacher's own RLS already permits them to read (per §3.2, this should require no new RLS policies — `teaching_assignments`, `scores`, `timetable_entries` are already staff-readable).
2. Wire the `home` branch in `App.tsx`.
3. Log in as the Tier-1 test teacher account. Confirm the dashboard is visually distinct from the admin one (different cards, scoped data) — this directly answers complaint #6/#7 together.
**Definition of Done:** Teacher dashboard on-device, showing only that teacher's classes/scores/timetable, visibly different from admin's school-wide view, confirmed by the same teacher account used in Problem 1.1.

### Problem 4.2 — No dedicated Student/Guardian dashboard with relevant cards *(user's #8)*
**Root cause:** Covered architecturally by Problem 1.2 — the component exists, the defect list from that problem's Goal 4/5 is the actual backlog here.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.4 (Guardian/Student rows, verbatim), §4.9 (exact content list — results, attendance, fees, announcements, calendar; "every figure must have context").
**Files touched:** `src/routes/GuardianPortal.tsx`, `src/services/portalService.ts` — same files as Problem 1.2, this time fixing the concrete defects that problem's testing pass produced rather than re-auditing from scratch.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Take the defect list produced in Problem 1.2's Goal 4 and Goal 5. Fix each one.
2. Specifically verify the "Summary tab as landing screen, never a menu" requirement (§4.4) is what a real guardian sees first — per §13's description this was the intended design; confirm it actually renders that way and not, e.g., a blank state because no `student_guardians` link existed before this PRD's Tier 1 work created one.
3. Specifically verify every figure carries context per §4.4's exact example pattern ("Absent 3 times... out of 45 school days (93% attendance)") — this is called out by name in the master plan and is easy to silently regress.
**Definition of Done:** Guardian login shows a Summary-first view with attendance/fees/results/announcements cards, all contextualized per §4.4's pattern, confirmed on-device with the real linked student created in Problem 1.2. Student login shows the equivalent single-self view with no child-switcher chrome.
