## TIER 5 — Module-level data correctness

These depend on Tier 4 because they need a *real, role-correct screen* to be tested inside — fixing the attendance save bug is pointless to verify if the screen showing it is still rendering as if every staff member were an admin.

### Problem 5.1 — Attendance doesn't save; unmarks immediately *(user's #15, first half)*
**Root cause:** Per §13's manifest, `attendanceService.ts` and `AttendanceModule.tsx` have **never been on-device tested**, across any session, despite `npm run build` passing clean. A clean build only proves the TypeScript compiles — it says nothing about whether the optimistic-UI-then-upsert flow actually round-trips correctly against live Supabase RLS. The manifest also flags a specific named risk: the `students!inner(class_node_id)` PostgREST foreign-table filter in `loadAttendanceRecords()` "should be confirmed against live Supabase instance on first test; fallback is two-step `.in('student_id', ids)` approach" — this exact join is the most likely candidate for "marks then immediately unmarks," because if the read-back query after a save silently fails or returns zero rows (due to the join not behaving as expected), the optimistic UI's confirming re-render would show the attendance as un-set even though the write may have actually succeeded.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.1 (optimistic UI principle — "the UI confirms immediately and syncs in the background... never wait for a server round-trip"; an UI that *un*-confirms after the round trip is the inverse of this principle and must be treated as a correctness bug, not a style choice), §3.2 (RLS — confirm the `attendance_records` write policy, "written by staff with `attendance.mark`," is satisfied by the Tier 3 permission the logged-in teacher actually holds), §7 attendance table definition exactly as written.
**Files touched:**
- `src/services/attendanceService.ts` — `saveAttendanceRecord()`, `saveBatchAttendance()`, and `loadAttendanceRecords()`. Test the flagged join directly in SQL Editor first (bypassing RLS and the app) to determine ground truth: does `students!inner(class_node_id)` actually return the expected rows against the live schema? If not, swap to the documented two-step fallback immediately rather than debugging the join further.
- `src/routes/AttendanceModule.tsx` — the optimistic update logic: confirm the local state update on tap is not being silently overwritten by a background refetch that uses the broken query path above.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Reproduce: mark one student present, on-device, and watch network tab / console for the actual Supabase response to the upsert call — confirm whether the **write** is succeeding (look for a 200/201 with the row back) or whether the **write succeeds but the subsequent read is wrong**. These are different bugs with different fixes; do not guess.
2. Fix whichever side is confirmed broken (per the join risk flagged above, the read side is the prime suspect, but confirm before fixing).
3. Confirm fix holds for `saveBatchAttendance()` (mark-all) as well as the single-row path — these are two different functions per the manifest and a fix to one does not guarantee the other.
**Definition of Done:** As the Tier-1 teacher account, mark a full class register (mix of Present/Absent/Late/Excused, plus one batch mark-all), reload the page, and confirm every mark is still exactly as set — not reset, not blank.

### Problem 5.2 — Attendance can be taken on non-school days *(user's #15, second half)*
**Root cause:** This is not a bug — it is a genuinely missing feature with no underlying data to check against. Grepping the entire 61-table schema (§7) for any concept of a school calendar, term dates, holidays, or non-school days returns nothing. `school_week_days` (§10 Phase 2) only encodes which *weekdays* the school generally runs (e.g. Mon–Fri vs. Sat–Sun for some madrasas) — it has no concept of a specific calendar date being a holiday, exam day, or mid-term break within an otherwise-normal weekday.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §7's table conventions (every new table needs `id uuid`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `school_id`, and its RLS policy in the *same* migration per §3.2 — this is a net-new table and must follow every convention in §7's preamble exactly), §3.6 (optional terms — a calendar concept must work whether or not `uses_terms` is true, since `sessions`/`terms` already handle that duality and a calendar table should follow the same pattern: scope to `session_id`, not `term_id`, so it works regardless), §10 (this requires a new roadmap line — do not silently fold it into an existing Phase; add it explicitly as a Phase 2 addendum since it's an academic-calendar concern).
**Files touched:**
- **New migration:** a `school_calendar_days` table (proposed shape: `id uuid pk`, `school_id`, `session_id` references `sessions(id)`, `calendar_date date not null`, `day_type text check (day_type in ('school_day','holiday','exam_day','break'))`, `label text` (e.g. "Eid al-Fitr", "Mid-term break"), `created_at`, `updated_at`, unique `(school_id, calendar_date)`). **Flagging explicitly, per master-plan instruction, that this is an invented table not present in the original §7 schema — it must be reviewed and either accepted into §7 or redesigned before migration, not silently added.**
- RLS: `[STD]` for school-scoped read (everyone in the school can see the calendar — teachers need to know it when marking attendance, guardians/students benefit from seeing it too per §4.9's "school calendar of activities"), write restricted to a permission key — propose `academics.manage_calendar`, to be added to the §7.3 `permissions` seed catalog (another explicit addition to flag, not silently insert).
- `src/services/attendanceService.ts` — `loadAttendanceSettings()`/new `isSchoolDay(date)` check, called before rendering the mark-attendance UI for a given date; if the date is not a school day, the UI should show the same "actionable empty state" pattern §4.3 requires elsewhere ("No school today — [reason from label]") rather than just disabling silently.
- Admin Settings (`settingsService.ts`, `AdminSettingsPanel`) — needs a new tab/section for the admin to actually populate `school_calendar_days` (bulk-mark a date range as holiday, or single-date entry) — without this, the table exists but nothing ever populates it, repeating the exact "configured a feature with no UI to use it" pattern seen elsewhere in this project (e.g. `theme_color` working but languages not).
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Get the proposed table shape reviewed/approved into master plan §7 before writing the migration (per §0's own instruction 7: "Always check there before inventing table names or columns").
2. Migration + RLS in the same step, per §3.2.
3. Build the admin settings UI to populate it — at minimum, a way to mark individual dates and a way to bulk-mark recurring public holidays for the session.
4. Wire the attendance module's date check.
**Definition of Done:** Admin marks a date as a holiday in Settings; teacher opens Attendance for that date and sees the actionable empty state instead of a markable register; teacher opens Attendance for the next school day and registers normally.

### Problem 5.5 — No student-specific roles (prefect, class rep, etc.) *(flagged 2026-06-29, StudentsModule walkthrough)*
**Root cause:** This is genuinely new scope, not a contradicted requirement or a bug. The schema's existing custom-roles architecture (§3.7, §7.3 — `roles`, `staff_roles`, `role_permissions`) was built specifically for **staff**. There is no equivalent structure for students. Per §3.7's own discipline ("roles are data, fully custom per school — there is no hardcoded 'teacher' role to special-case"), the correct approach is almost certainly the same pattern reused for students — a school-defined, freely-named list of student roles (prefect, head girl/boy, class rep, sports captain, etc.) rather than a hardcoded enum — but this must be confirmed as a deliberate design decision, not assumed, since student roles carry different implications than staff roles (no permissions attached, purely descriptive/organizational, likely class-scoped rather than school-scoped for things like "class rep").
**Master plan sections to observe:** §3.7 (re-read in full — the staff roles pattern this should likely mirror, including the "no hardcoded role names" discipline), §7.3 (existing `roles` table shape — do not reuse this table directly for students without confirming whether `roles.is_admin_role` and `role_permissions` make sense in a student context; they likely do not, since student roles aren't permission-bearing), §7.8 (student schema, where any new field/relation must be confirmed before being invented, per §0 instruction 7).
**Files touched (proposed, not yet confirmed — surface in §12 before SESSION 8 or whichever session is assigned this problem):**
- New migration: a `student_roles` table (`id`, `school_id`, `name`, `class_id` **nullable** — `null` means school-wide, a real class id means that role instance is scoped to that one class) plus a join table `student_role_assignments` (`student_id`, `student_role_id`) — mirroring `roles`/`staff_roles`'s separation of "the role exists" from "who holds it," but explicitly without `role_permissions`, since student roles do not gate any permission in this PRD's scope. **Decided 2026-06-30: scope is a per-role admin choice, not a fixed architectural split** — see resolved decision below.
- `src/services/studentsService.ts` — add CRUD for student roles and assignments.
- `src/routes/StudentsModule.tsx` — Info/Edit subtab: add a roles section (multi-select against the school's defined student roles, admin can add/remove the student's roles inline). A separate small admin screen (likely under Settings, alongside staff role management) to define the list of available student roles per school — do not let this be defined ad-hoc inside each student's edit screen, or the same role name will get retyped inconsistently across students. **This admin-side screen must ask, at role-creation time, whether the new role is school-wide or tied to a specific class** (e.g. a toggle or class-picker that's empty/skippable for school-wide roles).
- This problem also folds in the related space/layout request from the same walkthrough: the Info/Edit subtab needs more room for inputs generally (not just roles) — treat as part of the same redesign pass rather than a separate patch, since both touch the same screen.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Confirm the proposed table shape in §7 before migrating (per §0 instruction 7), including the nullable `class_id` design below.
2. ~~Decide and record in §12: are student roles school-scoped, class-scoped, or both?~~ **Resolved 2026-06-30: admin decides per role at creation time.** A role like "Head Boy" would be created school-wide (`class_id = null`); a role like "Class Rep" would be created once per class that wants one (`class_id` set, so JSS1A's Class Rep and JSS1B's Class Rep are two separate role rows sharing the same `name`, each scoped to its own class). The admin-side "define student roles" screen must surface this choice explicitly — it is not inferred from the role name.
3. Build the admin-side "define student roles" screen before the per-student assignment UI — assigning from an empty list is not useful.
**Definition of Done:** Admin creates one school-wide role and one class-scoped role (e.g. "Head Boy" school-wide, "Class Rep" scoped to a specific class) in a settings-adjacent screen, assigns each correctly to real students from those students' edit screens, and confirms a student in a *different* class cannot be assigned the class-scoped role created for another class. Role is visible somewhere sensible (Brief tab, list row badge, or similar — to be decided in §12, not invented in-session).

**✅ Resolved 2026-07-03** — see SESSION 13 spec block and tracker row 13 for the full delivery arc (core build + three owner-reported follow-up rounds). Definition of Done met on-device, `npm run build` clean.

### Problem 5.3 — No space to add a student photograph *(user's #12)*
**Root cause:** §3.13 already architecturally allows for this ("Supabase Storage: small, auth-scoped files — student photos, documents"), and §4.8 explicitly requires it be done correctly ("Images: never serve a full-resolution photo where a thumbnail suffices. Student photos compressed and resized at upload time"). But per §13's `StudentsModule.tsx` manifest entry, the Brief tab explicitly shows *"a notice that medical/blood-group/critical-notes fields don't exist yet"* and makes no mention of a photo field at all — this was simply never built, despite the storage architecture already supporting it.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §3.13 (storage split — this is Supabase Storage, not Cloudflare R2, since it's a small auth-scoped file), §4.8 (compress/resize at upload time — do not skip this and upload raw camera photos, which on Nigerian 3G per §4.1 would be a serious regression), §7.8 (student schema — `photo_url` or equivalent column does not currently exist; flag as an addition, same discipline as Problem 5.2).
**Files touched:**
- Migration: add `photo_url text` (nullable) to `students` (or, if multiple photo sizes are wanted, a small `photo_thumbnail_url`/`photo_full_url` pair — recommend just one `photo_url` pointing at a pre-resized thumbnail, per §4.8's own instruction to never even store the full-resolution version if a thumbnail suffices for every place it's displayed).
- `src/services/studentsService.ts` — add `photo_url` to every select/insert/update, same pattern already established for `student_code` in session 31's bug fix (§11) — follow that fix's shape exactly, since it's the most recently-proven pattern for adding a field to this exact table.
- `src/routes/StudentsModule.tsx` — Info tab: add a photo upload control (tap avatar circle → camera/gallery picker → client-side resize before upload, per §4.8 — a library like `browser-image-compression` or a canvas-based resize is acceptable; do not upload unresized files). Brief tab and list-row avatar circles: replace the initials-only avatar with the photo when present, fallback to initials when not (per §4.3 "empty states are actionable" spirit — no broken image icons).
- Supabase Storage — confirm a bucket exists for student photos with RLS/policies scoped per §3.2 (school-scoped read, write restricted to staff holding `students.records.edit`, per §12's 2026-07-01 decision).
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Confirm/create the Storage bucket and its access policy.
2. Add the column via migration.
3. Build upload + resize + display across all three places a student avatar currently renders (list row, Brief tab, detail header).
**Definition of Done:** Admin uploads a photo for a real student from a real phone camera; the photo appears correctly sized in the student list row, the Brief tab, and survives a page reload.

### Problem 5.4 — No analytics at the top of the Students and Attendance pages *(user's #14)*
**Root cause:** §4.11 mandates analytics as "the default view of the admin dashboard" — and that mandate has been fulfilled there (`AdminDashboard.tsx`'s four stat cards). But §4.11 does not actually require per-module analytics headers on Students/Attendance specifically — this is a genuine **scope gap relative to the owner's expectation**, not a contradicted requirement. Treat this as new scope, sequenced here because it depends on Tier 5.1's attendance data actually being correct and Tier 5.2's school-day awareness existing (otherwise an attendance analytics card would compute percentages against non-school days, producing wrong numbers — a worse outcome than no card at all).
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.11 (data visualization rules — "bar charts for trends, progress rings for completion rates, simple tables for lists... never complex interactive charts that don't work on a phone"), §4.1 (these queries must be cheap/cached — do not add an analytics header that re-queries the entire student/attendance table on every render).
**Files touched:**
- `src/routes/StudentsModule.tsx` — add a small stat strip above the list: total active students, students by status breakdown (active/inactive/alumni/transferred), perhaps new-this-session count. Reuse `loadStudents()` data already being fetched — do not issue a second redundant query if the existing fetch already has what's needed; aggregate client-side.
- `src/routes/AttendanceModule.tsx` — add a stat strip above the register: today's present/absent/late/excused/unmarked totals for the selected class (this data is already computed by the existing `getAttendanceSummary()` helper per §13's manifest — this is wiring an existing pure helper into a new visual location, not new logic), and optionally a small weekly trend sparkline if time allows, per §4.11's bar-chart guidance.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Confirm `getAttendanceSummary()` (already listed as an existing pure helper in `attendanceService.ts` per §13) covers what's needed before writing any new aggregation logic.
2. Build the two stat strips using existing design tokens (§3 native-app-strategy: design tokens for everything) and existing card patterns from `AdminDashboard.tsx` (§4.7 consistency).
**Definition of Done:** Both Students and Attendance pages show a glanceable stat row at the top, on-device, using real data, that updates correctly as records change underneath it.

---

## TIER 6 — Design-system port & visual pass

Sequenced after Tier 5 among "must-fix" tiers, for the same reason the original visual pass was sequenced last: re-skinning or adding a new widget to a screen whose underlying logic, routing, and role-gating are still wrong means re-doing that work a second time once Tiers 1–5 change what the screen actually contains and who sees it. This tier assumes Tiers 1–5 are closed, **with two named exceptions**: Problem 6.7 (session/term progress display) depends on Tier 5.2's `school_calendar_days` table specifically, and Problem 6.4 (live period widget) depends on the timetable module's real data shape — both are called out again in their own sections below so the dependency isn't just implied by tier order.

This tier absorbs the original Tier 6 scope (token audit, icon replacement) as Problem 6.1, and adds ten more problems: six ported from the reference app's named features (Problems 6.2–6.7, per §0.4's original seven-item inventory), and four covering the reference app's *structural/flow* language — app shell and module-by-module UI (Problems 6.8–6.11, per §0.4's second-pass inventory, which corrected the original seven-item list from "the full scope" to "seven illustrative pieces of a much larger, whole-app design language the owner wants ported throughout"). Work the problems in the order listed: 6.1 first (every later problem renders using its tokens/icons), then 6.2–6.7 (the named ports), then 6.8 (app shell — becomes the frame 6.9–6.11 sit inside), then 6.9–6.11 (module-by-module, in decreasing order of direct reference-app equivalence).

### Problem 6.1 — UI isn't modern/intuitive; multicolored icons; school theme colors show no visible difference *(user's #2 and #11; design-port items #1, #3, #4)*
**Root cause:** Two separate but related defects, now folded together with the reference-app port since both are solved by the same pass:
1. **Visual language inconsistency** — the master plan's own §4.7 ("Consistency — one product, everywhere") is listed as non-negotiable but, per the session log, each module's CSS block was generated independently, in isolation, across 8+ separate sessions (`dash-*`, `tt-*`, `att-*`, `res-*`, `stu-*`, `fin-*`, `hr-*`, `lib-*`, `ann-*`, `port-*` — ten separate prefix families). Each was checked only for *not colliding* with the others (a class-name uniqueness check), never for *looking consistent* with the others (a design check).
2. **Theme color not visibly propagating** — Per §11 session 24, `updateSchoolSettings` does apply the new theme color live via `document.documentElement.style.setProperty('--color-brand-600', ...)`. If different schools' colors "show no difference," the most likely cause is that most of the ten CSS prefix families above were authored against *hardcoded* color values rather than consistently referencing the `--color-brand-600` design token.
3. **New scope, not a defect — the token *values* themselves:** the owner has separately asked (design-port item #1) for the actual palette to visually match the reference app's emerald/parchment/gold identity, not just for the existing tokens to be consistently *applied*. Per design-port item #4, this cannot mean hardcoding emerald as the app's fixed color the way the reference app does — the reference app is single-school and bakes its palette into `:root` directly. This app is multi-tenant with a customizable `--color-brand-600`. **Resolution: port the reference app's token *architecture* (the full semantic scale — `-950` through `-50` shades, parchment neutrals, gold as a fixed secondary/accent color, semantic red/amber/blue/purple, the shadow and radius scale, the dark-theme override block), but derive the brand-600-and-up/down shades from whatever hex an admin sets, not from a hardcoded emerald value.** Gold stays fixed (it's an accent, not the brand color, and reads as "premium/highlight" regardless of a school's chosen brand color — confirm this reading with the owner before finalizing if any doubt remains, per §12 discipline). Record the finalized token names and shade-derivation approach in master-plan §12 as a locked decision before Session 23 below writes any code.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.7 (full section), §3 native-app-strategy point on design tokens, §4.10 (accessibility — sufficient contrast at every brand-color a school might pick, not just the reference app's emerald; the token system must guarantee readable text/border contrast algorithmically — e.g. deriving a readable foreground from the chosen brand color — rather than assuming any admin-picked hex works), Phase 0's "design tokens implemented" line in §10.
**Files touched:** `src/index.css` in full (every one of the ten prefix blocks named above, plus the `:root`/`[data-theme="dark"]` token block itself, which needs the reference app's fuller scale ported in) — this is a full audit-and-extend pass, not a single-file fix. Icon usage: wherever emoji or ad-hoc icon characters are used for nav/quick-action icons — replace with the icon set from Problem 6.3 (built in the same tier, sequenced immediately after this problem so the token pass and icon pass don't fight over the same files twice).
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Port the reference app's token *shape* into `:root` (shade scale, parchment neutrals, gold accent, semantic colors, shadow/radius/motion scale) — but replace every hardcoded emerald value with a reference to the existing `--color-brand-600` mechanism, deriving the rest of the brand shade scale from it (a small color-math helper, e.g. HSL lightness steps off the admin-set base color, is acceptable and should live in a shared util, not be recomputed per-component).
2. Audit every hardcoded hex/rgb color value across all ten CSS prefix blocks; replace each with the nearest matching design token. Do not introduce new tokens casually — reuse existing ones.
3. Re-verify the theme color live-update against the now-token-consistent CSS — confirm changing a school's theme color in Settings visibly changes buttons, active nav states, and accent elements across *every* module.
**Definition of Done:** Setting two different schools to two different theme colors produces two visibly, consistently different app experiences across every module, confirmed on-device — using the reference app's fuller token architecture (parchment backgrounds, gold accents, proper shadow scale, dark-mode support), not just the old flat token set; a side-by-side screenshot review of all module screens shows one coherent visual language.

### Problem 6.2 — Login page and timetable screen don't match the target design language *(design-port item #1)*
**Root cause:** Genuinely new scope, not a contradicted requirement — the current `LoginPage.tsx` and `TimetableModule.tsx` were built before this design reference existed. The owner has specifically named these two screens ("the timetable design, the login page design") as the clearest examples of what to match.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.2 (mobile-first, thumb-reachable), §4.7 (consistency — once ported, these two screens become the reference every other screen in Problem 6.1's audit is checked against, so get them right first), §4.10 (accessibility — the reference app's focus-return-on-modal-close and keyboard-operable form fields are part of the pattern, not just its visuals).
**Files touched:**
- `src/routes/LoginPage.tsx` (or equivalent — confirm exact filename via §13) — restructure to match the reference app's `.att-login-card` pattern: centered card, icon + title + description header, labeled inputs with focus-ring styling, primary button spanning full width, inline error state below the form (not a toast), a subtle secondary-action link below the primary form (the reference app uses this for "staff login" as a secondary path below the primary student-login form — this app's equivalent secondary path, if any, should follow the same visual treatment, e.g. any alternate login method).
- `src/routes/TimetableModule.tsx` — restructure the grid/table rendering to match the reference app's `.tbl-wrap`/`.cell` pattern: rounded-corner table container with shadow, colored subject cells with subject-name + teacher-name stacked inside each cell, hover/focus lift effect on cells, and the "now" column highlight treatment (`th.now-col`/`td.now-col`) which visually marks whichever period column is currently running — this last piece depends on Problem 6.2's sibling, the live-period widget (6.2 covers the table; 6.3... see Problem 6.2b below for the widget itself, sequenced immediately after).
- `src/index.css` — new `.att-login-*`-equivalent and `.tbl-wrap`/`.cell`-equivalent blocks, using Problem 6.1's token system exclusively (no new hardcoded colors).
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Rebuild the login screen's markup/CSS against the reference pattern, using the resolved brand-token scale from Problem 6.1.
2. Rebuild the timetable grid's markup/CSS against the reference pattern.
3. Confirm both screens work in dark mode if Problem 6.1's dark-theme tokens are in scope for this app (confirm this is in-scope with the owner before building — the reference app has a full dark theme; check whether this target app already has one per §13, since master-plan §7 does not currently document a `theme_mode` concept the way it documents `theme_color`. If dark mode is not existing app scope, port only the light-theme values and flag dark-theme as a possible future addition rather than silently building it).
**Definition of Done:** Login page and Timetable module visually match the reference app's design language (card layout, colors via brand token, typography weight/scale, cell styling), confirmed on-device by the owner via direct side-by-side comparison with the reference app.

### Problem 6.3 — Icon set: replace every emoji-as-icon instance app-wide with a consistent monochrome set *(user's #2/#11 rewrite, and explicit clarification: scope is app-wide, not limited to the reference app's named set; design-port item #3)*
**Root cause:** Same underlying defect as originally documented in Tier 6's Problem 6.1 (emoji used directly in JSX as icons throughout the app) — but the owner has now supplied a concrete icon system to model the replacement on, instead of an abstract "pick a library" instruction. The reference app's `ICONS` object is a ~45-entry object of hand-authored inline SVG strings (menu, mosque/school-mark, printer, bell, calendar, settings, edit, trash, and so on), each sized/colored via `currentColor` and CSS custom properties rather than a fixed fill — meaning it already satisfies the "inherits brand color automatically" requirement without any extra work. **Scope correction — read carefully before starting Session 25:** this is not "replace whichever emoji happen to have a same-named counterpart in the reference app's 45 icons." The reference set is the *pattern and quality bar* to build to, not a ceiling on what gets fixed. In the reference app, icons — not emoji — are used throughout, for every nav item, action, status marker, and empty state. The defect in this app is that emoji are standing in for icons everywhere a proper icon should be, not just in the handful of spots previously spot-checked. Every one of those needs a real icon; where the reference app's 45 don't cover a concept this app needs (e.g. a concept the reference app's single-school design never needed), a new icon must be authored in the same visual style — 24×24 viewBox, `stroke="currentColor"`, `stroke-width="2"` — not skipped for lack of a ready-made match.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.7 (consistency), §5 (native app strategy — inline SVG has no external-library dependency, which is actually *more* portable to React Native than most icon libraries, since it avoids a web-only npm package; confirm this reasoning holds before locking the decision), §4.10 (accessibility — icons used as the only signal for an action need an `aria-label` on the interactive element, same as the reference app's `aria-label="القائمة"` pattern on its menu button).
**Files touched:**
- **New file:** `src/components/icons/index.ts` (or `Icon.tsx` as a single parameterized component, e.g. `<Icon name="calendar" />`) — port the reference app's icon set as React components or a lookup map, preserving the exact `viewBox="0 0 24 24"`/`stroke="currentColor"`/`stroke-width="2"` conventions so every icon inherits size and color from its CSS context automatically, per the app's existing design-token discipline. This module must be extensible — new icons authored during the app-wide sweep below get added here in the same style, not parked in a separate ad-hoc file.
- **Every** module file in the app (a full, fresh grep — not a re-check of the master-plan manifest's previously-flagged 🖨, 👨‍👩‍👧, ⟟, which were illustrative examples from an earlier partial pass, not the complete list) — replace every emoji used as an icon, whether or not the reference app happens to have a same-concept icon already.
- `BottomNav.tsx` — same replacement for nav icons.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Port the full reference `ICONS` set as a single reusable component/module — the practical starting inventory, not the boundary of what gets built.
2. Grep the entire codebase, fresh, for every emoji used as an icon (nav, buttons, cards, status badges, empty states — everywhere, not just the three previously flagged as examples). List every file and location before changing anything.
3. For each emoji found: if the reference set has an equivalent concept, use it; if it doesn't, author a new icon in the same visual style rather than leaving the emoji in place or picking a mismatched substitute.
4. Replace every emoji icon with its (ported or newly authored) equivalent, confirming each renders at the correct size and inherits the brand-token color where appropriate (not every icon should be brand-colored — status icons like a red "conflict" warning should keep their semantic color per the reference app's own pattern, e.g. `.header-conflict-badge`'s amber, not brand color).
**Definition of Done:** Zero emoji used as navigation, action, status, or empty-state icons anywhere in the app, confirmed by a fresh full-codebase grep (not a spot-check). Every replacement icon is monochrome, SVG-based, sized via CSS, and inherits brand-token color case-by-case matching the reference app's own choices. Any icon with no reference-app equivalent has been newly authored in the same visual style rather than omitted. Confirmed on-device.

### Problem 6.4 — No live "what's happening right now, across every class" summary above the timetable *(design-port item #2)*
**Root cause:** Genuinely new scope. This app's `TimetableModule.tsx` currently shows a static weekly grid with no live/real-time layer. The reference app's `.live-banner` widget is a self-contained, second-by-second-ticking summary that sits above the timetable and shows: whether school is in session right now, which period is running, a live countdown to the period's end, and — when expanded — a card-per-class grid of exactly what every class is doing in the current period simultaneously, plus a short list of upcoming periods. This is the single feature the owner named as "most important... the function above the timetable."
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.1 (perceived speed — this widget must feel instant and never block on a network round-trip once initial data is loaded; the reference app's `tick()` runs entirely off already-fetched data plus `Date.now()`, no per-second network calls), §4.4 (Teacher row: "Timetable is always one tap from anywhere... Show the current lesson in the header" — this widget is the natural home for that requirement, not a separate build), §7 (timetable schema — confirm the exact shape of `timetable_entries`/period-definition tables before writing the period-boundary logic; the reference app hardcodes period start/end times and a Sat/Sun week — this app's schema is meant to support arbitrary school-week days per `school_week_days`, so the widget's period-boundary logic must read from real data, not hardcoded times).
**Files touched:**
- **New file:** `src/components/LivePeriodWidget.tsx` (or equivalent) — houses the ticking state, the expand/collapse interaction, and the countdown/progress-bar rendering, modeled on the reference app's `getState()`/`updateWidget()`/`tick()` structure but rewritten against this app's actual timetable data shape and Zustand state management (per §5, business logic like period-boundary calculation belongs in a service/util, not inline in the component — port `getState()`'s logic into `src/services/timetableService.ts` as a pure function, e.g. `getCurrentPeriodState(timetableData, now)`, and have the component call it).
- `src/services/timetableService.ts` — add the pure period-state-resolution function above; reuse `getCurrentPeriod()` if it already exists per §13 (Tier 4's `TeacherDashboard.tsx` was already specified to reuse this — confirm this widget and that dashboard feature share the same underlying function rather than each re-deriving "what period is it right now" independently, which would risk the two disagreeing).
- `src/routes/TimetableModule.tsx` — render `<LivePeriodWidget />` above the grid, matching the reference app's placement.
- `src/index.css` — port `.live-banner`/`.live-dot2`/`.live-countdown`/`.live-prog`/`.live-expanded`/`.live-grid`/`.live-card`/`.upcoming-list` styles, using Problem 6.1's token system.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Confirm (or build, if genuinely absent) a pure function that answers "what period is happening right now, across every class, given the current timetable data and the current time" — this is the actual hard logic; the widget is presentation on top of it.
2. Build the collapsed-state banner (dot indicator, status text, live countdown, expand toggle) and the expanded-state panel (per-class-per-period-live grid + upcoming-periods list).
3. Confirm the widget correctly handles all the states the reference app handles: before school starts, a period actively running, break time, after school ends, and a non-school day/weekend — each with its own status text and visual treatment (not just "ongoing," which is the only state a naive first pass tends to build).
4. Confirm the widget respects `prefers-reduced-motion` for its pulse/expand animations, per §4.10.
**Definition of Done:** On-device, during real school hours with real timetable data, the widget shows the correct currently-running period, a live-updating countdown, and — when expanded — a card for every class showing what that class is doing right now, all without a page reload, ticking correctly across multiple real minutes of observation (not just a single glance). **Confirmed satisfied — see PRD §11 row 26.**

### Problem 6.5 — No popup/modal pattern for period details, pickers, or confirmations *(design-port item #5)*
**Root cause:** New scope / consistency gap — confirm via §13 whether this app already has *a* modal pattern before treating this as fully new; if one exists but is inconsistent module-to-module (the same "each module built its own CSS in isolation" pattern from Problem 6.1), this problem is a consolidation, not a from-scratch build.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.10 (accessibility — the reference app's exact pattern of storing `lastFocusBeforeModal` and returning focus to it on close, plus focusing the close button on open, is a real accessibility requirement, not decoration — port the behavior, not just the visuals), §4.1 (the reference app's popIn animation is fast — 0.18s — matching the "feels instant" principle).
**Files touched:**
- **New file (if not already present):** `src/components/Modal.tsx` — a single reusable overlay+card component (backdrop with blur, centered card, close button, `popIn` animation, focus-trap-lite behavior: store/restore focus, close on backdrop click or Escape key) matching the reference app's `.modal-ov`/`.modal`/`.modal-x` pattern.
- Every place in the app that currently uses `window.confirm()`/`alert()` or an ad-hoc modal implementation — audit via grep and consolidate onto the new shared component. (This directly benefits Session 11's guardian-delete/regenerate confirmation work and Problem 2.2a/2.2b's popover/sheet patterns from Tier 2 — confirm those don't need retrofitting or whether they intentionally use a different, lighter-weight pattern per their own Definition of Done; if they do, document why the popover/sheet pattern and this modal pattern coexist rather than treating the difference as an inconsistency to fix.)
- `src/index.css` — port `.modal-ov`/`.modal`/`.modal-x`/`.modal-badge`/`.modal-row`/`.picker-btn` styles onto Problem 6.1's token system.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Confirm whether a modal pattern already exists in this app (§13) before building a new one from scratch.
2. Build/consolidate onto the single shared `Modal.tsx`, including the focus-management behavior.
3. Migrate at least one real use case (e.g. a period-detail popup on the timetable grid, matching the reference app's `openModal()` use case exactly) to prove the pattern before mandating it app-wide.
**Definition of Done:** Tapping a timetable cell opens a modal showing that period's subject/teacher/class/time details, matching the reference app's visual and interaction pattern (backdrop, centered card, close button, focus returns to the trigger element on close), confirmed on-device.

### Problem 6.6 — Settings panel lacks the depth the owner wants, including a real alarm system *(design-port item #6)*
**Root cause:** New scope. Per Tier 7's Problem 7.2, this app's Settings currently has some inert controls (language) and an unaudited set of others. The owner is asking for a specific, much richer settings surface modeled on the reference app: dark mode, clock format, default landing day/view, highlight-current-period-column toggle, and — the named centerpiece — a genuine alarm/notification system with configurable tone, volume, repeat count, an early-warning lead time before a period ends, alert scope (all periods vs. a specific teacher's periods only), and browser notifications, all backed by real Web Audio API tone synthesis (no audio files to host or load).
**Master plan sections to observe:** §5 (native app strategy — Web Audio API is available in React Native via community modules but is not identical; flag this explicitly as a note for the future native port rather than silently assuming portability, per §5.1's own discipline), §4.10 (the reference app's settings rows include disabled-state styling when a dependent toggle is off, e.g. sound-type/volume/repeat controls grey out when the master sound toggle is off — port this dependency-disabling behavior, not just the individual controls), §7 (this app's persisted-settings shape does not yet exist per current schema/store — decide whether these are per-user preferences (stored client-side only, e.g. `localStorage`/Zustand-persisted) or server-synced per-staff preferences; the reference app uses `localStorage` only, meaning alarm settings don't follow a teacher between devices — flag this choice explicitly to the owner rather than silently deciding).
**Files touched:**
- **New file:** `src/services/alarmService.ts` — port the reference app's tone-synthesis functions (`playTone`, the four dispatch families — chime/bell/buzzer/digital — each with an "end" and "warn" variant) as pure functions operating on the Web Audio API, gated behind a lazy-created `AudioContext` (the reference app's `getAudioCtx()` pattern — audio contexts must be created/resumed on a user gesture in most browsers, so this needs to be wired to fire on first genuine user interaction, not on mount).
- `src/stores/uiStore.ts` or a new `src/stores/settingsStore.ts` — the full settings shape: `theme`, `clockFormat`, `defaultDay`, `defaultView`, `showSeconds`, `highlightCol`, `soundEnabled`, `soundType`, `soundVol`, `soundRepeat`, `earlyWarning`, `warnLeadMin`, `browserNotif`, `alertScope` — matching the reference app's `DEFAULT_SETTINGS` shape as a starting point, adjusted for whatever subset is confirmed relevant to this app's actual role structure (e.g. `alertScope` scoped to "this teacher's periods" needs a real teacher-identity concept, which now exists post-Tier-1/Tier-4).
- **New file:** `src/routes/SettingsPanel.tsx` (or extend the existing one per §13) — the settings-row UI pattern (title + description + control on the right — switch, select, or button), sectioned (general / clock & display / alerts / account), matching the reference app's `.settings-row`/`.switch`/`.settings-select` visual pattern.
- `src/routes/TimetableModule.tsx` / `LivePeriodWidget.tsx` (Problem 6.4) — wire `processAlerts()`-equivalent logic: fire a tone when a period is about to end (if `earlyWarning` is on) and when a period actually ends, respecting `alertScope`.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Decide and record in §12: are alarm/display settings per-device (`localStorage`, matching the reference app) or per-account (synced via a new `staff_preferences`-style table)? Do not silently pick one — this changes the files-touched list materially.
2. Build `alarmService.ts` and confirm all four tone types are audibly distinct and correctly gated by volume/repeat settings (test each on a real device, not just headphone playback in a dev environment — the reference app's use case is a phone speaker in a classroom).
3. Build the settings UI, wiring every control to the settings store, including the dependency-disabling behavior (sound sub-controls grey out when master sound is off).
4. Wire the alarm into the live-period widget from Problem 6.4 so it actually fires at real period boundaries.
**Definition of Done:** As a real teacher account, on-device: opening Settings shows the full control set; toggling dark mode/clock format/default view visibly changes app behavior; the "test sound" button plays the selected tone at the selected volume; sitting through a real (or manually-shortened-for-testing) period boundary produces an audible alert matching the configured tone/repeat/lead-time settings, with sound-dependent controls correctly disabled when sound is off. **Partially built, 2026-07-09 (Session 28) — NOT yet satisfied. Storage decision made (per-account), `alarmService.ts`/settings UI/dependency-disabling/browser-notification piece all built and verified. The core "sitting through a real period boundary produces an audible alert" criterion is confirmed FAILING on-device: no sound, no error. One real bug found and fixed (stale `staffId` closure) but the alarm still did not fire after that fix — root cause still unknown. See master-plan §11 progress log, 2026-07-09 (Session 28) entry, for full diagnostic state and required next steps before re-attempting. Do not mark this problem done until a live console read during an actual failing test has been obtained.**

### Problem 6.7 — No visible session/term display, and no holiday-aware auto-adjustment of term dates *(design-port item #7)*
**Root cause:** New scope, and explicitly **depends on Tier 5.2's `school_calendar_days` table existing first** — this problem cannot start until that table is built, since there is currently no concept of a calendar holiday in this app's schema at all (confirmed by the original Tier 5.2 grep of the full 61-table schema). The reference app's equivalent (`trimesters`/`holidays` tables, `.trim-card` progress-bar UI, `attAutoExtendTrimester()`) is a working, complete pattern for exactly this — a visible term-progress card showing current term, date range, a progress bar with the current date marked, elapsed/remaining school-week counts, and a proportionally-positioned notch on the bar for every holiday inserted into that term, plus **automatic extension of the term's end date** by one school day for every school-day holiday added.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §3.6 (optional terms — `uses_terms` flag; this feature must degrade sensibly when a school has terms disabled, e.g. show nothing or show a simpler session-wide progress bar against `sessions` dates instead of `terms` dates — confirm which with the owner rather than assuming), §7 (this app's actual `sessions`/`terms` schema — confirm exact table/column names before writing any query; do not assume the reference app's `trimesters` table name or shape carries over), Tier 5.2 in full (the `school_calendar_days` table this problem is built on top of), §4.11 (progress-bar/visualization guidance — "simple... never complex interactive charts that don't work on a phone," which the reference app's bar-plus-notches pattern already satisfies).
**Files touched:**
- `src/services/` — a new function (or addition to an existing terms/sessions service) computing the same shape the reference app's `buildTrimCardHtml()` computes: elapsed/total percentage, elapsed/total/remaining school-week counts (school days per week from `school_week_days`, not a hardcoded 2), and a list of school-day holidays within the term's date range with their calendar-position percentage for notch placement — ported as pure data-computation, with rendering split into a separate component per this app's React architecture (the reference app mixes computation and HTML-string-building in one function; this app should not repeat that pattern).
- **New component:** `src/components/TermProgressCard.tsx` — renders the computed shape as a card: term label, date range, progress bar with today-marker pin and holiday notches, elapsed/remaining/total counters, and a break-impact summary line when holidays exist within the term (matching the reference app's `.trim-break-row`/`<details>` expandable list of named breaks).
- Wherever the reference app shows this card (staff dashboard, student dashboard) — confirm the equivalent placement in this app (likely `AdminDashboard.tsx`, `TeacherDashboard.tsx` from Tier 4, and the guardian/student portal) and render `<TermProgressCard />` in each.
- `src/services/` (wherever `school_calendar_days` writes happen, per Tier 5.2's `settingsService.ts` addition) — the auto-extend/auto-shrink logic (see resolved decision below).
**Goals before moving on — and one explicit decision to make before writing code:**
1. **Resolve the extend/shrink symmetry** (flagged in §0.4): the reference app only extends a term's end date when a school-day holiday is inserted; it never reverses that extension when the holiday is later deleted. The owner's ask explicitly includes "auto changes with holiday inserted/**removed**," which is a stricter, more correct spec than the reference app actually implements. **Recommended resolution, to be confirmed with the owner before building:** store the *original* admin-set term end date alongside the *effective* (auto-extended) end date as two separate columns (e.g. `terms.end_date_base` and a computed/derived `terms.end_date_effective`, or compute the effective date live from `end_date_base + count of school-day holidays in range` rather than storing a mutating end date at all — the live-computation approach is safer, since it makes insert and delete symmetric for free with no risk of the stored value drifting from the holidays that justify it). Record the chosen approach in master-plan §7/§12 before migrating anything.
2. Confirm the exact `sessions`/`terms` schema and `uses_terms` behavior with a direct read of the live schema before writing any query (per §0's standing instruction 7).
3. Build the computation function, the `TermProgressCard` component, and wire it into every dashboard surface where it belongs.
4. Test both directions explicitly: insert a school-day holiday inside an active term, confirm the term's effective end date and the progress bar's total-week count update; then delete that same holiday, and confirm the effective end date and week count revert — this reverse case is the one the reference app never actually tested, so do not assume it works without a direct on-device check.
**Definition of Done:** Admin inserts a school-day holiday inside the current term's date range — the term-progress card (on at least the admin and teacher dashboards) visibly shows a new notch, an updated remaining-week count, and an extended effective end date, without a page reload. Admin then deletes that same holiday — the card reverts to its pre-holiday state (updated end date, notch gone, week count restored), confirmed on-device. A school with `uses_terms = false` shows a sensible equivalent (session-wide progress, or the card is hidden — per the decision made in Goal 1) rather than erroring or showing garbage dates. **Status 2026-07-10 (Session 29, third resume): ✅ Complete — both gates confirmed.** All three previously-flagged visual gaps (today-pin, holiday notches, break-impact summary) built, dashboard placement done on both `AdminDashboard.tsx`/`TeacherDashboard.tsx`, extension-eligibility signal corrected against the real `attendanceService.ts` (`qualifiesForExtension`), and `GuardianPortal.tsx` confirmed compatible via real typecheck — all per prior entries. **This pass's own finding:** the card's footer and the attendance summary card showed disagreeing day-counts for the same term (owner-reported via screenshot) because the card's `totalDays`/`elapsedDays`/`remainingDays` were calendar-day counts, not the school-day counts Problem 6.7's own spec calls for ("elapsed/total/remaining school-week counts... not a hardcoded 2"). Fixed: fields renamed to explicit `totalCalendarDays`/etc., real `totalSchoolDays`/etc. added via the same `getSchoolDayMap()` the attendance card uses, and the footer became a **School days / All days / Weeks** toggle (default: School days) rather than silently picking one and hiding the other. A separate, unrelated `npm run build` failure (`settingsService.ts`, `TS2352` — a `.select()` call passing a variable instead of a literal column-list string, breaking Supabase's type inference) was found and fixed the same session, unblocking the build gate for the whole app. **Both of this problem's Definition-of-Done gates are now confirmed, not assumed:** `npm run build` reported clean, and the on-device Done Check performed — insert/delete of a school-day holiday confirmed updating/reverting the card without reload, and the toggle confirmed showing the correct, attendance-card-matching school-day count. See PRD §10 row 29 and §11 2026-07-10 (Session 29, third resume) entry for full detail.

**Root cause:** Genuinely new scope, surfaced by the owner's clarification that the original seven-item list was illustrative, not exhaustive — see §0.4's second-pass inventory. - App header component (confirm exact file via §13 — likely inline in `App.tsx` or a dedicated `Header.tsx`) — restructure against `.header`/`.header-user-chip`/`.header-brand` pattern: brand/logo, compact identity chip, a status-badge slot (reusable, not conflict-specific — Tier 3/4 dashboards decide what it shows per role).

**Definition of Done:** ✅ **Satisfied, 2026-07-10 (Session 30).** Header, bottom nav, and Admin Dashboard visually match the reference app's structural rhythm (spacing, card shape, chip/badge treatment), confirmed on-device across both a phone-sized viewport and a larger one — a persistent, collapsible sidebar was added for every actor type (staff, teacher, guardian/student, admin, per the owner's explicit correction to Goal 1 below), bottom nav unregressed at phone width, sidebar/collapse-toggle/no-gap-at-breakpoint all confirmed at ≥1024px. The header gained a compact identity chip and a reusable (not conflict-specific) status-badge slot, wired to admin/platform_admin's pending-scores + pending-leave-requests count; a badge-count-increase toast was added as an explicitly-scoped piece of new work alongside it (not previously named in this PRD). `AdminDashboard.tsx` was reviewed in full and found already consistent with Problem 6.1's token system — no changes were needed or made. All existing actor-type nav-visibility and quick-action-intent behavior from Tiers 1–2 still works, confirmed on-device — the visual regression check passed, not just the new-feature check. `npm run build` confirmed clean by the owner. **Same-day follow-up, same problem's territory:** guardian/student's own `BottomNav.tsx` entry simplified further to just Dashboard + Library — every other slot (Schedule/Attendance/Fees/More) was already one tap away inside `GuardianPortal.tsx`'s own tab bar or not relevant to this actor type; Library is newly exposed here (previously staff-only, reachable only via More). No `App.tsx` change needed (its `library` route was already actor-type-agnostic); `Sidebar.tsx` needed no change either, since it consumes `getVisibleNavItems()` live. Both gates confirmed clean by the owner. See PRD §10 row 30 and master-plan §11 (2026-07-10, Session 30) for full implementation detail.

Each of the five modules' original Tier 5 Definition of Done still passes unchanged.
