## TIER 7 — Public-facing & platform-level additions (net-new scope)

Sequenced last because these are additive surfaces, not corrections to existing broken behavior — they don't block any of Tiers 1–6, and starting them earlier risks wasted rework (especially i18n, which should wrap finished UI copy, not copy that's still being rewritten throughout Tiers 1–6).

### Problem 7.1 — No public homepage before login *(user's #1)*
**Root cause:** This is explicitly **already specified** in the master plan but not yet built as the owner expects it. §1 states two user-facing surfaces exist by design: "A pre-login public homepage per school" and "the main application." §7.1 even documents the RLS already in place to support it (`"public can read school routing info"`, anon-readable) and `school_public_config` (§7.1, welcome message, contact info, announcements toggle) exists as a table specifically for this. However, §10 Phase 5's exit condition only says the app "shows public homepage" as an assumed step before the wizard — and nowhere in §13's manifest is there a `PublicHomepage.tsx` or equivalent file. It was designed at the schema level and never built at the component level.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §1 (two-surfaces requirement), §3.14 (subdomain-per-school — the homepage must read `window.location.hostname` before any login, exactly like the existing school-lookup step), §7.1 (`school_public_config` exact shape: `welcome_message`, `contact_info` jsonb, `show_announcements`, `custom_fields`, plus the new `welcome_post` jsonb column and `school_homepage_posts` table added this session), §4.9 (announcements visible to the public if `show_announcements` is true — reuse `announcementsService.ts`'s public-audience filtering, don't build a parallel query).
**Files touched:**
- **New file:** `src/routes/PublicHomepage.tsx` — renders `school_public_config` (welcome post, contact info, branding/logo/theme color from `schools`) plus a list of `school_homepage_posts`, with no auth required, plus a login button/link.
- `src/App.tsx` — new top-level pre-login routing branch: unauthenticated + homepage not yet acknowledged → `PublicHomepage`; "Log in" tap → `LoginPage`, which gained a back-to-homepage button.
- `src/services/publicHomepageService.ts` (new) — reuses the existing public-read RLS policy already documented in §7.1; no new RLS was needed for the base build (two further tables were added in the owner-follow-up expansion below, each with a matching public-read policy).
**Status: ✅ Complete, 2026-07-11 (PRD Session 34).** Both Definition-of-Done gates confirmed by the owner — `npm run build` reported clean, and the on-device Done Check performed and confirmed ("Npm ran cleanly, on-device testing is smooth").

**What actually shipped, across the owner's follow-up rounds in the same session (full detail in master plan §11, 2026-07-11 entry):**
1. **Base build** — `PublicHomepage.tsx`/`publicHomepageService.ts` built and wired, styled to match `LoginPage.tsx`'s existing visual language. The anonymous-read RLS policy was reconfirmed still in place via direct SQL before building on top of it, not assumed from this PRD's own note.
2. **Structured welcome post + login back button** (owner follow-up) — plain-text `welcome_message` upgraded to a real post (`welcome_post` jsonb column, additive — old data untouched, used as a fallback) with heading/body/images, editable via a new block editor in `SettingsModule.tsx`. `LoginPage.tsx` gained an `onBack` prop, deliberately not wired on the wrong-school security-redirect branch.
3. **Four real regressions found and fixed** (owner-reported, not misunderstandings): the "full page" layout was still centered on desktop (fixed with a genuine two-column grid at ≥1024px); no admin UI existed to create additional posts despite the CRUD already being built (wired in); a long post didn't collapse (root cause: `-webkit-line-clamp` doesn't reliably truncate across multiple block-level children — replaced with a max-height-plus-fade clamp); no per-post "always expanded" toggle existed (added).
4. **Homepage moved to its own gated Settings tab + rich text + brand color** (owner follow-up) — Homepage content separated from the School tab into its own tab; each post gated behind its own title (no edit/delete until opened, delete requires typing the title back — real mistake-prevention, not a generic confirm); H2–H4/bold/italic per-block controls added, grounded in a real web search on standard rich-text-editor conventions before building; brand-color accents swept across the whole public page (accents only, no background fills, per the owner's confirmed scope).
5. **Logo upload** (separate owner follow-up, same session: "check the logo upload... improve/correct it without affecting its downstream") — every downstream consumer of `logo_url` (five call sites) traced before building, confirming an upload feature is safe to add without touching any of them; `uploadSchoolLogo()` added, its own `school-logos` bucket, the School tab's plain URL field replaced with a real upload button, then simplified to upload-only per a further owner follow-up removing the paste-a-link fallback.

**New schema, this session (see master plan §7.1 for full detail):** `school_public_config.welcome_post jsonb` (additive column); `school_homepage_posts` table (new, one-to-many, RLS mirrors every other public-facing table in this Tier); `school-homepage-images` and `school-logos` public Storage buckets (both public rather than private+signed-URL, since these images render on the anonymous public homepage/login page — a signed-URL round trip would cost an extra request on that high-traffic surface with no session available to refresh an expired one).



### Problem 7.2 — Language setting and other Settings controls are non-functional *(user's #9)*
**Root cause:** Two distinct sub-problems requiring different treatment:
1. **Language switching specifically:** §2 ("What carries forward as patterns, not as code") explicitly lists "Three-language i18n (English/Arabic/Hausa) with RTL/LTR" as a pattern from the *old, abandoned* app — meaning the actual translation infrastructure was deliberately left behind in the pivot and has never been rebuilt in the new codebase. The `default_lang`/`enabled_langs` columns exist and the Settings UI writes to them correctly (§11 session 24), but there is nothing downstream consuming that value to actually translate any UI string. The control is wired to the database correctly; the database value has no effect because no i18n engine exists.
2. **Other non-functional Settings items:** needs a full audit — §13's `settingsService.ts` entry only documents School branding as fully wired; it doesn't enumerate every settings tab, so other tabs may share Tier 3's exact defect (writing a value with no UI ever reading it) rather than i18n's defect (no engine at all).
**Master plan sections to observe:** §4.5 (full section — this is the complete, non-negotiable spec for what i18n must look like once built: RTL flips the *entire* interface not just `dir="rtl"`, school name is *never* translated, Eastern Arabic numerals are configurable, every key must exist in all three languages before a component ships — "no fallback to English for missing keys in production"), §12 (locked answer: "Languages? English + Arabic + Hausa; English is system default" — this scope is already decided, do not re-ask it), §3.16-style discipline (do not let any per-component logic branch on language ad hoc — route everything through one i18n function).
**Files touched:**
- **New:** an i18n engine (e.g. a lightweight key→string lookup keyed by `enabled_langs`, a `t(key)` function exposed via Zustand `uiStore` per §5's platform-agnostic state requirement — do not use a browser-only i18n library that assumes `window`/`document` directly, since §5.1 requires this be portable to React Native later).
- **Every existing component** — every hardcoded English string in every module (`AdminDashboard.tsx`, `TimetableModule.tsx`, etc. — all ten-plus files) needs its strings extracted to translation keys. This is a large, mechanical pass and should be done **module by module**, matching the same order modules were originally built in, to keep the diff reviewable.
- RTL: a top-level layout flip (likely a `dir` attribute toggle plus token-level start/end logical properties rather than left/right — per §4.5's instruction this must flip "icon positions, padding sides, swipe directions, navigation alignment," which argues for adopting CSS logical properties — `margin-inline-start` instead of `margin-left`, etc. — across the existing token system rather than a one-off RTL stylesheet).
- `settingsService.ts` and each Settings tab — separately, audit every non-branding tab (whatever exists beyond "School") to confirm each write path has a corresponding UI read path, applying the same Tier-3-style fix wherever a control is found to be cosmetically wired but functionally inert.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. **Decide scope honestly before starting:** full 3-language i18n with RTL is a substantial build, not a small fix. Given this PRD's Tier 6 visual pass and Tier 4 dashboard work will keep changing UI copy, recommend explicitly sequencing this as the *last* item in this entire PRD, and in the interim, hiding the language switcher in Settings (or marking it "Coming soon") rather than leaving a control that silently does nothing — this single change removes the "broken" feeling immediately while the real engine is built properly.
2. Build the `t(key)` engine and `uiStore` wiring.
3. Convert one module fully (recommend the smallest, e.g. Announcements) as a proof of pattern, including RTL verification, before repeating across the rest.
4. Separately and in parallel, audit remaining Settings tabs against the Tier-3 pattern.
**Definition of Done:** Switching the language in Settings changes visible UI text app-wide (not just one module) for all three languages with zero fallback-to-English gaps, and switching to Arabic visibly mirrors the entire layout (nav, padding, icon positions) — confirmed on-device, with the school's own name visibly unchanged across all three language modes per §4.5's explicit rule.

---

## 7a. TIER 8 — Launch-blocking issues (first real school's enrollment)

Opened 2026-07-11 per the owner's launch-focus pivot (see §0.1's tier table). Rows 28/33/35–43 remain owner-deferred until this tier's scope is live and stable. Populated below as issues are found and root-caused together with the owner, per this PRD's own verification discipline (§0.2) — nothing here is marked done without an on-device confirmation.

### Problem 8.1 — Teacher's own account shows no teaching assignments for the current session *(flagged 2026-07-12, Results walkthrough)*
**Root cause:** `ResultsModule.tsx` resolved its own `staffId` via a raw `supabase.auth.getUser()` call, storing `data.user.id` — the Supabase Auth user ID. But `loadMyTeachingAssignments(staffId, sessionId)` filters `teaching_assignments.staff_id`, which per §7.11 is a FK to `staff(id)` — a different row, joined only via `app_identities.auth_user_id → actor_record_id`. This meant the query always compared the wrong UUID and returned zero rows for any real teacher, even though `teaching_assignments` had correct data — confirmed because `TimetableModule.tsx` and the teacher dashboard, which both already resolve `actorRecordId` correctly, showed the same assignments fine. Same bug class Session 9 found and fixed in `AttendanceModule.tsx`'s `markedBy`, and Finance found and fixed in `payments.received_by` / `fee_discounts.approved_by`.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §3.2-adjacent identity-resolution convention (every write/filter against a `staff(id)` FK must resolve through `authStore`'s `actorRecordId`, never a raw `auth.getUser()` call) — this is now the third module found repeating this exact defect, worth flagging as a pattern to grep for proactively across any remaining untouched modules rather than waiting for each to surface individually.
**Files touched:**
- `src/routes/ResultsModule.tsx` — removed the ad hoc `staffId` state + `auth.getUser()` effect; replaced with `useAuthStore((s) => s.actorRecordId) ?? ''`, matching the established fix pattern. Also corrects `entered_by`/`approved_by` on score-save handlers, which shared the same wrong-UUID source and would have hit the identical FK-mismatch failure Session 9 found in Attendance, just not yet surfaced because the empty-assignments bug blocked teachers before any save was attempted.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Fix applied and esbuild-parse-verified (static check only — no `tsc`/`authStore.ts`/`resultsService.ts` FK types available in the sandbox this fix was made in).
2. **On-device confirmation (partial — see result below).**
**Definition of Done:** Teacher logs in, opens Results, and sees their real subject/class assignments for the current session with a working score-entry grid. **Status: assignment list confirmed fixed on-device (2026-07-12 — teacher now sees "Agricultural Science — Aji 2" with correct student roster). Score-entry grid itself is blocked by Problem 8.3 below — DoD not yet fully met.**

### Problem 8.2 — HR staff detail panel misfiles all current-session teaching assignments under "Teaching History" *(flagged 2026-07-12, HR walkthrough)*
**Root cause:** The "Currently teaching" / "Teaching history" split in the staff detail panel reads `assignmentOptions?.currentSessionId` on every render, but `assignmentOptions` was only ever populated lazily, on Edit-mode entry (`ensureAssignmentOptionsLoaded()`, called from `startEditingStaff()`). Opening the detail panel without clicking Edit left `currentSessionId` as `undefined`, so `current = currentId ? staffAssignments.filter(...) : []` always evaluated to empty, and `pastAssignments = staffAssignments.filter(a => a.session_id !== currentId)` put every real row — including current-session ones — into history, since `!== undefined` is true for any real session ID. `staffAssignments` itself loaded correctly and eagerly; only the comparison value was late.
**Master plan sections to observe:** Same identity/data-availability-timing discipline as 8.1 — a value consumed by a render-time comparison must be loaded no later than the data it's being compared against, not gated behind a separate, later user action.
**Files touched:**
- `src/routes/HRModule.tsx` — `openStaffDetail()` now also calls `ensureAssignmentOptionsLoaded()` (already a no-op once `assignmentOptionsState === 'ready'`, so no duplicate work when Edit is later opened), so `currentSessionId` is available the moment the panel opens.
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Fix applied and esbuild-parse-verified (static check only).
2. **On-device confirmation still outstanding — not yet walked by the owner.**
**Definition of Done:** Admin opens any staff member's detail panel (without clicking Edit) and sees current-session assignments correctly under "Currently teaching," with only prior-session assignments under "Teaching history." **Status: fix written, not yet confirmed on-device.**

### Problem 8.3 — No school-configurable assessment structure; score-entry grid has no columns to enter data into *(flagged 2026-07-12, Results walkthrough, confirmed via SQL: zero `score_components` rows for Dilimi)*
**Root cause:** `score_components` (§7.14) is a real, school-scoped table, and `ResultsModule.tsx`'s grid already correctly renders one column per row returned from it — but nothing in the app has ever written to this table. `SettingsModule.tsx`'s full tab list (`school | homepage | calendar | schedule | classes | subjects | roles | student-roles | staff | audit`) has no assessment/grading tab, and `settingsService.ts` has zero references to `score_components`, `grade_scales`, or `report_card_templates`. This is not Dilimi-specific — no school on the platform has any way to define what its CA/Exam structure even is, meaning no teacher anywhere could enter a single score today. Confirmed via direct SQL (`select * from score_components where school_id = ...` → zero rows) rather than inferred.
**Decision (owner discussion, 2026-07-12):** rather than patch the existing static, school-wide `score_components` table with a settings screen, the owner opted to redesign this properly around a new **Assessment Window** concept — see Problem 8.4 for the full design, which supersedes and subsumes this problem. Problem 8.3 stays open only as the originating symptom; its Definition of Done is met by Problem 8.4's DoD, not by a standalone fix to the old table.
**Status 2026-08-26:** structurally closed by the Tier 8 build (Log row 72) — windows/profiles/components are now configurable (Settings > Assessments) and the grid consumes real per-class columns; the empty grid now explains itself instead of failing silently. DoD formally met via Problem 8.4's once its own on-device gates are observed.

### Problem 8.4 — Net-new: Assessment Window system (replaces static `score_components`; unifies CA/exam weighting, exam timetable + invigilation/venues, grade bands, promotion) *(owner design session, 2026-07-12)*
**Root cause:** Not a bug — a structural gap. Term-end processes (score entry, weighting, exam scheduling, invigilation, promotion, report cards, grade bands, remarks) exist today as unconnected islands: `score_components` is static and school-wide with no per-section variation and no UI to manage it (Problem 8.3); `report_card_templates` exists but nothing generates from it; `promotion_rules`/`promotion_history` exist but nothing triggers them; there is no concept of an exam timetable, no venues table, no invigilator assignment, no class-teacher remark field anywhere in the schema, and no aggregate (overall-average) remark band, only a subject-level `grade_scales`. The owner's proposed fix — an "Exam Events" concept — was worked through in full across a dedicated design discussion and is documented here as the resulting spec. **This is net-new schema and UI, not a fix to existing code — flagging explicitly per master-plan instruction that every table below is proposed and must be reviewed/accepted into §7 before migration, not silently added.**
**Master plan sections to observe:** §7's table conventions (`id uuid`, `created_at`, `updated_at`, `school_id` or correct parent scoping, RLS in the same migration per §3.2, for every new table below), §3.6 (optional-terms duality — windows must work whether or not `uses_terms` is true, scoped to `session_id`/`term` the same way `sessions` already handles it), §10 (this is a new roadmap phase-level addition, not a fold-in to an existing Phase — needs its own explicit roadmap entry once scoped into sessions), §12 (does not conflict with any existing locked Q&A decision reviewed so far — confirm against the full list before migration).

**Proposed schema (full shape, as designed with the owner):**

- **`assessment_windows`** — the shell. `id, school_id, session_id, term, name, scope ('whole_school'|'selected_classes'), scores_open_at, scores_submission_deadline, scores_close_at` (these three are *defaults*, overridable per class below), `status ('draft'|'open'|'closed'|'finalized'), created_by, created_at`. One per term in the common case; represents one round of assessment.

- **`assessment_window_classes`** — junction table carrying everything that varies by class/section within a window: `id, assessment_window_id, class_node_id, score_component_profile_id, is_promotional (per-class override — e.g. true for JSS1–2/SS1–2, false for JSS3/SS3 WAEC-track), sits_exam, scores_open_at, scores_submission_deadline, scores_close_at` (per-class, default-filled from the window when a class is attached, individually adjustable — this is what allows Primary to close a week before Secondary inside the same window).

- **`score_component_profiles`** — `id, assessment_window_id, name` (e.g. "Primary weighting," "Secondary weighting"). Lets one window hold multiple weighting formulas; a school needing only one just creates a single profile and every class points to it.

- **`score_components`** — becomes profile-scoped, not school-scoped: `id, score_component_profile_id, name` (1st CA / 2nd CA / 3rd CA / Exam / etc.), `weight_pct, max_score` (new — lets teachers enter "out of 20" instead of mentally converting to a percentage), `sort_order`. This is the table `ResultsModule.tsx`'s existing grid rendering already correctly consumes — populating it is what actually unblocks score entry.

- **`exam_timetable_entries`** — `id, assessment_window_id, class_node_id, subject_id, exam_date, start_time, end_time, venue_id, created_by, created_at`. Must surface inside `TimetableModule.tsx` itself (overlay/replace the relevant day's view for any date inside a class's active exam period), not as a standalone screen — file not yet reviewed to confirm the exact merge point.

- **`exam_invigilators`** — `id, exam_timetable_entry_id, staff_id, role ('lead'|'assistant')`.

- **`venues`** — new, does not currently exist in §7: `id, school_id, name, capacity`. Required before `exam_timetable_entries.venue_id` can be a real FK rather than free text.

- **`grade_scales`** — extends existing table, confirmed **per-school, fixed policy** (not per-window) per owner decision: `min_score, max_score, label, remark` — subject-level grade bands (A1/B2/C4.../"Excellent"/"Credit"/"Fail").

- **`aggregate_remark_bands`** — new, per-school fixed policy: `id, school_id, min_average, max_average, remark` — a separate overall-performance remark based on a student's average across all subjects in the window, distinct from any single subject's grade; this is what populates the general comment band on a printed report card.

- **`promotion_rules`** — extends existing table, per-school fixed policy: confirm/add `min_average_pct` (the mass-promotion cutoff).

- **`promotion_history`** — extends existing table: `decision` enum extended to `'auto_promoted'|'manually_promoted'|'manually_retained'|'demoted'`, plus `from_class_node_id`/`to_class_node_id`/`decided_by`/`decided_at` if not already present — full audit trail per promotion decision.

- **Class teacher's remark** — new field, one per student per assessment window (not per subject, matching how it actually appears on a report card) — exact table placement (own table vs. column on an existing per-student-per-window row) still to be decided at build time.

**Clash detection (exam timetable):** three dimensions, checked at save time rather than as a hard schema constraint for v1 — a class cannot have two overlapping exam entries; a staff member cannot invigilate two overlapping entries; a venue cannot host two overlapping entries beyond its capacity.

**Promotion flow (owner-specified, 2026-07-12):**
1. **Sequencing gate:** "Promote Class" is blocked unless the destination class has already been cleared (promoted out) first — promotion must proceed top-down, senior classes before junior, so a freshly promoted cohort is never mixed into a destination class that still holds its current occupants. Blocked attempts must show a clear, specific message naming what needs to happen first (which class, which session) — not a silent disable.
2. **Auto-promotion:** every student at or above `promotion_rules.min_average_pct` (computed from that window's `subject_results`) is promoted automatically on click.
3. **Manual review:** every student below the cutoff is surfaced in an explicit review list — never silently retained — for the admin to individually promote anyway, retain, or demote.
4. Every decision, automatic or manual, writes a `promotion_history` row.

**Explicitly out of scope for this build:** exam-room seating logic beyond simple capacity/overlap checks; any concept of *simultaneous, independently-run* windows within one term (the multi-profile + numbered-component design above was chosen specifically to remove the need for this — one window, multiple weighting profiles and multiple components, covers the same real-world cases with far less duplicated setup).

**Goals before moving on:**
1. Get the full proposed schema above reviewed and accepted into master plan §7 (or redesigned) before any migration is written, per §0.1's own instruction to check there before inventing table/column names.
2. Sequence the build: `assessment_windows` + `assessment_window_classes` + `score_component_profiles` + `score_components` first (this alone unblocks Problem 8.3/8.1's score-entry grid) → exam timetable + venues + invigilators → grade_scales/aggregate_remark_bands settings UI → promotion flow + sequencing gate. Report-card generation (already a known gap from the original Results review) depends on all of the above being live before it can be meaningfully built.
3. Build the admin-facing settings UI for each layer as it's built — schema alone repeats the exact "configured a feature with no UI to use it" pattern already seen elsewhere in this project (Problem 7.2's language-switcher precedent).
4. `TimetableModule.tsx` needs to be reviewed (not yet done) to confirm the correct merge point for overlaying exam-period days.
**Definition of Done:** Admin creates an assessment window for a term, attaches classes (with per-class weighting profile and dates where needed), builds an exam timetable with venues/invigilators (visible inside the regular Timetable module), a teacher enters scores against real weighted columns before the window's close date, the window closes, grade_scales/aggregate_remark_bands produce correct labels, and "Promote Class" run top-down correctly gates on destination-class clearing, auto-promotes above cutoff, and surfaces below-cutoff students for manual decision — all confirmed on-device, not just build-clean.

### Problem 8.6 — Net-new: Scheme of Work per subject/teaching assignment *(owner request, 2026-07-12, grounded in research on Nigerian scheme-of-work norms and comparable SIS platforms before proposing structure)*
**Root cause:** Not a bug — a structural gap, same class as Problem 8.4. No concept of a scheme of work exists anywhere in the schema. Term-end assessment (8.4) governs *what gets scored*; scheme of work governs *what gets taught, when* — a separate but related term-long planning artifact per teacher/subject/class, currently entirely undocumented and unbuilt. Researched before proposing structure, not invented cold: a scheme of work is a term-long, per-subject-per-class plan broken into weekly rows (topic, objectives, activities, resources), submitted by the subject teacher and reviewed by HOD/admin — standard Nigerian practice is that schemes are submitted to school administration at the start of each term, pulled from the WAEC/NECO or NERDC syllabus rather than written from memory. Comparable SIS platforms (e.g. SchoolWorkSpace) treat it as a real object with downstream consequences, not a static document: broken into weeks, coverage tracked against it, visible to admin — the operational payoff research consistently flags as the difference between a form that gets filed and a tool that actually gets used.
**Master plan sections to observe:** §3.6 (optional-terms duality — must work whether or not `uses_terms` is true), §3.7 (new permission keys via the existing catalog mechanism, not a bespoke gate), §7's table conventions, the identity-resolution convention already fixed in Problem 8.1 (`teaching_assignment_id` as the anchor, never a raw auth id). See new §7.15a for the full schema.
**Sequencing decision (owner, 2026-07-12):** No FK dependency on Problem 8.4's Assessment Window tables — deliberately decoupled (content pacing vs. scoring/weighting are separate concerns; a school can have one without the other). Despite being independently buildable in parallel with 8.4's Sessions 46–50, owner elected to sequence this **after** 8.4 closes, not alongside it, since 8.4 is the bigger, already-moving critical path and splitting attention across both risks landing both late — a resourcing call, not a technical dependency.
**Proposed schema (full shape — see master plan §7.15a for the complete table definitions and rationale):**
- **`schemes_of_work`** — the shell, one per `teaching_assignment_id` per `session_id`/`term`. `status` lifecycle `draft → submitted → approved`, with `returned` as a revise-and-resubmit path (`review_note` required by the UI on return) — never a silent/terminal rejection.
- **`scheme_of_work_weeks`** — the weekly rows: `week_number, topic, objectives, activities, resources`, plus per-week `coverage_status` (`planned`/`covered`/`partially_covered`/`not_covered`) and `coverage_note`, marked by the teacher as the term actually progresses — this is what makes coverage tracking live rather than a one-time submission.
**New permission keys:** `academics.scheme_of_work.manage` (teacher: create/edit/submit own), `academics.scheme_of_work.approve` (HOD/admin: approve or return with note), `academics.scheme_of_work.view_all` (admin: read across all teachers).
**Goals before moving on:**
1. Get the schema above reviewed and accepted into master plan §7.15a (done as part of this same session — see master plan) before any migration is written.
2. Add the three new permission keys to the live `permissions` catalog.
3. Migrate `schemes_of_work` + `scheme_of_work_weeks`, RLS included in the same migration per §3.2.
4. Build the teacher-facing create/edit/submit flow, with `total_weeks` suggested (not enforced) from `school_calendar_days` where available (Tier 5.2).
5. Build the HOD/admin-facing review queue: approve, or return with a required note.
6. Build the teacher-facing weekly coverage-marking UI, reachable once a scheme is `approved`.
7. Build the admin-facing coverage-at-a-glance view across teachers/subjects — the actual operational payoff, not just schema + a submission form.
**Definition of Done:** Teacher creates a scheme of work for a real teaching assignment, adds weekly rows, submits it; admin/HOD sees it in a review queue, approves it (or returns it with a note, teacher edits and resubmits); once approved, teacher marks weeks covered/partially covered/not covered as the term progresses; admin can view coverage status across teachers at a glance — all confirmed on-device, not just build-clean.

### Problem 8.7 — Net-new: Higher-institution academic model (universities, polytechnics, colleges of education) — the real first enrolling school *(owner design session, 2026-07-12, grounded in research on Nigerian NUC/NBTE/NCCE academic norms before proposing structure)*

**Root cause:** Not a bug — the largest structural gap surfaced in this project so far. Every table and screen built to date (`class_nodes`, `teaching_assignments`, daily `attendance_records`, subject-level term scoring feeding Problem 8.4's Assessment Window design) assumes a *secondary-school* academic model: a student belongs to one class, that class is a fixed daily container, and results are a per-term subject grade. The school actually enrolling first is a higher institution (university/polytechnic/college of education), whose real academic unit of scope is fundamentally different: a student's real semester load is the specific set of courses they *registered* for, not a class they belong to. Confirmed via direct research before proposing structure, not assumed: Nigerian universities (NUC-regulated) use a 5.0 CGPA scale; polytechnics (NBTE-regulated, ND/HND) use a 4.0 scale with different classification bands (Distinction/Upper Credit/Lower Credit/Pass); Colleges of Education (NCCE-regulated) mandate a fixed 40:60 CA:Exam split. Even within one regulator's institutions, the CGPA rollup method (weighted-average-of-all-attempted-units vs. average-of-semester-GPAs) and retake treatment (Replacement vs. Dilution model) genuinely differ school to school — a vendor building this kind of software professionally (FlexiSAF) reports having seen "at least 10 variations" of probation policy alone across real Nigerian institutions, and concluded the only workable approach is a fully configurable rules engine, not a hardcoded policy. This directly validates treating every academic-standing rule, grading scale, and CGPA formula as school-level configuration data, the same "school configures the policy, the engine evaluates it" philosophy already locked in for Problem 8.4's secondary-school promotion design — but built as genuinely separate schema, not forced into the same tables, since the underlying computation (subject-level term grade vs. credit-unit-weighted GPA rollup across a whole programme) is structurally different.

**Master plan sections to observe:** §3.16 (`school_type`/`node_type` are inert, never branched on — this problem is the moment that rule's stated future arrives: `school_type` becomes load-bearing for the first time, which is a locked-architecture change and must be recorded in §12 before any code reads it, not discovered later in a diff), §3.7 (permission catalog — every new institution action gets a real key from day one, not a blunt `isAdmin` check, avoiding a second Problem-3.1-class defect), §3.2 (RLS conventions, reused as-is), §7's table conventions (every table below is proposed, not accepted, per §0's own instruction 7 — must be reviewed into §7 before migration), the audit-trail pattern already designed for `promotion_history` in Problem 8.4 (reused for academic standing below).

**What deliberately stays shared vs. forks, decided before any schema was drafted:** Reused directly — the permission-catalog mechanism, RLS conventions, the `promotion_history` audit-trail pattern, `venues`/exam-timetable/invigilator concepts from Problem 8.4's design, and every already-completed Tier 6 design-token/icon/modal/navigation-shell asset (new institution screens are new routes inside the existing shell, not a new frame). Deliberately forked, not reused: `class_nodes` does **not** become "programme level" — a secondary class is a fixed daily container a student belongs to; a university level is administrative metadata a student's course registrations happen to cluster under, not a thing they attend. Forcing this into `class_nodes` would recreate the exact "inert flag becomes a landmine" risk this project's own Problem 6.1 root-cause writeup already diagnosed once. Likewise, `score_components` (secondary's CA:Exam weighting) is not reused for GPA — same underlying philosophy, two separate schema families, reviewed together for pattern consistency when Problem 8.4 resumes, not merged.

**Sequencing decision (owner, 2026-07-12):** Given the real enrolling school is a higher institution, this problem now leads Tier 8's remaining build work, ahead of Problems 8.4/8.6 — those remain fully designed and are resumed, not dropped, once this problem's scope is live and stable, the same "owner-deferred, not dropped" language already used for Rows 28/33/35–43. Within this problem's own five phases, sequencing is a hard technical dependency, not a preference: Phase 2 (course registration) cannot be built before Phase 1 (institutional hierarchy) exists to register a student *into*; Phase 3 (per-course attendance) and Phase 4 (GPA) both read `course_registrations` and cannot start before Phase 2 closes; Phase 5 (carryover/spillover/academic standing) depends on Phase 4's GPA computation being correct, since standing rules evaluate CGPA.

**Phase 0 — decisions to lock before any code (owner sign-off required, per §12 discipline, before Phase 1 starts):**
1. **Institution category at onboarding.** The onboarding wizard's school-type step gains a second-level choice once "Higher Institution" is picked: University / Polytechnic or Monotechnic / College of Education / Other. Pre-fills sensible defaults (grading scale, CA:Exam split, credit-load minimums) per the research above, but every value stays admin-editable afterward, since even schools of the same category vary.
2. **GPA rollup method and retake treatment** — the single highest-stakes value in this entire problem, since it determines whether every transcript the school produces is correct. Must be an explicit school-level setting (`cgpa_policy.rollup_method`: `weighted_all_attempts` vs. `average_of_semester_gpas`; `cgpa_policy.retake_treatment`: `replacement` vs. `dilution`), never assumed or hardcoded.
3. **Programme levels are administrative metadata, not a live daily container** — a student's real weekly schedule derives from their `course_registrations`, not from "which level they're in," confirming the fork decision above.
4. **No separate "carryover roster" screen** — carryover status is a flag on the individual `course_registrations` row (`is_carryover`) and surfaces wherever that student's registrations already show, not a parallel UI surface.
5. **Per-institution-type feature gating stays soft** — every control is configurable; the onboarding-time category choice only pre-fills and lightly guides toward the regulator norm (e.g. NCCE's fixed 40:60 split for Colleges of Education), it never hard-locks a field, since even regulator-mandated norms have been observed to vary by institution in practice.

**Status as of 2026-07-13 (see SESSION 54 in §10 and Log rows 49–50 for the full conversation):** All five decisions above confirmed with the owner; mechanisms unchanged from the original design in every case. The real enrolling school's institution category is resolved as a **school of nursing/midwifery**, selected as **"Other"** for now (exercising Decision 5 exactly as designed — "Other" is a fully-functional path, not a fallback failure state). This surfaced a genuine gap the four-category list above didn't anticipate: schools of nursing/midwifery are regulated by the **Nursing and Midwifery Council of Nigeria (NMCN)**, a real fourth Nigerian tertiary regulator distinct from NUC/NBTE/NCCE, whose Basic Nursing programme is a fixed national curriculum culminating in an external, twice-yearly, three-attempt-capped professional licensing exam rather than an internally-computed CGPA/degree classification. **Open, must be resolved before Phase 4 (SESSION 59):** whether this school wants/needs Phase 4's GPA/CGPA machinery at all, or only pass/fail-per-course tracking feeding toward professional-exam eligibility — owner is asking the school directly. NMCN may warrant its own proper institution-category treatment (a possible sixth Phase-1 addendum, alongside University/Polytechnic/College-of-Education/Other) once that answer is known, rather than remaining permanently under "Other."

**Proposed schema — Phase 1 (Institutional Hierarchy & Identity):**
- **`faculties`** — `id, school_id, name, code`. Nullable tier — not every institution has one (some polytechnics and colleges of education go straight to department).
- **`departments`** — `id, school_id, faculty_id (nullable), name, code`.
- **`programmes`** — `id, school_id, department_id, name, award_type` (degree/ND/HND/NCE/PGD/etc.), `duration_sessions, min_credit_load, max_credit_load`.
- **`programme_levels`** — `id, programme_id, level_label` ("100", "ND1", etc.), `sequence_order`.
- **`students`** addition: `matric_number text`, nullable, unique per `school_id`, format left school-configurable (numbering conventions genuinely differ by institution — session-year + programme code + sequence is common, not universal).

**Proposed schema — Phase 2 (Course Catalogue & Registration — the central object everything else reads from):**
- **`courses`** — `id, school_id, department_id, code, title, credit_units, course_type` (lecture/lab/practical/TP).
- **`programme_curriculum`** — `id, programme_id, programme_level_id, course_id, semester, requirement_type` (core/elective/GST), `prerequisite_course_id` (nullable).
- **`course_offerings`** — `id, course_id, session_id, semester, staff_id` (lecturer), `venue_id` (reuses Problem 8.4's `venues` design once accepted).
- **`course_registrations`** — `id, student_id, course_id, session_id, semester, status, is_carryover boolean, original_registration_id` (nullable self-FK linking a carryover attempt back to the original failed attempt). This single table plays the role class-membership and `teaching_assignments` jointly play for secondary schools — every later phase reads from this table and nothing else, the same "one anchor, everything reads from it" discipline the PRD used when it fixed the `staffId` identity bug in Problem 8.1.

**Proposed schema — Phase 3 (Per-Course Attendance):**
- **`course_attendance_sessions`** — `id, course_offering_id, session_date, week_number` — one row per actual lecture/contact-hour held, not one row per calendar day.
- **`course_attendance_records`** — `id, course_attendance_session_id, student_id, status` — reuses the existing attendance status values; register composition is computed live from `course_registrations` for that offering, not stored redundantly.
- **`course_offerings`** addition: `attendance_eligibility_threshold_pct` (school/lecturer-configurable, not hardcoded at the commonly-cited 75% — confirmed by research as a real, common Nigerian tertiary mechanism where a minimum attendance percentage gates exam eligibility), surfaced as an informational computed flag in this phase; wiring it to actually block exam entry is Phase 4 scope, once scoring exists to block against.

**Proposed schema — Phase 4 (Scoring, GPA, CGPA):**
- **`grade_scales`** (extended) — `school_id, scale_max` (5.0 / 4.0 / other), `letter, min_pct, max_pct, grade_point` — school-owned rows, not a constant.
- **`cgpa_policy`** — `school_id, rollup_method, retake_treatment, min_credit_load, max_credit_load` — the Phase 0 Decision 2 table.
- **`course_results`** — `id, course_registration_id, score, letter_grade, grade_point, finalized_at`.
- **`student_gpa_snapshots`** — `id, student_id, session_id, semester` (nullable for CGPA rows), `gpa, cgpa, total_units_attempted, total_quality_points` — cached and recomputed on score finalization, not derived live on every read, per §4.1's caching discipline.
- **Computation, written out explicitly so it is not reinvented ad hoc in code:** per course, `quality_points = grade_point × credit_units`. Semester GPA = (sum of quality_points that semester) ÷ (sum of credit_units attempted that semester). CGPA under `weighted_all_attempts`: (sum of quality_points across every attempt, every session) ÷ (sum of credit_units across every attempt, every session). CGPA under `average_of_semester_gpas`: straight arithmetic mean of each completed semester's GPA. Retake handling under `replacement`: a passed retake's quality points replace the failed attempt's in the calculation (the failed attempt still shows on the transcript, annotated as superseded, but is excluded from the CGPA math). Under `dilution`: both attempts remain in both the numerator and denominator permanently.

**Proposed schema — Phase 5 (Carryover, Spillover, Academic Standing):**
- A carryover (a single failed course, retaken next time it's offered — student stays with their cohort) and a spillover (a student who has exceeded their normal programme duration and is now outside their original cohort, with real financial/scholarship consequences per the research) are two related but genuinely different objects, not one flag at two severities.
- **`academic_standing_rules`** — `school_id, probation_cgpa_threshold, withdrawal_cgpa_threshold, max_carryover_courses_before_review, max_registration_multiplier` (the residency-cap equivalent of the commonly-cited 1.5× normal programme duration) — every value school-configured, confirmed necessary by the "10 variations" finding above.
- **`academic_standing_events`** — `id, student_id, session_id, standing` (good_standing/probation/spillover/withdrawal_recommended), `cgpa_at_evaluation, triggered_by, note` — an audit trail mirroring the `promotion_history` pattern already designed in Problem 8.4.
- **Evaluation flow, run at session-end:** compute CGPA + outstanding-carryover count per student → compare against `academic_standing_rules` → below `probation_cgpa_threshold` or above `max_registration_multiplier` always logs a review-pending event, **never a silent automatic status change** — a human (admin/registrar) must review and confirm, the same "never silent retention, mandatory manual review" principle already locked into Problem 8.4's promotion design for secondary schools.

**New permission keys:** `academics.course_registration.manage`, `academics.course_registration.view_own`, `academics.curriculum.manage`, `academics.institution_structure.manage` (faculties/departments/programmes/levels), `academics.cgpa_policy.manage`, `academics.academic_standing.review`.

**How this connects to every other part of the existing project (so nothing downstream is surprised later, per §0.3's own standing-list discipline):**
- **Onboarding wizard (Tier 2):** gains the institution-category branch (Phase 0/1). The existing secondary-school path is unchanged — an additional branch, not a modification.
- **Permissions catalog (Tier 3):** every new action gated on a real key from day one, per Problem 3.1's established conversion pattern — no institution feature ships on a blunt `isAdmin` check.
- **Role-specific dashboards (Tier 4):** a lecturer's dashboard reuses `TeacherDashboard.tsx`'s shape, scoped to course offerings and pending score entries instead of classes/subjects — same component pattern, different data source.
- **Attendance (Tier 5.1/5.2, Tier 6.9):** the daily secondary-school attendance table and flow are completely untouched — Phase 3 is a parallel system. `school_calendar_days` (Problem 5.2) still applies: a `course_attendance_session` should not be creatable on a school-calendar holiday.
- **Results module (Tier 5, Tier 8.4):** `ResultsModule.tsx` gains an institution-aware branch rendering the course-registration/GPA view from Phase 4 in place of the secondary subject/term grid. Problem 8.1's `staffId` identity-resolution fix (`authStore.actorRecordId`, not raw `auth.getUser()`) is reused as-is for lecturer identity resolution — same bug class, same fix.
- **Finance module:** not built in this problem's phases, but flagged: tuition/fee structures for higher institutions are commonly per-session or per-semester, and spillover students often face separate, higher registration fees per the research. Recommend a follow-up Problem 8.8 once Phase 5 closes, rather than folding it in here.
- **Design system (Tier 6):** no new design language needed — every new screen (course catalogue, registration, per-course register, GPA/transcript view) uses the already-completed token system, icon set, modal pattern, and navigation shell.
- **Guardian/Student portal:** a higher-institution guardian/student sees registered courses, GPA/CGPA, and attendance-eligibility standing in place of the class-based summary secondary guardians see — same portal shell, institution-aware content branch.
- **i18n (Tier 7, still owner-deferred):** no special handling needed now — every new string is written the same translatable-key-ready way Tier 6 already committed to, so the eventual i18n sweep picks these up in one pass.

**Goals before moving on:**
1. Confirm Phase 0's five decisions with the owner and record them in master plan §12 before any schema is migrated.
2. Get the full schema above reviewed and accepted into master plan §7 (§7.27) before any migration is written, per §0's own instruction 7.
3. Build in the phase order above — Phase 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5 — since Phases 3/4/5 each read directly from the immediately preceding phase's tables and cannot be meaningfully tested without them.
4. At the close of each phase, confirm that phase's own Definition of Done on-device before starting the next — per §0.2's verification rule, no exceptions for this problem despite its size.

**Definition of Done (full problem, composed of five phase-level gates — see §10 SESSIONS 54–63 for the per-session breakdown):** Admin at the real institution creates their actual faculty/department/programme/level structure and a real curriculum on-device; one real student is admitted with a correctly formatted matriculation number, registered into a real mix of core and elective courses (including a manually-seeded carryover, correctly pre-populated and flagged); a lecturer takes per-course attendance across several real sessions with marks persisting on reload; a lecturer finalizes a real score and the student's semester GPA and running CGPA are both correct by hand-calculation cross-check under the school's configured `rollup_method`/`retake_treatment`; a session-end evaluation correctly generates a review-pending probation event and a review-pending spillover event for two separate worked test cases, both reviewed and confirmed by an admin with a correct, visible `academic_standing_events` audit trail. A second real role (lecturer, then student/guardian) confirms the full arc end to end, per §0.2's "a second/third real role logged in" requirement.

### Problem 8.7a — Net-new: the operational gap Problem 8.7's own Definition of Done assumed away *(owner walkthrough, 2026-07-17)*
**Root cause:** Not a bug in delivered code — a real scoping gap in Problem 8.7 itself, caught on re-reading its own Definition of Done against what SESSIONS 54–62 actually built. That DoD says "one real student is admitted with a correctly formatted matriculation number" and "registered into a real mix of core and elective courses" as if admission and self-service registration were existing, working flows — they were never scoped as their own sessions. Every real student and registration used in SESSIONS 55–62's own Done Checks was created by an admin using ad hoc tools (`InstitutionTab`'s "+ Place student" action, direct SQL for matric numbers) never meant to be the school's real operational workflow. Eleven concrete gaps were identified directly by the owner, verified one by one against the real schema/code rather than taken at face value:

1. No matriculation-number generator UI — `matric_number` is a real, unique, school-scoped column (§7.27.1), assigned exactly once, by hand, during SESSION 55's own Done Check.
2. `StudentsModule.tsx` has no integration with the higher-ed model at all — it still reads/writes `class_node_id` and the secondary `active/inactive/alumni` status convention exclusively; `student_programme_placements` (§7.27.1a) is written only from `InstitutionTab`'s placement action, never surfaced in the main student record itself.
3. `AttendanceModule.tsx` (secondary, class-based) is not branched away from higher-institution schools — it renders unconditionally alongside the new `course_attendance_*` tables (§7.27.3, SESSION 58), rather than the two being mutually exclusive per school type the way `ResultsModule.tsx`'s dispatcher split (SESSION 60) already handles for scoring.
4. `TimetableModule.tsx`/`timetable_entries` are entirely `class_nodes`/`subjects`-anchored (§7.9) — no timetable of any kind exists for `course_offerings`.
5. `schools.uses_terms` is real, working infrastructure (§3.6) that already supports naming `terms` rows "First/Second Semester" — but this school's `uses_terms` is currently `false`, so no operational session/term split exists yet for it. Confirmed as a configuration gap, not a missing feature, during this same walkthrough (see §12).
6. No promotion mechanism exists for `programme_levels` at all — `promotion_rules`/`promotion_history` (§7.15) are entirely `class_node_id`-anchored, secondary-only, and were never extended (or paralleled) for level-to-level advancement.
7. No admission screen exists that both creates a `student_programme_placements` row and assigns a matriculation number in one real workflow — SESSION 55's "+ Place student" action does the former only; matric-number assignment has never been anything but a manual SQL step.
8. Students cannot register themselves for courses — confirmed at the RLS layer, not just the UI layer: `course_registrations` (§7.27.2) has a staff-manage write policy (`academics.course_registration.manage`) and a student **self-read** policy only; no student self-write policy exists. `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx` is additionally gated `isAdmin` at the `App.tsx` route level (line ~1138). Building a student-facing registration screen alone would do nothing without a new RLS write policy first.
9. No electronic payment gateway integration exists anywhere in this codebase — Paystack/Flutterwave appear only as a market-positioning note in the master plan's competitive-analysis section (§2), never built. Remita specifically has never been discussed or scoped before this walkthrough.
10. No payment receipt generation of any kind exists.
11. No mechanism gates any student-facing activity (registration, transcript/results access, etc.) on fee-payment status — no fee-status field is read by any access-control check anywhere in the codebase.

**Master plan sections to observe:** §7.27 (all five phases — this problem touches Phase 1 identity/admission, Phase 2 registration RLS, and introduces new Phase 6/7-equivalent finance scope), §3.6 (`uses_terms`, for the semester-conversion sub-session), §3.7 (new permission keys for self-service registration and fee-gating, via the existing catalog mechanism), §7.5/§7.9/§7.15 (the secondary-school tables that must be explicitly, not silently, excluded from higher-ed schools).

**Sequencing, reasoned from real dependencies, not the order the owner listed them in:** the student identity/status model (item 2) is the true root — every other item either reads a student's current placement/level (admission, self-registration, promotion, fee-gating) or needs the secondary-vs-higher-ed branch point that fixing item 2 establishes (attendance, timetable). Terms/semesters (item 5) is real infrastructure already built; converting this school's configuration is small and unblocks the timetable and self-registration sessions, which are both term-scoped. Finance (items 9–11) is its own vertical, largely independent of the academic-model fixes, but item 11 needs a real fee-status field to check, which must come from at least a minimal fees/payment concept before it can be built — sequenced last for that reason, not because it matters least.

**Goals before moving on:** see SESSIONS 64–70 below for the full per-session breakdown; each session's own Done Check must be confirmed on-device, per §0.2, before the next begins.
**Definition of Done:** every one of the 11 items above is independently confirmed on-device: a student can be admitted through one real screen that assigns a matric number; the student record shows one unambiguous current level/status; a higher-ed school sees course attendance only, a secondary school sees class attendance only; a real course-offering timetable renders for a higher-ed school; this school operates on real semester terms; a real promotion run advances a level; a student logs into their own portal and registers for a real course themselves; a real payment gateway transaction (Remita) completes and is verifiable by the school; a receipt is generated for a real payment; and at least one real activity is correctly blocked for a student with an outstanding fee balance and correctly unblocked once paid.

## 9. What this PRD deliberately does not re-decide
Per master-plan §0 and §12, the following remain locked and are not reopened by any tier above: unlimited-depth class hierarchy, fully custom roles via the permissions catalog (Tier 3 *consumes* this design, it does not change it), optional terms flag, optional ledger flag, computed-view results, consent-gated inter-school transfer, English as system default language, and the rule that school names are never translated. Any future session picking up this PRD should treat a perceived conflict with these as a signal to re-read the master plan section in question, not as license to re-litigate it.

Paste the **CONTINUITY PROMPT** from `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md §0` at the top of your new chat, then add:

At the end of every session, update `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md §11` with what was done, then mark the session row in §11 of this document (Session Log, below).

### TIER 0 — Verification discipline

#### SESSION 1 — Reconcile §10 vs §13 and adopt the verification rule
**PRD ref:** Tier 0 / Problem 0.1  
**What this is:** Documentation-only. No app code touched.  
**Why it must go first:** Every future session's "done" check depends on §13 being honest. Right now it isn't.

**Exactly what to do:**

2. For every `[x]` checkbox in §10, find the matching file in §13. If §13 says anything other than "confirmed on-device with a date," **un-tick that §10 box** back to `[ ]`.

#### SESSION 5 — Diagnose and fix the onboarding-loop-on-reload bug
**PRD ref:** Tier 2 / Problem 2.1  
**Touches:** `src/services/onboardingService.ts`, `src/routes/OnboardingWizard.tsx`, `src/App.tsx`

2. Immediately open Supabase SQL Editor and run:
   ```sql
   select onboarding_complete from schools where id = '<your school id>';
   ```
   Record whether it is `true` or `false`.
3. **If `false`:** The bug is in `completeOnboarding()` in `onboardingService.ts` — the write is not succeeding or is being swallowed silently. Add explicit error logging, find the failure, fix it, re-run the query to confirm `true`.
4. **If `true` but the wizard still reappears on reload:** The bug is in `App.tsx`'s routing read — it is probably reading a stale cached value of `onboarding_complete`. Find where the school row is fetched and cached in `App.tsx`; ensure the cache is invalidated after the wizard completes.
5. Fix only the side that the query confirms is broken. Do not speculatively fix both.
6. `npm run build` must pass clean.

**✅ Done Check:** Admin completes the wizard, then force-reloads the browser tab **three separate times**, and lands on the main app shell's home tab every time — not the wizard.

### TIER 3 — Permission enforcement

#### SESSION 8 — Audit live permissions table and build permissionsService.ts
**PRD ref:** Tier 3 / Problem 3.1 (Goals 1–2)  
**Touches:** New file: `src/services/permissionsService.ts`, `src/stores/authStore.ts`  
**Depends on:** SESSION 3 (authStore already knows the user's roles)

> **✅ Resolved 2026-07-01 (catalog keys added; conversion still pending):** Generating and regenerating guardian/student login codes is currently hidden from teachers because `StudentsModule` is gated on `isAdmin`. The live `permissions` audit (step 1) confirmed the entire `students` module was missing from the catalog — no `students.*` key existed at all. Four keys were added: `students.records.view`, `students.records.edit`, `students.records.manage`, `students.portal.manage_access`. Provisioning actions ("Set up guardian login," "Set up student login," "Regenerate code") are **planned to gate** on `students.portal.manage_access` — this conversion is SESSION 10's job and has not happened yet. **Correction, confirmed 2026-07-01 via on-device testing during Session 9:** a staff account with every permission checked in Settings → Roles still cannot see student/guardian login codes or edit student records — expected, since only `AttendanceModule.tsx` reads `can()` today (Session 9's scope). Do not read this note as Session 10 already being complete. A school will be able to grant `students.portal.manage_access` to class teachers via the roles/permissions settings screen without granting `students.records.edit`, once Session 10 is done.

> **✅ Resolved 2026-07-01 — class-scoped permissions:** confirmed **Option 1** (general mechanism) plus a per-staff independent-override layer, per owner decision — not a `students.records.edit`-only special case. Schema locked into `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md §7.3`: `role_permission_class_scope` (role-level default scope) and `staff_permission_class_scope` (per-staff override — **can grant a key the staff member's role doesn't hold**, by explicit owner decision, not merely narrow/widen an existing role grant). Resolution order: staff override first (stops there if present) → role grant + role-level scope → not granted. `class_id IS NULL` in either table means unrestricted. Full design recorded in §12.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Open Supabase SQL Editor. Run:
   ```sql
   select key, module, description from permissions order by module, key;
   ```
   Copy the full result. This is your canonical permission key list. Paste it into `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md §11` under today's session. **Do not guess any key name from memory — only use keys confirmed in this query.** **Resolved 2026-07-01:** this audit found zero `students.*` keys in the catalog — the entire module was missing. Four keys added: `students.records.view`, `students.records.edit`, `students.records.manage`, `students.portal.manage_access`. Re-run the query after adding them and confirm all four appear before proceeding to step 3.
2. Resolve the class-scoping architecture decision above into §7/§12 before writing any code in this step.
3. Create `src/services/permissionsService.ts` with the exported functions:
   ```ts
   loadEffectivePermissions(staffId: string): Promise<Set<string>>
   loadEffectivePermissionScopes(staffId: string): Promise<Map<string, Set<string> | null>>
   ```
   `loadEffectivePermissions` resolves `staff_roles → role_permissions → permissions.key` for the given staff member, unions across all roles, and returns a `Set<string>` — unchanged from the original design. `loadEffectivePermissionScopes` additionally resolves any class-scope restriction per key (per the Option 1 design above): the map's value is `null` for an unscoped/school-wide grant, or a `Set<string>` of allowed class ids for a scoped grant. A key the staff member doesn't hold at all is simply absent from the map.
4. In `authStore.ts`, call both functions at login for staff actors. Store both results. Expose two selectors: `can(key: string): boolean` (unchanged — true if the key is granted in any scope) and `canForClass(key: string, classId: string): boolean` (true if the key is granted and either unscoped or the given class is in the scoped set).
5. `npm run build` — clean.

**✅ Done Check:** `npm run build` passes. Both `can()` and `canForClass()` selectors are available. No UI change yet — that is SESSION 9. Class-scoping table design recorded in §7, decision recorded in §12.

#### SESSION 9 — Add real permission gating to Attendance module + fix `markedBy` id bug — ✅ COMPLETE
**PRD ref:** Tier 3 / Problem 3.1 (Goals 3–4, Attendance module)  
**Touches:** `src/routes/AttendanceModule.tsx`, `src/services/permissionsService.ts`, `src/store/authStore.ts`, `src/routes/SettingsModule.tsx`  
**Depends on:** SESSION 8

> **⚠️ Corrected 2026-07-01, before any code was written:** this session's original text assumed `AttendanceModule.tsx` gated its write actions on `isAdmin &&`, the same as every other module in Problem 3.1's file list. On actually reading the file, that's not what's there. Recorded here so the next session doesn't re-assume the original text:
> 1. **No gate exists to convert.** The file's only two `isAdmin &&` occurrences are cosmetic empty-state hint lines (`att-empty-sub`, in `renderClassSelector` and `renderRegister`), not gates on a write action. The real write handlers — `handleStatusTap`, `handleBatchMark` — are gated only on `disabled={isSaving || !markedBy}`, i.e. "is someone signed in," not "does this person have permission." This session adds a gate that has never existed, rather than converting one.
> 2. **`markedBy` resolves to the wrong id — likely already broken independent of permissions.** Schema: `attendance_records.marked_by uuid not null references staff(id)`. Code: `markedBy` is read from `supabase.auth.getUser().data.user.id` — the Supabase Auth user id, not `staff.id`. Those are separate rows, joined only via `app_identities.auth_user_id → actor_record_id`. Unless the two ids happen to coincide (they don't, by the schema's own design — that's the entire reason `app_identities` exists), every `saveAttendanceRecord`/`saveBatchAttendance` call hits a foreign-key violation today, regardless of who's signed in. `authStore.ts` already resolves the correct id as `actorRecordId` for staff actors (added Session 8's predecessor work) — same store `can()` lives in — so this session reads `actorRecordId` instead of calling `supabase.auth.getUser()` directly, removing that ad hoc resolution entirely.
> 3. **Locked decisions (owner, 2026-07-01), since the catalog has three separate `academics.attendance.*` keys but the code has no matching distinction:**
>    - **One key, not two:** every status tap (`handleStatusTap`) and batch mark (`handleBatchMark`) gates on `academics.attendance.mark`, whether the tap is a first-time mark or a correction of an already-saved status. `academics.attendance.edit` is not used this session — the code has no concept of "already marked vs not" to hang a second key on without new detection logic, and building that wasn't asked for.
>    - **Class-list filtering deferred:** `loadAllLeafClasses()` still returns every class to every staff member, unfiltered, this session. `academics.attendance.view_all` and the `canForClass`/class-scoping mechanism built in Session 8 are **not** wired into the class selector here — logged as a known gap (see §11), not silently skipped. A future session should filter the class selector via `canForClass('academics.attendance.mark', classId)` (or a dedicated `view_all` check) once prioritized.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. In `AttendanceModule.tsx`, import `useAuthStore` and read `actorRecordId` and `can`. Replace the `supabase.auth.getUser()`-derived `markedBy` state/effect with `actorRecordId` directly — drop the ad hoc effect and the `../lib/supabase` dynamic import it used.
2. Gate `handleStatusTap` and `handleBatchMark` (and the buttons that trigger them) on `can('academics.attendance.mark')`, in addition to the existing `!markedBy` check (now `!actorRecordId`) — both must hold for a write to be attempted.
3. When the permission check fails, surface a plain-English message (per §4.3 — not a raw RLS error) rather than silently disabling with no explanation, consistent with §4.12's "fail gracefully with a clear message" requirement.
4. Test the §4.12 scenario live: as admin, go to Settings → Roles → revoke `academics.attendance.mark` from the test teacher's role. On the teacher's device/tab, attempt to mark attendance. Confirm it fails with a user-friendly message. The teacher should not need to reload for this to take effect — if a reload is required, document that limitation explicitly in §11.
5. Separately, confirm the `markedBy`→`actorRecordId` fix actually resolves the foreign-key issue: mark attendance as a staff member who **does** hold `academics.attendance.mark`, confirm the row is written (not just optimistically shown), and confirm `marked_by` in the database matches that staff member's `staff.id`.
6. `npm run build` — clean.

**✅ Done Check:** Step 4's revoke scenario works end-to-end, on-device, with two browser tabs open simultaneously (admin + teacher). Step 5's write confirmed landing in the database with the correct `marked_by`. Document both results in §11, including the class-list-filtering gap as still open.

> **✅ Confirmed 2026-07-01, on-device:** both parts of the Done Check passed. Revoke test passed bidirectionally (admin revokes → teacher reload → blocked with visible message; admin grants → teacher can mark, correct `marked_by`). The "should not need to reload" language above did **not** hold as originally hoped — a reload is required, exactly as anticipated by the Option A decision in §12 — this is accepted, documented, working behavior, not an open defect.
>
> **Five further items surfaced from this same on-device pass** (see §11 for full detail) — resolution status as of 2026-07-01, close of Session 9:
> 1. Non-admin staff can't act on Students/Guardians/Timetable/etc. even with every permission checked — expected; this is exactly what SESSION 10 below fixes. **→ Carried into SESSION 10, next.**
> 2. `SettingsModule.tsx` RolesTab shows "0 permissions" for admin roles — **✅ Fixed 2026-07-01.** Now shows "Full admin access — bypasses individual permissions" when `r.is_admin_role`. Confirmed `npm run build` clean and on-device by owner.
> 3. `SettingsModule.tsx` RolesTab shows raw permission keys instead of the catalog's existing plain-English descriptions — **✅ Fixed 2026-07-01.** Raw key (`p.key`) removed from display entirely rather than merely reordered — an initial reorder-only attempt left the raw key rendering below the description but visually heavier (stylesheet not in hand, so the safer fix was removing it rather than guessing at a CSS override). `p.key` remains in use functionally (React list key, checkbox state, toggle handler) — only the visible `<span>` was removed. Confirmed `npm run build` clean and on-device by owner.
> 4. **New required scope:** no UI exists to assign/change which role a staff member holds. Locked to live in `HRModule.tsx`'s existing staff edit form. **→ Scheduled as SESSION 10a, after SESSION 10.** Not yet built.
> 5. **New required scope, larger:** admin needs to view/print/regenerate a staff member's default password until claimed. Requires a schema change (`staff.default_password`, `staff.default_password_set_at`), an edge function change (`create-staff-account/index.ts` + new regenerate logic), new HR UI, **and** a net-new staff self-service password-change screen (does not exist at all today — required for the auto-clear mechanism to ever fire). **→ Scheduled as SESSION 10b, after SESSION 10a.** Not yet built.
>
> **Admin-bypass prerequisite — ✅ Implemented and confirmed 2026-07-01.** `can()`/`canForClass()` in `permissionsService.ts` gained an optional `isAdminRole` parameter that short-circuits to granted; `authStore.ts`'s `can`/`canForClass` selectors now pass the store's own resolved `isAdminRole` through automatically on every call, so every module (present and future) gets the bypass with zero per-call-site changes. `AttendanceModule.tsx` required no edits to inherit this. Confirmed `npm run build` clean and on-device by owner. **This was SESSION 10's blocking prerequisite — it is now cleared. SESSION 10 has no remaining blockers.**
>
> **Known edge case, flagged not fixed (pre-existing, not introduced by the admin-bypass work):** `platform_admin` actors get `isAdminRole: false` and an empty `permissionScopes` map in `resolveIdentity` (platform_admin is a separate concept from school-scoped admin roles, assumed to never reach a screen that calls `can()`). If a future shared module a platform_admin can reach ever calls `can()`, it will currently return `false` for everything. Not a Session 9/10 concern; noted so it isn't rediscovered cold later.

**SESSION 9 is fully closed as of 2026-07-01.** All Done Check items, all five on-device findings, and the admin-bypass prerequisite are either resolved or explicitly scheduled into a named future session (10, 10a, 10b) — nothing from this session is left ambiguous or silently dropped.

**Status: ✅ Complete, 2026-07-02.** See §10 summary table row 10a-3 for what was actually built and confirmed.

**Depends on:** SESSION 10a-2 (pay frequency must exist to compute against)

> **Why this exists, and why it's scoped down from the original ask:** owner's request could have meant either (A) a read-only "who's due" list/filter using existing data, or (B) the system automatically generating payroll runs/payslips on a schedule. (B) would require new scheduled-job infrastructure — confirmed via project-wide grep that none exists today (every Edge Function is user-click-triggered, never timer-triggered) — plus real product decisions (mid-cycle salary changes, already-partially-paid payslips, staff leaving mid-month) that shouldn't be silently assumed. Owner explicitly agreed to scope this session to (A) only, treating (B) as a distinct future ask requiring its own greenlight.

**Exactly what to do (for reference — already done, see status above):**
1. `hrService.ts`: `loadStaffDueForPayment(schoolId, targetMonth)` — for each active staff member with a salary structure, compute whether they appear due in the target month: `monthly` → due if no payslip yet covers that month; `termly`/`sessional` → due if the current term's/session's real `end_date` (confirmed against schema, not assumed) falls inside the target month, and not already paid past that date; `custom` → flagged `custom_unknown` explicitly, never silently guessed at or omitted.
2. `HRModule.tsx`: collapsible panel in the Payroll tab, above "+ New payroll run", with a month picker (default: current month). Loads only when expanded — never on module boot, matching the app's existing lazy-load discipline. UI frames this explicitly as an estimate to help decide who to include in a payroll run, not a record of what's actually owed.
3. `npm run build` — clean.

#### SESSION 10b — Default password view/print/regenerate + staff self-service password change (flagged 2026-07-01)
**Status: ✅ Complete, 2026-07-02.** See §10 summary table row 10b for what was actually built and confirmed.
**PRD ref:** Tier 3 follow-on, surfaced during Session 9 on-device testing (§11, §12)  
**Touches:** New migration (`staff.default_password`, `staff.default_password_set_at`), `supabase/functions/create-staff-account/index.ts`, new edge function or extension for regenerate, `src/routes/HRModule.tsx` (view/print/regenerate UI), new staff-facing password-change screen (location not yet decided — likely a Settings/profile area for staff, doesn't exist today)  
**Depends on:** none structurally, but logically follows SESSION 10a since both touch HR's staff detail view

> **Why this exists, and why it's bigger than it looks:** owner wants the auto-generated temp password viewable/printable by admin until claimed, and regeneratable if a staff member loses it. Checked `create-staff-account/index.ts` directly — today the plaintext password exists only in that function's memory and the one-time response body; nothing persists it, which is why it's unrecoverable today. There is also, confirmed 2026-07-01, no staff self-service password-change screen anywhere in the app — a genuine, previously unlogged gap, not just a missing "nice to have." Without it, the auto-clear-on-change trigger below has nothing to trigger on.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Migration: add `staff.default_password` (nullable text) and `staff.default_password_set_at` (nullable timestamptz). RLS: readable only by admin roles for that school, per §3.2's existing pattern.
2. `create-staff-account/index.ts`: write both columns alongside the existing `auth.admin.createUser()` call — the plaintext value already exists in that function's scope for that call, so this adds no new place the plaintext transits.
3. New "regenerate" capability (new edge function, or a branch of an existing one — decide during implementation): generate a new temp password via the same `generateTempPassword()` helper, call `adminClient.auth.admin.updateUserById()` to update the real credential, and overwrite both new columns.
4. Auto-clear logic — **both** conditions must independently be able to clear it (owner's locked decision, "both together," not either alone): (a) a scheduled job or check-on-read that nulls the columns once `auth.users.last_sign_in_at` is populated and postdates `default_password_set_at`, and (b) a 7-day expiry from `default_password_set_at`, checked the same way. Confirm which mechanism (cron/scheduled function vs. check-on-read) fits this project's existing infra before building — not yet decided.
5. HR UI: on a staff member's detail view, if `default_password` is non-null, show it with a print affordance; if null, show "claimed" or "expired" with a "Regenerate" button.
6. **Net-new:** build a staff-facing password-change screen (self-service). Confirm with owner where this should live in the app's navigation before building — not decided yet. On successful change, this is what should trigger clearing `default_password` immediately (in addition to the two automatic conditions above).
7. `npm run build` — clean.

**✅ Done Check:** Admin creates a new staff member, sees the default password in HR, prints/copies it. Regenerates it, confirms the old one no longer works and the new one does. Staff member logs in with the temp password, uses the new self-service screen to set a real password, confirms `default_password` clears immediately in the DB. Separately, confirm the 7-day and login-based auto-clears both work (may require a manual DB timestamp edit to test the 7-day path without waiting). §13 updated: `create-staff-account/index.ts`, `HRModule.tsx`, and the new password-change screen file.

> **✅ Confirmed 2026-07-02, on-device — closes this session, with two locked decisions and one honestly-flagged gap recorded below, not silently resolved:**
> - **Cron vs. check-on-read (step 4), decided:** check-on-read. No scheduled-job infrastructure exists anywhere in this app (confirmed by grep before deciding) — building one just for a 7-day expiry check would be new, first-of-its-kind infra for a problem that doesn't need it. Expiry is computed fresh on every read (`loadStaffDefaultPassword()`), not stored or cron-cleared. The "both conditions independently clear it" requirement in step 4 is **partially met, not fully**, and this is the one open item from this session, not swept under the rug: the 7-day expiry path is real and working (client-computed, confirmed on-device); the login-based auto-clear is not — `auth.users.last_sign_in_at` is only reachable via the Auth Admin API (service-role, server-side), and `app_identities` (§7.2) has no mirrored column for it. Rather than invent one, `claimed` is honestly typed and documented as always `false` today. Closing this now on the owner's on-device confirmation of everything else working, with this specific gap named so a future session can pick it up deliberately (a small Edge Function or a login-trigger mirror column) rather than rediscover it cold.
> - **Self-service screen location (step 6), decided:** the account popover, as a new "Account preferences" item between Language and Audit log — not a Settings/profile area, which doesn't exist for non-admin staff/guardians/students anyway. Built as `AccountPreferencesScreen.tsx`, deliberately scoped wider than just password change on the owner's direction: it's the intended future home for theme, language, font size, and alarm settings too, each as its own section on the same screen using a shared placeholder shell — avoids a rename/re-route when those land later.
> - **Everything else in the Done Check confirmed working on-device** by the owner, `npm run build` clean, no further findings reported this pass.

**SESSION 10b is closed as of 2026-07-02**, with the login-based auto-clear signal named as a specific, scoped-out follow-up (not a new session number yet — pick up if/when it matters) rather than left ambiguous.

#### SESSION 11 — StudentsModule: Guardians-tab UI rework
**Status: ✅ Complete, 2026-07-02 — `npm run build` and on-device confirmation both observed. See final confirmation note at the end of this session's log.**
