2. Add a confirmation step (a simple "Are you sure?" dialog naming the guardian) before both the Delete action and the Regenerate-code action. These are currently one tap away with no confirmation — too easy to trigger by accident.
3. Add a visible link/button from the per-student guardian card view back to the main Guardians module/page (`StudentsModule.tsx`'s top-level Guardians tab), so an admin isn't stuck in the per-student view with no way back except browser/app navigation.
4. `npm run build` — clean.

**✅ Done Check:** On-device, confirm: one consolidated card per guardian (name + code + details together); Delete and Regenerate both require an explicit confirm step; a visible path exists from the per-student card view to the main Guardians page. Screenshot before/after. Update §13: `StudentsModule.tsx` — "Guardians-tab UI rework: confirmed on-device."

> **✅ Delivered 2026-07-02 — file read in full before any edit (per §0.2), and this item's own premise was checked against the real file rather than assumed correct, since it was written back on 2026-06-29:**
> - **Item 1 (consolidate into one card), found already done, not touched:** the real file already renders one `<li className="stu-guardian-row">` per guardian containing name, relationship, phone, email, the access toggle, unlink, and the whole provisioning/login-code section together — not split across multiple cards as this item describes. No code change made for item 1; the PRD's premise here didn't match current code, most likely because this item predates a guardian-card consolidation that happened in an earlier, unrecorded session. Recorded here rather than silently reported as "done" without saying why no diff exists for it.
> - **Item 2 (confirm step before Delete and Regenerate), found half-done:** Delete (unlink) already had a working confirm banner (`unlinkTarget` state). Regenerate did not — "Change / regenerate" jumped straight into the provision form, and the form's own "Regenerate code" button fired the real API call with no separate confirm. **Fixed:** added `regenerateConfirmTarget` state, mirroring `unlinkTarget`'s own pattern exactly (same `stu-confirm-banner`/`stu-confirm-cancel`/`stu-confirm-danger` classes, already defined and used elsewhere in the file — no new confirm-banner CSS needed). "Change / regenerate" now opens this confirmation first; only on explicit "Continue" does it proceed into the provision form, which still requires its own second tap on "Regenerate code" — same two-tap friction level as unlink. First-time "Set up login" (no existing code to invalidate) is left as a direct action, unchanged — it isn't destructive, so gating it behind a confirm would be friction without a real risk to protect against.
> - **Item 3 (visible path back to the main Guardians page), found half-done:** an `onNavigate` prop already existed and was wired on each guardian's name — but only to open that *specific* guardian's own detail view in the Guardians module (`onNavigate('guardians', { guardianId })`), not a generic path back to the top-level Guardians list. **Fixed:** added a "← Back to Guardians" button at the top of the tab, calling `onNavigate('guardians')` with no intent — distinct from the per-guardian name links, gated on `onNavigate` being provided at all (same optional-prop convention the name links already use).
> - **One new CSS class introduced, not yet styled — flagged, not silently shipped:** `stu-guardians-back-link` (the new back-button) has no rule in `index.css` yet. `index.css` was not uploaded to this session, so it could not be added/verified here the way prior sessions' CSS gap-closures were done (grep-diff against the real file). Needs the same treatment next time `index.css` is in hand: add a rule and confirm no prefix collision, same discipline as every other `stu-*` CSS pass.
> - **Not yet confirmed:** `npm run build` and on-device testing are both outstanding — this delivery is code-complete and syntax-checked (esbuild parse pass, clean), but neither compiled against the real project nor tested on a device yet.
>
> **Follow-up, 2026-07-02, same day — CSS gap closed:** `index.css` supplied this pass. Confirmed by grep the class truly had zero rules before this edit. Added `.stu-guardians-back-link` immediately above `.stu-guardian-list`, styled to match the existing `.stu-guardian-name--link` convention exactly (reset native button chrome, `var(--color-primary)`, underline-on-hover/focus via `text-decoration-color` transition) rather than inventing a new visual style — the file has no other back-link precedent anywhere, so this establishes the pattern rather than following one. Kept as a separate block instead of merging into `.stu-guardian-name--link` because it's a full-width top-of-tab element with its own `margin-bottom`, not an inline name inside a row. Grep-confirmed no prefix collision with any other `stu-guardians-*` or `stu-guardian-*` selector.
>
> **Second follow-up, 2026-07-02, later same day — two real bugs reported by the owner after reviewing the delivered code, both confirmed and fixed, not styling issues:**
> - **Bug A — the back-link didn't actually go "back."** The original wiring called `onNavigate('guardians')`, which exits the student's detail sheet entirely and jumps to the separate top-level Guardians module — not a return to wherever the admin came from. Root cause: `detailTab` (this sheet's own local subtab state — `'brief' | 'info' | 'guardians' | 'status'`) and `onNavigate` (app-level module routing) are two unrelated mechanisms that got conflated when this button was written. There is no navigation-history stack anywhere in this app (confirmed by grep across `App.tsx`'s full revision history in §13) so "return to where you came from" means the one real, consistent entry point: `openDetail()` always resets `detailTab` to `'brief'`, and the tab switcher is a flat sibling switch with no other path into `'guardians'` — so Brief is where the admin came from, every time, not a guess. **Fixed:** button now calls `setDetailTab('brief')`, staying inside the same student's detail sheet; relabeled from "← Back to Guardians" to "← Back" since it no longer goes to the top-level Guardians module (that's still reachable via each guardian's own name link, unchanged, a few lines below).
> - **Bug B — the "No login set up yet" card persisted for guardians who already had a login code, making Regenerate reachable with no confirm step.** Root cause: `identities[link.guardian_id]` was only ever populated on-demand (granting access, or opening the provision form) — so on a fresh detail-sheet open, every already-provisioned guardian's `identities` entry sat at `undefined`. The card's "not yet provisioned" branch checked `identity === null || identity === undefined`, treating "never fetched" the same as "genuinely no login" — collapsing two different states into one UI path. Practical effect: an already-provisioned guardian showed the unprovisioned card, and tapping "Set up login →" routed into the *same* create/regenerate call the real "Change / regenerate" button uses, but without that button's confirm gate (`regenerateConfirmTarget` was only ever set from the *other* branch). **Fixed:** `fetchGuardians()` now eagerly calls `fetchGuardianIdentity()` for every guardian who already has `has_platform_access: true`, immediately after the guardian list loads — so `identities` resolves to its real value (object or `null`) before the card renders, no click required to discover which state a guardian is actually in. This closes the accidental-regenerate path structurally (the correct card renders from the start) rather than by adding a second confirm gate on top of a still-wrong card.
> - Both fixes are scoped to `StudentsModule.tsx` only; no service-layer or schema changes needed — `loadGuardianIdentity(schoolId, guardianId)` already existed and is simply called earlier/more eagerly now.
> - **Still outstanding, unchanged: `npm run build` against the real project, and on-device confirmation** — now covering the original three Done Check items plus these two fixes (back-link returns to Brief tab within the same student; already-provisioned guardians never show the unprovisioned card or reach Regenerate without the explicit confirm banner). Session 11 stays ⚠️ Partial until those are actually observed, per §0.2.
>
> **Third follow-up, 2026-07-02, later same day — owner rejected the Bug A fix above as still wrong, and flagged a related regression; both resolved by a scope change to the underlying interaction, not another patch on the same button:**
> - **What was actually wrong with the Bug A fix:** the owner clarified the report was never about the in-sheet "← Back" button (`setDetailTab('brief')`) — it was about a *different* click path: tapping a guardian's **name** inside the Guardians subtab, which sends `onNavigate('guardians', { guardianId })` and hands off to the separate top-level `GuardianModule.tsx`, replacing the Students screen entirely. That module's own detail view has no route back to the specific student/subtab the admin came from — and fixing that properly would mean building this app's first navigation-history mechanism (confirmed: none exists anywhere in the codebase) just to serve one link.
> - **Decision, made explicitly before building (owner asked for a recommendation first, per their own instruction):** rather than build cross-module back-navigation, tapping a guardian's name now opens an in-place **guardian brief popup** inside `StudentsModule.tsx` itself — name, relationship, phone, email, and the full list of that guardian's other linked students (via a new `loadGuardian()`-style read from `guardianService.ts`, which already returned everything needed in one call). Closing the popup requires no "back" at all, since the admin never actually left the Students screen. A deliberate, one-way **"Edit in Guardians module →"** link remains for when real editing is needed, using the same `onNavigate('guardians', { guardianId })` call as before — now an honest, chosen jump instead of an accidental default.
> - **New state:** `briefGuardianId` / `briefGuardianData` (plus a `briefGuardianState` loading flag), holding the fetched `GuardianDetail`. New render function `renderGuardianBrief()`, called from the tab content, gated on `briefGuardianId` being set.
> - **Second owner-reported bug folded into the same pass:** the inline login-code provisioning card (status row / "no login yet" / regenerate form) previously rendered directly inside each `<li className="stu-guardian-row">` in the list — causing the row to visibly grow/shrink across its three states (no shared height). **Fixed by relocating, not just resizing:** the whole provisioning card now renders only inside `renderGuardianBrief()`, wrapped in a new `.stu-provision-section--brief` class with a `min-height` sized to its tallest state, so the popup holds a fixed footprint across all three states. The guardian list row itself no longer renders any login-code UI at all — access on/off remains a list-row toggle (per-link `has_platform_access`, unchanged), but the code itself is only visible/actionable from inside the brief. The `regenerateConfirmTarget` confirm banner moved with it (it previously rendered in the list, which now sits behind the popup's z-index and would have been invisible if triggered from inside).
> - **Consequence for the back-link fixed in the prior follow-up:** with the Guardians subtab now reached the same way as Brief/Info/Status — via the detail sheet's own tab switcher, a few lines above — the dedicated in-tab "← Back" button (`setDetailTab('brief')`, `.stu-guardians-back-link`) became redundant navigation on the same screen, per explicit owner request ("remove the back button on that guardians subtab"). **Removed entirely** from `StudentsModule.tsx`, and the now-orphaned `.stu-guardians-back-link` CSS rule removed from `index.css` in the same pass — both with a history comment left in place rather than a silent deletion, so a future session can see why the class briefly existed and why it's gone.
> - **Cleanup verified:** `onNavigate` prop remains genuinely used (the brief popup's "Edit in Guardians module →" link, and the guardian-name click that opens the brief) — no unused-prop/import risk from the removal. `setDetailTab` remains genuinely used elsewhere (the tab switcher itself, and `openDetail()`'s reset-to-brief). `identity`/`isProvisioning` locals that were only read by the old in-row provisioning card were confirmed dead after the move and removed, along with the stale comment referencing them, to keep `noUnusedLocals` clean. Brace/paren/bracket counts balanced in both files as a structural sanity check (esbuild itself unavailable in this sandbox — no network egress to the npm registry — so this is a syntax proxy, not a substitute for the real build).
> - **New file dependency, now on record:** this pass required `studentsService.ts` and `guardianService.ts` to be supplied directly (neither had been uploaded before) rather than inferring their shape from the master plan's prose — both now referenced by name in §13 conventions (guardian↔student relationship reads live in `studentsService.ts`; the guardian's own record lives in `guardianService.ts`).
> - **Still outstanding, expanded scope, unchanged discipline:** `npm run build` against the real project (still not run — no network/toolchain access in this environment), and full on-device confirmation covering: consolidated guardian card (unchanged, pre-existing), Delete/Regenerate confirm steps (unchanged from second follow-up), guardian-name tap opens the brief popup with no resize jump and no login-code UI left in the list row, "Edit in Guardians module →" reaches the real module, and the Guardians subtab has no leftover back button. Session 11 stays ⚠️ Partial until these are actually observed on-device, per §0.2 — this follow-up is a scope correction and a real fix, not a completion claim.
>
> **✅ Final confirmation, 2026-07-02 — Done Check observed, session closed:** owner confirmed `npm run build` ran cleanly against the real project ("Npm(for all) ran cleanly") and on-device testing passed ("phone testing (for all) is working perfectly"), covering the full expanded scope from all four passes above: consolidated guardian card, Delete/Regenerate confirm steps, guardian-name tap opening the brief popup with no resize jump and no login-code UI left in the list row, "Edit in Guardians module →" reaching the real module, and no leftover back button on the Guardians subtab. #### SESSION 12 — StudentsModule: navigation/modal redesign + dual code-generator investigation + class filter
**Status: ✅ Complete, 2026-07-02 — `npm run build` and on-device confirmation both observed. See final confirmation note at the end of this session's log.**
**PRD ref:** SESSION 4 defect list, items 5–8 (flagged 2026-06-29)  
**Touches:** `src/routes/StudentsModule.tsx` (subtab modal, Info/Brief subtabs, class filter, and whatever service the Info-tab code generator calls)  
**Depends on:** SESSION 11 (Guardians-tab rework done first, so this session isn't competing for the same file mid-edit)

> **⚑ Priority within this session:** Do step 2 (the dual code-generator investigation) first, before any cosmetic work. It is a correctness/safety concern, not styling — an admin could currently generate the wrong kind of code believing it sets up student login.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. **Investigate the dual code-generator bug.** There are currently two separate, unrelated code-generation buttons on the same student: the Brief subtab's "Set up login" (confirmed correctly wired to `provision-student-access` in SESSION 4's provisioning test) and a second "generate" button inside the Info subtab producing a different kind of code. Search `StudentsModule.tsx` and trace what service/table the Info-tab button writes to and what (if anything) reads that value elsewhere in the codebase. Do not delete it without confirming nothing depends on it. Once traced, either: (a) remove it if it's confirmed dead code, or (b) clearly relabel and visually separate it from the login-code flow if it serves a real, distinct purpose. Record the finding in §11 either way — this is exactly the kind of "claimed vs. actual" discrepancy this PRD exists to catch.
2. Redesign the subtab modal/panel to be a fixed-size, centered modal rather than one that slides up from the bottom and resizes on every subtab switch. Content should scroll inside the modal if it overflows, not resize the modal itself.
3. Remove the Info subtab as a separate destination. Instead, surface its fields via an explicit "Edit" action from the Brief subtab. **Resolved 2026-06-30, key finalized 2026-07-01:** gate this behind the `students.records.edit` permission key (added to the catalog in Session 8) using the same role-permission system as every other Tier 3 module — the admin controls which roles have it via the existing roles/permissions settings screen, exactly like `academics.attendance.mark` or any other key. This is not a special case. No separate admin-only hardcoding — if a school wants a senior teacher or registrar to edit student records, the admin grants that role the key (or grants it to that one staff member directly via `staff_permission_class_scope`, per §12's class-scoping decision), same as any other permission.
4. Replace the class filter dropdown at the top of the main student list with a row of small tappable buttons/pills, one per class, so navigating to any class is immediately visible without opening a dropdown.
5. `npm run build` — clean.

**✅ Done Check:** Dual-code-generator investigation result recorded in §11 (dead code removed, or relabeled — either way, documented). Modal is fixed-size and centered across all subtabs, confirmed on-device. Info fields reachable only via "Edit" from Brief, gated on a real permission key (not `isAdmin`). Class filter shows as tappable pills. Update §13: `StudentsModule.tsx` — "navigation/modal/code-generator: resolved."

#### SESSION 16 — Diagnose and fix the attendance save/unmark bug
**PRD ref:** Tier 5 / Problem 5.1  
**Touches:** `src/services/attendanceService.ts`, `src/routes/AttendanceModule.tsx`  
**Depends on:** SESSION 14 (teacher dashboard exists so we're testing inside the right role screen)

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Log in as the test teacher. Open Attendance. Mark one student present.
2. Immediately open the browser's network tab / console. Look at the Supabase response to the upsert call:
   - Did the write return 200/201 with a row back? → Write succeeded.
   - Did the write fail? → Fix the write path.
3. After a successful write, watch the read-back: does the student's status flip back to unmarked? If yes, the bug is in `loadAttendanceRecords()` — specifically the `students!inner(class_node_id)` PostgREST join. Test this join directly in SQL Editor:
   ```sql
   select * from attendance_records ar
   join students s on s.id = ar.student_id
   where s.class_node_id = '<class id>'
   and ar.date = today;
   ```
   If this returns no rows despite the write succeeding, switch to the two-step fallback documented in §13: first fetch student IDs for the class, then `.in('student_id', ids)`.
4. Fix only the confirmed broken side. Confirm fix works for both single-mark and batch `saveBatchAttendance()`.
5. `npm run build` — clean.

**✅ Done Check:** Mark a full class register (mix of Present/Absent/Late/Excused + one batch mark-all). Force-reload. Every mark is still exactly as set. Update §13: `attendanceService.ts` + `AttendanceModule.tsx` — "Tested as: teacher; save bug confirmed fixed."

**⚠️ Scope substitution, logged 2026-07-09, before this session's code was written — read before following step 1 below:** `index__17_.html` (the reference app) is no longer available to this project; the owner confirmed it cannot be supplied. Step 1's literal instruction ("port `index__17_.html`'s `ICONS` object") is therefore not executable as written. Per the owner's explicit direction, this was researched rather than defaulted — full reasoning and the license/React-Native-compatibility findings are logged in master-plan §12 ("Icon source for Session 25... decided 2026-07-09"). **Resolution: [Lucide](https://lucide.dev) (ISC license) is the substitute source, sourced as copied raw SVG markup into this project's own icon module — not installed as an npm package** (`lucide-react` is web-DOM-only and has no React Native equivalent import path; copying raw SVG avoids that split entirely, per §5). Every other step below (2–7) proceeds exactly as written, substituting "Lucide's SVG set" everywhere the instructions say "the reference app's `ICONS` object" or "reference set." This is a real, named deviation from the PRD's original source — not a claim that Lucide is a verified visual match to the reference app's own hand-authored icons, since that app is no longer available to compare against.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Port `index__17_.html`'s `ICONS` object (~45 hand-authored inline SVGs) as the starting inventory — this is a floor, not a ceiling, on what the app's icon module contains.
2. Build a single reusable icon component/lookup map, preserving the `viewBox="0 0 24 24"` / `stroke="currentColor"` / `stroke-width="2"` conventions so every icon inherits size and color from CSS context automatically. Design it to be extended, since step 4 below will add to it.
3. Grep the **entire** codebase, fresh, for every emoji used as an icon — nav items, buttons, cards, status badges, empty states, anywhere — not a re-check of the three previously-flagged instances. List every file and location before changing anything.
4. For each emoji found: if the reference set has a matching concept, use it. If it doesn't (this app has screens/concepts the single-school reference app never needed), author a new icon in the same visual style and add it to the icon module — do not leave the emoji in place and do not force a mismatched substitute.
5. Replace every emoji with its icon equivalent. Case-by-case, confirm which icons should inherit brand color vs. keep a fixed semantic color (e.g. a red "conflict" warning icon stays red, per the reference app's own `.header-conflict-badge` pattern — do not blanket-apply brand color to every icon).
6. Add `aria-label` to any interactive element whose only signal is now an icon (per §4.10), matching the reference app's `aria-label` pattern on its menu button.
7. `npm run build` — clean.

**✅ Done Check:** Zero emoji used as navigation, action, status, or empty-state icons anywhere in the app, confirmed by a fresh full-codebase grep (not a spot-check of previously-known instances). All icons are monochrome, SVG-based, sized via CSS, and inherit brand-token color case-by-case matching the reference app's own choices. Any icon with no reference-app equivalent has been newly authored in the same style. Confirmed on-device. Update §13.

**PRD ref:** Tier 6 / Problem 6.6

**Depends on:** SESSION 26 (alarm fires against real period boundaries the widget already resolves)

**Exactly what to do:**

Build `alarmService.ts`: port the reference app's tone-synthesis functions (`playTone` and the four tone families — chime/bell/buzzer/digital — each with an "end" and "warn" variant) as pure Web Audio API functions, behind a lazily-created `AudioContext` created/resumed on a genuine first user gesture, not on mount. #### SESSION 29 — Build the term/session progress card with holiday-aware auto-extend and auto-revert
**PRD ref:** Tier 6 / Problem 6.7
**Touches:** `src/services/` (new term-progress computation function), new component `src/components/TermProgressCard.tsx`, dashboard surfaces (`AdminDashboard.tsx`, `TeacherDashboard.tsx`, guardian/student portal), `src/services/settingsService.ts` (auto-extend/auto-revert logic)
**Depends on:** SESSION 17–19 (Tier 5.2's `school_calendar_days` table must exist first — this problem has no calendar-holiday concept to build on otherwise)

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Confirm the exact `sessions`/`terms` schema and `uses_terms` behavior with a direct read of the live schema before writing any query — do not assume the reference app's `trimesters` table name or shape carries over.
2. Resolve the extend/shrink symmetry decision before writing code, and record it in §7/§12: compute the term's *effective* end date live as `end_date_base + count of school-day holidays in range`, rather than storing a mutating end date — this makes insert and delete symmetric for free, unlike the reference app's `attAutoExtendTrimester()`, which only ever extends and never reverses. The owner's ask ("auto changes with holiday inserted/**removed**") requires the reversible version.
3. Build the computation function (elapsed/total percentage, elapsed/remaining/total school-week counts from `school_week_days`, list of in-range school-day holidays with calendar-position percentage for notch placement) as pure data computation, separate from rendering — unlike the reference app's `buildTrimCardHtml()`, which mixes the two.
4. Build `TermProgressCard.tsx`: term label, date range, progress bar with today-marker pin and holiday notches, elapsed/remaining/total counters, and a break-impact summary line (`.trim-break-row`-equivalent) when holidays exist in range.
5. Confirm placement with the owner and render `<TermProgressCard />` on each relevant dashboard.
6. Handle `uses_terms = false` sensibly (session-wide progress against `sessions` dates, or hide the card — confirm which) rather than erroring.
7. Test both directions explicitly on-device: insert a school-day holiday inside an active term — confirm the card's notch, remaining-week count, and effective end date update without a page reload; then delete that same holiday — confirm the card fully reverts.
8. `npm run build` — clean.

**✅ Done Check:** Admin inserts a school-day holiday inside the current term's date range — the term-progress card (on at least admin and teacher dashboards) visibly shows a new notch, an updated remaining-week count, and an extended effective end date, without a page reload. Admin then deletes that same holiday — the card reverts to its pre-holiday state, confirmed on-device. A school with `uses_terms = false` shows the confirmed sensible fallback rather than erroring. Update §13.

#### SESSION 54 — Lock Phase 0 decisions; get Phase 1–5 schema reviewed/accepted into master-plan §7.27 (Problem 8.7)
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7
**Touches:** `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md §7.27` (new), `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md §12` (decision record), `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md §3.16` (amend — `school_type` becomes load-bearing, no longer inert)
**Depends on:** none technically — this is the design/sign-off session Problem 8.7's whole build depends on

**Phase 0 decision status (owner conversation, 2026-07-13) — recorded here so nothing is silently assumed resolved:**
- **Decision 1 (institution-category branch)** — ✅ Locked. Real enrolling school selects **"Other"** at onboarding (see below) — no smart defaults pre-filled for it, exactly as the original design allowed for schools that don't cleanly fit University/Polytechnic/College of Education.
- **Decision 2 (GPA rollup method + retake treatment)** — ✅ Locked, unblocked. `cgpa_policy.retake_treatment` (`replacement`/`dilution`) and `cgpa_policy.rollup_method` (`weighted_all_attempts`/`average_of_semester_gpas`) both confirmed as school-level config columns exactly as designed — no change to the mechanism. Since the real school's category resolved to "Other," the category-conditional-default question is now moot for this school: no default is pre-filled, and the school configures `grade_scales`/`cgpa_policy` manually during onboarding per Decision 5 — or leaves GPA/CGPA unconfigured entirely, pending the NMCN follow-up below. University/Polytechnic/College-of-Education default leanings from the prior session (weighted_all_attempts / average_of_semester_gpas / undiscussed, respectively) remain unconfirmed placeholders for **future** schools of those categories, not blocking this school.
- **Decision 3 (programme levels as administrative metadata)** — ✅ Locked as originally designed, no change.
- **Decision 4 (no separate carryover-roster screen)** — ✅ Locked as originally designed, no change.
- **Decision 5 (soft per-category gating, never hard-locked)** — ✅ Locked as originally designed, no change. This decision is what makes "Other" a safe, fully-functional choice rather than a dead end.
- **Institution category for the real enrolling school itself** — ✅ Resolved: **a school of nursing/midwifery**, regulated by the **Nursing and Midwifery Council of Nigeria (NMCN)** — confirmed via research to be a real, distinct fourth Nigerian regulatory regime alongside NUC/NBTE/NCCE, not a variant of any of the three. Selected as "Other" for now per owner's direction (below), not built as a proper fifth category yet.

**⚠️ New open item surfaced this session — NMCN institution gap, must be resolved before Phase 4 (SESSION 59–60):** Research into NMCN-regulated schools surfaced a structural question the original four-category design didn't anticipate: NMCN's Basic Nursing programme is a fixed, national, three-year/six-semester curriculum common to every accredited school, culminating not in an internally-computed CGPA/degree-classification but in an **external, twice-yearly, council-administered professional exam with a hard three-attempt cap** — a pass/fail licensing gate, not something this software computes. Internal semester coursework and continuous assessment still happen and likely still fit Phase 1–3 (hierarchy, registration, per-course attendance) largely as designed, but it is **genuinely unresolved whether Phase 4's GPA/CGPA machinery applies to this school at all**, versus the school only needing pass/fail-per-course tracking that feeds toward professional-exam eligibility. Owner does not yet know the answer and needs to ask the school directly. **Decision, 2026-07-13:** treat as "Other" with fully manual configuration for now (no smart defaults, no assumptions about GPA/CGPA need); revisit as a possible proper fifth institution category once the school's answer comes back. This must be answered before SESSION 59 (Phase 4 schema/settings UI) is built, since building GPA/CGPA machinery this school may not want would be wasted, unverifiable work — flagged explicitly here so it isn't silently assumed resolved by the time that session starts. **✅ Resolved, 2026-07-14, before SESSION 59 began:** owner confirmed the school wants full GPA/CGPA machinery, not pass/fail-only tracking — recorded directly in `migration_session_59.sql`'s own header comment at the point the migration was written, and treated as authoritative here rather than re-litigated. The "Other" category treatment (no smart defaults, fully manual configuration) stands regardless of this answer, per Decision 5 — the resolution settles *whether* GPA/CGPA machinery is wanted, not *how* it's configured. The possible-fifth-category question for NMCN schools remains genuinely open and is not addressed by this resolution.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Walk the owner through Phase 0's five decisions (institution category branch at onboarding; GPA rollup method + retake treatment; programme levels as administrative metadata, not a live daily container; no separate carryover roster screen; soft per-category gating, never hard-locked) and get explicit sign-off on each — do not proceed to schema review on an assumed answer. **✅ Done, 2026-07-13.**
2. Record all five decisions in master plan §12, matching the discipline already used for Problem 8.4's own locked decisions. **Not yet done.**
3. Explicitly amend §3.16: document that `school_type`/`node_type` stop being inert as of this session, and state exactly what they now gate (the institution-vs-secondary academic-engine branch), so this is a visible, deliberate architecture change, not something discovered later in a diff. **Not yet done.**
4. Walk the full Phase 1–5 schema (as documented under Problem 8.7) with the owner; capture any revisions. **Not yet done.**
5. Write the reviewed/revised schema into master-plan §7.27, following §7's existing table-documentation conventions exactly. **✅ Done** — §7.27 written 2026-07-13, all five sub-sections (7.27.1–7.27.5), including the RLS notes and the NMCN open item.
6. Confirm via SQL Editor that none of the proposed new table names collide with anything already live. **Not yet done.**
7. **New, added 2026-07-13:** Owner to ask the real school directly whether they want/need an internal GPA/CGPA-style running average, or only pass/fail-per-course tracking toward NMCN professional-exam eligibility — answer needed before SESSION 59.

**✅ Done Check:** §7.27 exists with the full reviewed schema; §12 records all five Phase 0 decisions; §3.16 is amended to reflect `school_type` becoming load-bearing — all three confirmed present by direct read of the master plan, not assumed written. **✅ Met, 2026-07-13** — §7.27 (all five sub-sections), §12 (five new decision rows), and the §3.16 amendment are all now written into `MASTER_PROJECT_PLAN.md`. Table names confirmed non-colliding with the live schema by construction (§7.27's twelve tables are all net-new names). The NMCN GPA/CGPA-applicability question remains a separately tracked open item that must be resolved before SESSION 59 — it does not block this session's own Done Check, which concerns schema/decision documentation, not the open question itself.

Migrate `faculties`, `departments`, `programmes`, `programme_levels` per §7.27.1, RLS included in the same migration per §3.2 (`[STD]`, school-scoped). **✅ Done, 2026-07-13** — `migration_session_55.sql`, `programme_levels` scoped via join to `programmes` (no `school_id` column of its own, per §7.27.1's printed schema).
2. Migrate `students.matric_number` (nullable text, unique per `school_id`) as an additive column. **✅ Done, 2026-07-13** — partial unique index (`students_matric_number_school_uniq`), not a bare `unique()` constraint, since `matric_number` is nullable and NULLs must not collide.
3. Insert `academics.institution_structure.manage` into the live `permissions` catalog. **✅ Done, 2026-07-13.**

Add the institution-category branch to the onboarding wizard (per Phase 0 Decision 1): once "Higher Institution" is selected, a new step requires defining at least one department → programme → level (faculty optional) plus the matriculation-number format template before the wizard can complete. Pre-fill grading-scale/CA-split defaults per category, but leave every value editable. **✅ Done, 2026-07-13**, with one addition beyond the original card, found during the build rather than anticipated by it: **Step 5 ("Subjects") originally ran unconditionally for every school type.** `subjects`/`class_subjects` (§7.6) has no home in the higher-institution model at all — course definition is separate, later work (§7.27.2, SESSION 56). Fixed in the same session: the institution-category picker was relocated to occupy **Step 5's own slot** (`Step5Gate`), not tucked inside Step 6 as first drafted — Step 6 alone can't gate Step 5's render, since Step 5 comes first. Once category is known: secondary renders `Step5Subjects` unchanged; higher-institution auto-advances straight to Step 6 (`Step6Structure`), skipping Subjects entirely. Total step count (8) and every other step's numbering stayed untouched — this lives inside the existing Step 5/Step 6 slots, not an inserted Step 9. Matric-number format is a guidance-only text field in the wizard UI, not a stored template — §7.27.1 only adds the raw `matric_number` column; no template schema exists, so none was invented silently. CA-split/grading-scale category pre-fills are deferred to SESSION 59 (Phase 4), where `cgpa_policy`/`grade_scales` actually get built — flagging here rather than leaving it looking silently dropped.
5. Build a new institution-only admin Settings tab (hidden entirely when `school_type` is not a higher-institution type) to manage faculties/departments/programmes/levels post-onboarding, modeled on the existing `RolesTab`/`StudentRolesTab` structure. **✅ Done, 2026-07-13** — new `InstitutionTab`, mirrored off `ClassesTab`'s exact `openForm`/`handleSave`/sheet pattern; `SettingsModule`'s `TABS` array is now built conditionally (`buildTabs`) on `school_type` so `Classes` and `Institution` never both show. Required a small additive fix outside this card's original file list: `App.tsx` did not select `school_type` in either of its `schools` queries and did not pass it to `SettingsModule` — the new tab's visibility check needs it. Fixed as a 4-line, diff-confirmed-additive change (two `.select()` calls, `SchoolData` interface, one prop pass-through). No academic-engine render branch was added to `App.tsx` — that stays out of scope for this session, per §3.16's amendment (`school_type` read once, at the wizard/settings-visibility layer only).
6. `npm run build` — clean. **✅ Done, 2026-07-13** — confirmed clean by the owner directly against the real repo (prior verification in-chat was esbuild syntax-parse only, which is not equivalent to `tsc`'s type-checking; this step needed the owner's real build to actually close).

On-device: complete onboarding as a real higher-institution admin, defining a real faculty/department/programme/level structure; confirm the secondary-school onboarding path is completely unaffected (re-run it once as a regression check). **✅ Done, 2026-07-13** — real school (Medical Career College of Science and Technology) onboarded through the wizard; category picker confirmed to appear correctly at the Step 5 slot on repeat visits once `school_type` is set (Subjects correctly skipped, no regression); 1 faculty, 4 departments, 4 programmes created and confirmed visible in the new Institution settings tab; a separate secondary/general-type school re-run confirmed unaffected (Subjects shows normally, `Classes` tab shows, not `Institution`).

**⚠️ New open item surfaced this session — must be resolved before SESSION 56, not discovered after:** §7.27.2 (`programme_curriculum.semester`, `course_offerings.semester`, `course_registrations.semester`) uses a bare `semester text not null` literal (`'first'`/`'second'`) instead of referencing the existing, already-built `terms` table (§7.4) via `term_id uuid references terms(id)`. This disconnects a higher-institution school from the one real "which half of the session is this" mechanism the platform already has (`terms`, school-scoped, free-text `name`/`sequence`, fully built out in `CalendarTab`) — exactly the kind of two-disconnected-sources-of-truth gap §3.6's "one schema serves both shapes" precedent (`uses_terms`) was meant to prevent. **Not fixed in this session** — §7.27.2 is SESSION 56's migration, not 55's, and the owner explicitly directed (2026-07-13) to flag only, not touch the master-plan schema text yet. Practical effect once fixed: a higher-institution school just names its `terms` rows "First Semester"/"Second Semester" instead of "First Term"/"Second Term" — same table, same UI, labels only. **This must be corrected in §7.27.2 before SESSION 56 migrates those tables as currently written, or the bug ships.** See master-plan §12 for the full decision record.

**⚠️ Second, larger open item surfaced this session — blocks this session's own Done Check, not just a future one:** while drafting the SQL Editor confirmation query for the Done Check below, discovered that **no column or table anywhere in §7.27.1 as accepted links a student to a `programme_level` at all.** `students`' real shape was confirmed directly (`select column_name, data_type from information_schema.columns where table_name = 'students'`): it has `matric_number` (this session) and `class_node_id` (§7.5, secondary-only) — no `programme_level_id`, no join table, nothing. This is not a wrong-guess-at-a-column-name problem; the linkage was simply never designed. Walked the owner through the real tradeoff (direct FK matching `class_node_id`'s existing no-history precedent, vs. a new history table) before defaulting either way — **owner chose a history table**, since retroactive "what level was this student in last session" is a real, known need for this school. New table `student_programme_placements` designed and added to master-plan §7.27.1a (schema, RLS, and the `is_current` demote-then-insert pattern mirroring `sessions.is_current`) and §12 (decision record) — **not yet migrated, and no migration file has been run for it.** See §7.27.1a for the full schema.

**This session's own Done Check (matric-number half) cannot close until `student_programme_placements` is migrated, an admin UI to admit a student into a placement exists, and a real student is admitted on-device and confirmed via SQL Editor read — none of which are done yet.** Treat this as a required follow-up step for this session's own card, not a deferred future-session item like the semester/term_id gap above.

**✅ Done Check:** Admin at the real institution creates their actual hierarchy on-device; a real student is admitted into a specific programme/level with a correctly formatted matriculation number, confirmed via direct SQL Editor read. **✅ Met, 2026-07-13** for the hierarchy-creation half (1 faculty / 4 departments / 4 programmes, confirmed visible in the Institution tab, on-device). **Not yet met** for the matriculation-number half — and the reason turned out to be bigger than "not yet tested": there is no column or table anywhere in §7.27.1 as originally accepted that links a student to a `programme_level` at all. `students` has `matric_number` (this session) and `class_node_id` (§7.5, secondary-only) — nothing for higher-institution placement. This was found while drafting the confirmation query below; the first draft guessed `s.programme_level_id`, which was then checked directly against the real `students` table shape (`select column_name, data_type from information_schema.columns where table_name = 'students'`) and confirmed **not to exist**.

**Design decision made 2026-07-13 (see master-plan §7.27.1a and §12 for the full record):** owner chose a new history-tracking join table, `student_programme_placements`, over a direct `students.programme_level_id` FK — the latter would have matched this codebase's only existing precedent (`class_node_id`, overwritten each session with no history) but loses retroactive "what level was this student in last session" the moment it's overwritten, which the owner confirmed is a real need for this school. `student_programme_placements` is **not yet migrated** — this is now a hard blocker on this Done Check, not a testing gap. Once migrated, the confirmation query is:

```sql
-- Confirm a real student has a matric_number and a current programme/level placement
select s.id, s.matric_number, pl.level_label, p.name as programme_name, spp.session_id
from students s
join student_programme_placements spp on spp.student_id = s.id and spp.is_current = true
join programme_levels pl on pl.id = spp.programme_level_id
join programmes p on p.id = pl.programme_id
where s.matric_number is not null;
```

**✅ Done Check closed, 2026-07-13.** All four items met: (1) `student_programme_placements` migrated (`migration_session_55b.sql`) — first run hit a real error, `current_actor_id() does not exist`, because the self-read policy (and the matching prose in §7.27.2, copied from the same wrong assumption) referenced a helper function that has never existed anywhere in this codebase; verified against §3.2's real function list (only `current_school_id()` and `current_actor_type()` exist) and corrected to the actual established pattern — a lookup against `app_identities` (`auth_user_id = auth.uid()` → `actor_record_id`), matching every other self-read policy in this schema. Re-run succeeded. (2) Admin UI built: a "+ Place student" action on each level row inside `InstitutionTab`, with a debounced type-to-search student picker (`useDebouncedValue`, ≥2 chars, 300ms) rather than a static dropdown — the load-everything-then-filter pattern used elsewhere in this file for small lists (staff, class nodes) doesn't scale to a school's full student body. Required four new functions added to `settingsService.ts` (`searchStudents`, `loadCurrentSession`, `loadPlacementsForProgramme`, `upsertPlacement`), written against the file's own real conventions rather than guessed. (3) A real student (Musa Aminu, Medical Career College of Science and Technology) admitted on-device into Nursing Science / Year 1. **Note on this step:** an earlier confirmation attempt returned zero rows — traced to a query error, not a data error: the placement's own `id` (`spp.id`) had been mistaken for the student's `id` when assigning a matric number, and a same-named second "Musa Aminu" row existed with a different id — both resolved by joining explicitly through `spp.student_id` rather than assuming which id was which. (4) Confirmation query run and returned a real, fully-joined row: student name, `matric_number` (`MED/NSC/26/001`, assigned after the above mix-up was resolved), level, and programme name all correct. See Log rows 54 and 55.

#### SESSION 56 — Migrate Phase 2 schema (course catalogue + registration tables); curriculum-definition admin UI ✅ Done Check closed 2026-07-13
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7
**Touches:** Supabase migration (`courses`, `programme_curriculum`, `course_offerings`, `course_registrations`), `permissions` catalog (`academics.curriculum.manage`), new admin curriculum UI
**Depends on:** SESSION 55 (a real programme/level structure must exist to attach a curriculum to)

**Open item carried forward from SESSION 55 — ✅ resolved before step 1, 2026-07-13:** §7.27.2 originally used a bare `semester text not null` literal on `programme_curriculum`, `course_offerings`, and `course_registrations`. Corrected to nullable `term_id uuid references terms(id)` (falls back to `session_id` when `schools.uses_terms = false`, per §7.4's convention) before `migration_session_56.sql` was drafted. The card's own step 3/5 wording below has also been corrected from "semester" to "term" to match — the original text was stale once the schema fix landed. §7.27.2's `course_registrations` self-read RLS policy (`current_actor_id()`, the same invented-function bug found in `student_programme_placements`' first migration) was also corrected to the real `app_identities` pattern before this migration was written, so it never hit that error directly. See master-plan §12.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Migrate `courses`, `programme_curriculum`, `course_offerings`, `course_registrations` per §7.27.2, RLS included per §3.2 (`course_registrations` staff-manage + student self-view-own, matching the `academics.course_registration.view_own` key). **✅ Done** — `migration_session_56.sql` run against Supabase, confirmed via `information_schema.tables` returning all four table names.
2. Insert `academics.curriculum.manage` and `academics.course_registration.manage`/`.view_own` into the live `permissions` catalog. **✅ Done** — all three keys inserted (`academics.institution_structure.manage` was already inserted in SESSION 55, not re-inserted here).
3. Build the admin-facing curriculum-definition UI: for a given programme/level/term, define the course list with `requirement_type` (core/elective) and optional `prerequisite_course_id`. **✅ Done** — new `CurriculumTab` in `SettingsModule.tsx`: Programme → Level → Term (term picker only rendered when the school has any terms defined, otherwise curriculum is session-scoped only per §7.4) → core/elective list with running credit-unit total. A course-catalogue CRUD section (`courses` table had no UI anywhere before this session) was added inside `InstitutionTab`, next to Programmes, since curriculum can't reference courses that don't exist yet — this was implicit in the card's step 3 but not called out explicitly; flagging here rather than leaving it looking unplanned.
4. `npm run build` — clean. **✅ Done**, after two real bugs found and fixed during the build/test cycle, not anticipated by the original card:
   - `CurriculumTab` was referenced in the render tree before it was ever defined — would have been a hard build failure. Written and verified against real `Course`/`CurriculumEntry`/`Programme`/`ProgrammeLevel`/`Term` types.
   - First real `npm run build` (run on the actual repo, not the sandbox check) failed with `TS6133: 'currentSessionId' is declared but its value is never read` inside `CurriculumTab` — dead state left over from the terms-fetch gate (the actual gating logic reads `terms.length`/`sessRes.data.id` directly, never the stored session id). Removed the unused state and setter; rebuilt clean.
5. On-device: admin defines a real curriculum for one programme/level/term, mixing core and elective courses, confirmed via SQL Editor spot-check of `programme_curriculum` rows. **✅ Done** — see Done Check below.

**Real bug found and fixed during on-device testing, outside the card's original scope:** the course-catalogue form's "Credit units" field used `onChange={v => setCCreditUnits(Number(v) || 1)}` against a `number`-typed state. `Number('') === 0`, which is falsy, so clearing the field to type a new value snapped it straight back to `1` — made the field practically stuck at the default, and specifically broke any value starting with `1` (10, 12, 100, etc.) since the intermediate empty state during editing kept resetting it. Fixed by holding the field as a raw digit-only string while typing (`cCreditUnits: string`), deferring numeric coercion and validation (whole number, ≥ 1) to save time in `handleSaveCourse`, with a real error message instead of a silent fallback.

**✅ Done Check:** A real curriculum row confirmed via direct SQL read, joined across `programme_curriculum` → `programmes`/`programme_levels`/`courses`/`terms`: MLS programme, level 100, course NSC 101 ("Intro to Nursing", 5 credit units), `requirement_type = 'core'`, `term_name` correctly `null` (this school does not use terms — nullable `term_id` fallback behaved exactly as designed, not as a gap).

#### SESSION 57 — Registration workflow: core auto-populate, elective selection, carryover pre-population, credit-load cap ✅ Done Check closed 2026-07-14
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7
**Touches:** new `courseRegistrationService.ts`, new `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx` (admin/course-adviser-facing, own top-level module — not folded into `SettingsModule.tsx`, matching the existing `AttendanceModule.tsx`/`StudentsModule.tsx` operational-vs-configuration split), `App.tsx` (route + nav wiring), `index.css` (new `.reg-*` block)
**Depends on:** SESSION 56

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Build the registration flow: admin/adviser opens a student's programme/level/term curriculum; core courses auto-populate; electives are chosen from the allowed list.
2. If the student has an outstanding carryover (a `course_registrations` row with `status = 'carryover'` from a prior session), pre-populate it automatically into the new registration list, flagged `is_carryover = true`, linked via `original_registration_id` back to the original failed attempt.
3. Enforce the school's configured `min_credit_load`/`max_credit_load` (from `programmes`, per Phase 1) across the full registration set including any pre-populated carryover — surface a clear error if the load would exceed the cap, do not silently truncate.
4. Gate all writes on `academics.course_registration.manage`; gate the student/guardian's own read-only view on `academics.course_registration.view_own`.
5. `npm run build` — clean.
6. On-device: register one real student into a mix of core/elective courses; separately, manually seed a carryover row for a second student via SQL Editor and register them for the following session, confirming the carryover pre-populates and is correctly flagged.

**✅ Done Check — closed in full, 2026-07-14:**
- `courseRegistrationService.ts` built: resolves a student's current placement (`student_programme_placements`, `is_current = true`) → curriculum join; `loadOutstandingCarryover()` finds prior-session rows with `status = 'carryover' and original_registration_id is null`; `checkCreditLoad()` is a pure, nullable-aware function (no cap enforced when `min_credit_load`/`max_credit_load` are unset); `registerStudent()` writes the full batch (new + carryover re-registration) as one call, soft-withdraw-then-insert, no hard deletes.
- `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx` built as its own top-level module (search student → placement resolves → term picker, conditional on `uses_terms` → core/elective/carryover selection → running credit total with live cap warning → save).
- `App.tsx` wired: `NavTab` union extended, module imported, `TabContent` branch added, nav entry placed in the "Administration" section of the More sheet (next to Settings), gated on `isAdmin` + higher-ed `schoolType` (double-gate — the module itself also checks internally).
- `index.css`: new `.reg-*` block added (was missing entirely at the point wiring finished) — mirrors `.set-*`'s exact tokens/spacing/radius so the module doesn't visually diverge; every class the component actually references was cross-checked against the stylesheet with zero gaps.
- `npm run build` — confirmed clean on the real repo.
- **On-device, Scenario A (ordinary registration):** one real student registered into 1 core + 1 elective course. Verified via joined SQL read.
- **On-device, Scenario B (carryover-inclusive registration):** a carryover row manually seeded for a second student against a prior session (`status = 'carryover'`, `original_registration_id = null`), confirmed visible to `loadOutstandingCarryover()`'s exact query shape, then re-registered for the current session as a new row (`is_carryover = true`, `original_registration_id` pointing back at the seeded row). Confirmed the new row is correctly linked, and — separately and explicitly — that the original seeded row was left completely untouched (`status` still `carryover`, `is_carryover` still `false`, `original_registration_id` still `null`), which is the specific guarantee SESSION 57's carryover design depends on.
- **One real bug found during Scenario A's own verification, not in shipped code:** see PRD Log row 57 and master-plan §12 for the full write-up — a join in the verification query itself (not in `courseRegistrationService.ts`) fanned one registration out into two display rows. Traced, confirmed the underlying data was already correct (2 rows, not 3), and the verification query fixed to match `loadCurriculum()`'s real resolution path. No application code changed as a result.

Closes Phase 2's Definition of Done from Problem 8.7.

#### SESSION 58 — Migrate Phase 3 (per-course attendance); attendance UI reusing Problem 5.1's save-flow pattern ✅ Done Check closed 2026-07-14
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7
**Touches:** Supabase migration (`course_attendance_sessions`, `course_attendance_records`, `course_offerings.attendance_eligibility_threshold_pct`), new per-course attendance UI
**Depends on:** SESSION 57 (needs real `course_registrations` rows to derive a register from)

**Progress, 2026-07-14:** Steps 1–2 (migration) ✅ confirmed — `migration_session_58.sql` run against Supabase, verified via direct `information_schema`/`pg_constraint`/`pg_policies`/`permissions` reads (not just an absence-of-error assumption): both tables exist with correct columns, the `attendance_eligibility_threshold_pct` column landed on `course_offerings`, the `(course_attendance_session_id, student_id)` unique constraint is real, all 4 RLS policies present, both permission keys inserted. **Two deliberate deviations from §7.27.3's original text, decided before migrating, not discovered after:** `marked_by uuid not null references staff(id)` added (the original spec had no lecturer attribution at all); the unique constraint itself added (the original spec had none, which would have broken `onConflict`-based retap-to-update). **RLS self-read, decided to cover both audiences rather than picking one precedent:** unlike `course_registrations` (student-only self-read) or `attendance_records` (guardian-only self-read), `course_attendance_records` grants both — student self-read as the primary path (a higher-ed student is the platform's primary user for their own exam-gating attendance percentage), guardian self-read as an additive policy scoped through `student_guardians` and correctly gated on `has_platform_access = true` (a real constraint, checked and initially missed on a first draft, then fixed before finalizing — not shipped with the gap). See master-plan §7.27.3 for the full reasoning on all three deviations, recorded there before the migration ran.

`courseAttendanceService.ts` built — mirrors `attendanceService.ts`'s exact `Result<T>`/optimistic-write/offline-queue pattern (not `courseRegistrationService.ts`'s `{data, error}` shape), per this session's explicit "reuse as-is" instruction. Register composition computed live from `course_registrations`, never stored redundantly. `ensureCourseAttendanceSession()` reuses `isSchoolDay()` as-is (imported, not reimplemented) to refuse session creation on a non-school day. Type-checked clean (zero errors) against the real `attendanceService.ts`/`courseRegistrationService.ts` — two real issues (unused type import, implicit-any sort params) caught by the actual `tsc` run and fixed, not left in. **A fourth deviation found while writing this file, not before:** `course_attendance_sessions` had no DB-level uniqueness on `(course_offering_id, session_date)`, only `course_attendance_records` did — `migration_session_58b.sql` (a genuine follow-up, run after the original migration was already live, not a silent edit to shipped SQL) adds `unique (course_offering_id, session_date)`; confirmed landed via a direct `pg_constraint` read.

**Steps 3–7 completed 2026-07-14.** `CourseAttendanceModule.tsx` built — offering picker, date bar, register (composition computed live from `course_registrations`, matching `courseAttendanceService.ts`'s own design), optimistic mark/batch-mark ported directly from `AttendanceModule.tsx`'s `handleStatusTap`/`handleBatchMark` (saving-id tracking, background write, revert-on-failure with a 3s error badge, offline queue), eligibility-percentage badge, holiday-gated session creation via `ensureCourseAttendanceSession()`. Deliberately excludes AttendanceModule.tsx's whole-school analytics panel, two-tier parent/class picker, history strip, and after-hours edit lock — none asked for by this card, and `course_attendance_records` has no "school hours end" concept to lock against; adding them would have been scope creep. Reuses `.att-*` CSS tokens directly for generic pieces (status buttons, empty/error states, pills); 11 new `.catt-*` rules added for genuinely new elements, cross-checked class-by-class against the component with zero gaps. `App.tsx` wired: `NavTab` union, import, `TabContent` branch, and a `canViewCourseAttendance` nav entry in the More sheet's Modules section — **deliberately not admin-gated** like `course_registration`, since this is a per-lecturer operational task (`can('academics.course_attendance.mark') || isAdmin`, gated by `isHigherEd`), matching the operational-vs-admin split already drawn for `course_registration` itself.

**One real gap found and fixed during this same close-out, not anticipated by the original card:** `CourseAttendanceModule.tsx` initially had no internal `schoolType` gate of its own — the nav link correctly hid via `isHigherEd`, but the route itself had no second gate, unlike `CourseRegistrationModule`'s own documented internal `isHigherInstitutionSchoolType` check. Fixed: `schoolType` added as a prop, a local `isHigherInstitutionSchoolType()` helper added (matching `CourseRegistrationModule`'s own local-copy pattern), checked before any data loading starts and again at render, with an honest "not available for this school type" empty state rather than a blank/broken screen for a stale-nav-state or deep-link edge case.

**A separate, real `npm run build` failure found and fixed, unrelated to any TypeScript issue:** the new `.catt-*` CSS comments contained apostrophes adjacent to hyphenated selectors (e.g. `.res-approve-badge--incomplete's warning-subtle pattern`) and one double-quoted phrase nested inside a comment, which broke Tailwind's Lightning-CSS-based parser (`CssSyntaxError: Unterminated string`) despite parsing cleanly under a plain CSS parser during development. Fixed by rewording every comment in the new block to remove apostrophes/quotes entirely; re-verified balanced and parses clean. `npm run build` subsequently confirmed clean by the owner on the real repo.

**On-device, confirmed via screenshots, not just SQL reads:** a real course_offerings row and course_registrations row were seeded via SQL (`sql/session_58_test_data_final.sql`) as a deliberate, documented stopgap — no `course_offerings` creation UI exists anywhere in the codebase yet (a real, separate gap surfaced during this close-out; see the open-items note below). Idris Abdulkarim (lecturer) marked Musa Aminu (MED/NSC/26/001) present for ANA 102 on 2026-07-14. **After a full re-login (not just a soft reload)**, the mark persisted correctly, matric number displayed, and the eligibility badge showed an accurate 100% (1/1 sessions present) — the one Done Check clause that had not been checked at all until this point. Navigating to 2026-07-13, a real school-calendar holiday added via Settings, correctly showed the "No school today" empty state instead of a markable register, confirming `ensureCourseAttendanceSession()`'s holiday gate fires against real `school_calendar_days` data, not just a code read. **SESSION 58's Done Check is now ✅ closed in full** — both named clauses (reload-persistence, accurate eligibility percentage) confirmed on a real device, not assumed from a clean build or a passing typecheck.

**Real, separate open items surfaced during this close-out, deliberately not fixed here (out of this session's scope, tracked forward):**
1. **No `course_offerings` creation UI exists anywhere.** `courses` (catalogue, `InstitutionTab`) and `programme_curriculum` (course↔programme/level/term, `CurriculumTab`) both have real admin UI; the actual per-session "this course, this term, this lecturer" object does not. Every test in this session used a direct SQL insert. This blocks any real (non-SQL) use of course attendance, and by extension SESSION 59/60's scoring work once it depends on real offerings too.
2. **No `schoolType`-aware nav swap.** `BottomNav.tsx`'s `NAV_ITEMS` has no `schoolType` field at all — the secondary-school `AttendanceModule` occupies the permanent bottom-bar `'attendance'` slot for every school type, including higher-ed, where `CourseAttendanceModule` is reachable only one level deeper via the More sheet. `App.tsx`'s `'attendance'` branch is gated by actor type only, never by `schoolType`, contradicting §3.16's own stated intent that `school_type` should be read once to swap the whole academic engine cleanly.
3. **`GuardianPortal.tsx`/`guardianService.ts` have zero higher-ed awareness** — no references to `course_registrations`/`course_attendance`/`schoolType` anywhere. A guardian of a higher-ed student today sees the secondary attendance tab inside `GuardianPortal`'s own internal tab bar, with no path to course-registration attendance or eligibility at all. Same class of gap as item 2, on a second surface.
4. **`StudentsModule.tsx` has no higher-ed field set.** Entirely `class_node_id`-driven; no `matric_number` or programme/level field anywhere in the add/edit form. A higher-ed student added after onboarding (which is the only place `student_programme_placements` currently gets written, via `InstitutionTab`'s "+ Place student" action) has no ongoing-operations path to receive a programme placement.
5. **Onboarding-wizard role/permission seeding is not linked to any higher-ed permission key.** No role at this school (or, by inference, any school) was ever granted `academics.course_attendance.mark` by default — the real lecturer used for on-device testing (Idris Abdulkarim) held **zero roles at all** (`staff_roles` had no row for him), and even the school's real "Lecturer 1" role (11 permissions) does not include this key. Unblocked for testing via a direct `staff_permission_class_scope` override, a legitimate independent grant mechanism per `permissionsService.ts`'s own documented design — but the underlying gap (no admin screen links new permission keys to existing roles, and this particular staff record was never assigned any role at all) is real and unresolved. Likely affects `academics.course_registration.*` from SESSION 57 identically; not separately confirmed.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Migrate `course_attendance_sessions`, `course_attendance_records` per §7.27.3, RLS per §3.2.
2. Add `attendance_eligibility_threshold_pct` to `course_offerings` (school/lecturer-configurable, no hardcoded default such as 75%).
3. Build the per-course attendance UI: register composition computed live from `course_registrations` for that offering (never stored redundantly); same optimistic-save/dirty-state/offline-queue UX Problem 5.1 established for `AttendanceModule.tsx`, pointed at the new table instead.
4. Gate a `course_attendance_session` from being creatable on a school-calendar holiday, reusing `school_calendar_days`/`isSchoolDay()` from Problem 5.2 exactly as-is.
5. Surface the computed attendance-eligibility percentage wherever a lecturer views their register or a student views their own attendance — informational only this session, not yet wired to block exam entry.
6. `npm run build` — clean.
7. On-device: a lecturer takes attendance for a real course offering across several sessions; only students with a live registration for that offering appear; marks persist correctly on reload; attendance is correctly blocked on a real school-calendar holiday.

**✅ Done Check:** Attendance persists correctly across a reload for a real course offering; the eligibility percentage is visible and accurate for at least one real student — closes Phase 3's Definition of Done.

#### SESSION 60a — CA/exam component breakdown for course_results (owner-directed follow-up, not in original Phase 4 scope)
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7 (extension, scoped 2026-07-15 — SESSION 60's single-`score` shape was correct for Phase 4's own Done Check, but does not reflect how CA + exam are actually recorded at most real institutions)
**Touches:** new tables (`course_result_components`, `course_result_component_scores`), `courseResultsService.ts`, `CourseResultsModule.tsx`, possibly a new Settings tab
**Depends on:** SESSION 60 (this extends `course_results`, does not replace it)

**Why this exists as a parallel schema to `score_components`, not a reuse of it:** same underlying philosophy as `score_components` — school defines named, weighted components once, an engine sums them — but a different anchor object (`course_registrations`, not `class_nodes`/subject), matching the precedent already set for why `cgpa_policy` is its own table rather than folded into `score_components` (§12 decision log, "Should `class_nodes`/`score_components` be reused or forked," 2026-07-12).

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Migrate `course_result_components` (`school_id`, `name`, `weight_pct numeric(5,2) check (0-100)`, `sort_order`) — column-for-column the same shape as the existing `score_components` table, renamed to this domain. Application enforces `sum(weight_pct) per school = 100`, same as `score_components`'s own header comment — **note for whoever picks this up: confirm first whether that sum-to-100 rule is actually enforced anywhere today (UI validation, a DB constraint, or neither) before assuming there's a working pattern to copy.** A grep across `SettingsModule.tsx`/`settingsService.ts`/`resultsService.ts` at the time this card was written found only a read function (`loadScoreComponents()`) and a schema comment — no write/upsert function or admin UI tab for `score_components` in any file reviewed. If that's still true, this session needs to build real validation from scratch (for both the secondary and higher-ed component tables), not copy an existing pattern.
2. Migrate `course_result_component_scores` (`course_registration_id`, `course_result_component_id`, `value numeric(5,2)`, `entered_by`, timestamps, `unique(course_registration_id, course_result_component_id)`) — mirrors `scores`' shape, minus `approved_by`/`approved_at` (SESSION 60's `course_results` has no approval-gate concept; don't introduce one here without a separate decision, since higher-ed `finalized_at` may or may not need to become a two-step approve flow — flag to owner if this comes up, don't assume).
3. RLS on `course_result_component_scores`: write access gated to the offering's own `staff_id` — same intent as `scores`' `teaching_assignments`-based policy, but resolved through `course_offerings.staff_id` (this domain's equivalent), not `teaching_assignments`. Confirm the exact live join path via SQL before writing the policy, same discipline SESSION 60 itself used for `course_offerings`' RLS.
4. Decide and document: `course_results.score`/`letter_grade`/`grade_point` become populated by summing `course_result_component_scores` **at finalize time** (recommended — matches `student_gpa_snapshots`' existing "cache, recomputed on finalize, never derived live" precedent from §4.1), not as a live view (unlike `subject_results`, which is a view for the secondary path). State explicitly in code comments why this session chose cache-on-finalize over a view, so it isn't re-litigated later.
5. `CourseResultsModule.tsx`: replace the single score input per student row with one input per configured component, a running weighted-total preview before finalize, and a school-admin screen for defining components (name/weight/order) — likely a new `SettingsModule.tsx` tab, structure TBD pending step 1's finding.
6. `npm run build` — clean.
7. On-device: define at least two components for a real school (e.g. CA 40% / Exam 60%), enter both for a real course_registration, finalize, and confirm the resulting `course_results.score`/`letter_grade`/`grade_point` match a hand-calculated weighted sum exactly. Then confirm SESSION 60's existing GPA/CGPA recompute still produces correct results downstream (`gpaService.ts` itself needs no changes — it only ever consumes the final `score`, confirmed by design in SESSION 60 — this step just proves that boundary held).

**✅ Done Check — CONFIRMED 2026-07-15.** A real finalized score, entered as separate weighted components, produced a correct summed `course_results` row, hand-verified against the configured weights, and the existing GPA/CGPA pipeline downstream was confirmed still correct (see SESSION 60's own Done Check re-confirmation).

#### SESSION 60c — Per-course override of CA/exam component weights (owner-directed follow-up, not in original Phase 4 scope) — ✅ Complete, 2026-07-15 (override mechanism, both reported bugs, and the CSS fix all confirmed on-device — see Done Check below)
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7 (extension of SESSION 60a — a school-wide CA/Exam split doesn't fit every course; some need a different split or an extra component, e.g. a lab-based course needing "Practical" alongside CA/Exam)
**Touches:** `course_result_components` (altered, not a new table — see design correction below), `courseResultsService.ts`, `CourseResultsModule.tsx`, `SettingsModule.tsx`
**Depends on:** SESSION 60a (extends the component set it introduced)

**Design correction mid-build, recorded here so it isn't re-litigated:** the first drafted migration created a separate `course_result_component_overrides` table. This was actually applied live before a real design bug was caught: `course_result_component_scores.course_result_component_id` references `course_result_components(id)` only, and an override row in a second table has its own, disjoint id — that FK cannot accept it. Corrected by folding the override concept into `course_result_components` itself via a nullable `course_id` column (`NULL` = school-wide, set = course-specific override-or-addition, merged by name) instead of a second table. The abandoned table was dropped in the same corrected migration. No RLS policy changes were needed — existing policies are scoped on `school_id` only, unaffected by whether `course_id` is null or set.

**Two real bugs found on-device by the owner after the first cut shipped, both fixed same session:**
1. School-wide components were not editable from the per-course override screen at all (rendered read-only), leaving no way to rebalance CA/Exam to make room for a new course-specific component.
2. The fix for (1) over-corrected: editing a school-wide row from the per-course screen wrote directly to the real school-wide row (`course_id: null`), so adding a course-specific "Lab" component silently changed the school-wide CA/Exam split for every other course too. Root-caused to the save/delete handlers, both fixed so the per-course screen can now only ever write/delete a `course_id`-scoped row — "editing" an inherited value there creates a course-specific override of that name instead, leaving the real school-wide row untouched.

**A third, unrelated defect surfaced during this session's verification, not owner-reported:** `CourseResultsModule.tsx` and `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx` were both shipping with zero matching CSS for the classNames they use (28 and 27 classes respectively) — likely unstyled since first built, since every prior check on these files only verified TypeScript, never cross-checked classNames against `index.css`. Fixed by adding both class families, modeled on already-shipped sibling patterns rather than invented fresh. Not yet re-confirmed on-device at a real viewport — flagged as the one open item below.

**✅ Done Check — CONFIRMED 2026-07-15 in full.** The corrected single-table migration is live; `resolveCourseResultComponents()`/`mergeCourseResultComponents()` are the single by-name merge function used by both the admin preview and the server-side finalize check; a course-specific override no longer touches the school-wide row under any interaction path, confirmed by tracing the actual save/delete code, not just re-testing the reported scenario. **CSS fix now on-device confirmed:** both `CourseResultsModule.tsx` and `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx` render correctly styled at a real viewport, confirmed directly by the owner — no longer static-analysis-only. This row's Definition of Done is fully closed.

**Depends on:** SESSION 60 (reads `course_results` + `student_gpa_snapshots`, both populated there)

Student-facing screen: read-only view of the above for the signed-in student, scoped the same way `portalService.ts`'s existing student-self functions are scoped (resolve `auth.users.id` → `app_identities` → student record, per that file's own established identity-resolution pattern — reuse it, don't re-derive).

PDF export — confirm what PDF-generation approach (if any) already exists elsewhere in this codebase before introducing a new dependency; check e.g. `report_card_templates`/`promotion_history`-adjacent code or any existing print/PDF flow for the secondary-school side first, since duplicating a working pattern is preferable to a second PDF library.

**✅ Done Check:** A real student's transcript is viewable on-screen and exportable as a PDF, with course/grade/GP/CGPA all matching the already-verified `student_gpa_snapshots` data exactly — no new computation introduced, this session is display-only. **Fully confirmed, 2026-07-16, with real scope growth beyond the original card:**

- Steps 1–4 delivered as specified: `loadStudentTranscript(studentId)` in `courseResultsService.ts`; a student-facing transcript tab in `GuardianPortal.tsx`; an admin-facing equivalent added to `StudentsModule.tsx`'s detail sheet (higher-ed schools only); PDF export via a new `transcriptPdf.ts` (confirmed no existing PDF-generation library first, per step 4's own instruction — `jsPDF`/`jspdf-autotable` genuinely new, not a duplicate of an existing pattern).
- **Owner-directed extension, not in the original card:** an admin-publish gate on `course_results`, added mid-session once the transcript surfaced that a finalized score was instantly visible to guardians with no review step. New `published_by`/`published_at` columns, RLS tightened to `published_at is not null` for guardian/student reads, mirroring the already-working secondary-school `scores.approved_by`/`approved_at` pattern rather than inventing a new one. `CourseResultsModule.tsx` gained per-row Publish/Unpublish plus a bulk "publish all finalized" action, gated on the previously-uncatalogued-but-unused `academics.results.publish` key, kept deliberately separate from Finalize/Update to preserve scorer/reviewer separation of duties.
- **Four real bugs found on-device and fixed, not caught by static analysis:** (1) unpublishing/updating a result didn't actually hide it from guardians — two independent causes, both fixed (the cached `student_gpa_snapshots` table wasn't recomputed on publish/unpublish, and `loadStudentTranscript()`'s own per-course query separately still filtered on `finalized_at` instead of `published_at`); (2) a genuine, pre-existing, unrelated RLS hole on `course_registrations` surfaced during the investigation (its staff policy checked school match only, never actor type, letting guardians read/write every registration in the school) — closed with a real staff-only check plus a properly scoped guardian read policy; (3) Publish/Unpublish required two taps due to a stale-closure bug reading state from the render closure instead of live state; (4) closing the guardian leak broke admin's own CGPA/GPA display, since the cached snapshot has only one variant (published-only) — fixed by having admin's view compute CGPA/GPA live from `gpaService.ts`'s real functions instead of reading the guardian-only cache.
- A genuine, twice-recurring `TS2589` (`tsc` type-instantiation-too-deep) build failure was found via the project's real `npm run build`, not the isolated stub-based typecheck used earlier in this arc — the first fix attempt was reported clean but wasn't re-verified against the real compiler and the same error resurfaced relocated; the real fix restructures the conditional query into two fully-separate `if`/`else` branches instead of unifying two query-builder types across a reassignment or ternary.
- **Owner confirmed the full arc on-device, 2026-07-16:** guardian transcript view, admin `StudentsModule` transcript view with drafts explicitly marked, publish/unpublish toggling correctly in both directions, and PDF export — not just a clean build.
