#### SESSION 60d — Publish/unpublish write-path lockdown at the RLS layer (owner-directed follow-up, not in original Phase 4/60b scope) — ✅ Complete, 2026-07-16 (migration run, on-device confirmed by owner)
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7 (extension of SESSION 60b — closes a real DB-layer gap SESSION 60b's own publish-gate work left open)
**Touches:** new migration (`migration_publish_lockdown.sql`) — `course_results` trigger only, no application code changed
**Depends on:** SESSION 60b (introduces `published_by`/`published_at`, and the `academics.results.publish` application-layer gate this session hardens)

**The gap found, precisely:** SESSION 60b's `academics.results.publish` permission key was, and remains, correctly checked client-side in `CourseResultsModule.tsx` (`canPublish = useAuthStore((s) => s.can('academics.results.publish'))`) before showing the Publish/Unpublish controls. But `course_results`' only write RLS policy, `course_results_staff_manage`, is an unkeyed `ALL` policy scoped on school membership alone — it does not check that permission key at all. This meant any staff member at the school, holding the key or not, could call `publishCourseResult()`/`unpublishCourseResult()` directly (e.g. via API/devtools, bypassing the UI) and it would succeed at the database level — the client-side check was a UI convenience, not a security boundary. The separate read-side gate (`published_at is not null`, gating what guardians/students can see) was independently confirmed already correct and unaffected by this gap.

**Why a trigger, not a second RLS policy:** Postgres RLS's `with_check` sees the whole row being written, not a column-level diff, so "gate only these two columns" can't be expressed as a plain policy without either splitting the table or accepting a coarser check. A `BEFORE UPDATE` trigger on `course_results` was used instead, firing its permission check only when `published_by`/`published_at` are actually changing in that statement — ordinary finalize/edit writes that never touch those columns pass through untouched, preserving `course_results_staff_manage` for everything else.

**A real, deliberate asymmetry, not an oversight:** `saveCourseResult()` (the finalize/edit path) already clears `published_by`/`published_at` to `null` on every re-finalize, unconditionally, by design (SESSION 60b's own re-review-after-correction behavior) — for any staff member who can finalize, not just those holding `academics.results.publish`. Gating *both* directions equally would have broken that already-verified behavior. The trigger therefore only gates the *setting* of `published_by`/`published_at` to non-null (`current_is_admin() or current_has_permission('academics.results.publish')`); clearing them to null (unpublish, or the re-finalize side-effect) is left ungated, matching both `unpublishCourseResult()`'s intended single-key design and `saveCourseResult()`'s existing unconditional-clear behavior. **Known, accepted residual gap:** a direct unpublish (not via re-finalize) by a staff member who lacks the permission key is not blocked at the DB layer today — no practical exposure through the app itself, since the UI already hides the Unpublish control from non-holders, but it remains open against direct API/DB access. Logged here rather than silently left undocumented; candidate for a follow-up session if the owner wants it closed too.

**✅ Done Check — CONFIRMED 2026-07-16.** Migration run; owner tested on-device inside the real app and confirmed the publish/unpublish gate works correctly.

#### SESSION 61 — Migrate Phase 5 schema (academic standing); standing-rules settings UI
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7
**Touches:** Supabase migration (`academic_standing_rules`, `academic_standing_events`), new settings UI
**Depends on:** SESSION 60 (standing rules evaluate CGPA, which must be computable first)

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Migrate `academic_standing_rules` (`probation_cgpa_threshold`, `withdrawal_cgpa_threshold`, `max_carryover_courses_before_review`, `max_registration_multiplier`) and `academic_standing_events` per §7.27.5, RLS per §3.2, mirroring the `promotion_history` audit-trail pattern already established in Problem 8.4.
2. Insert `academics.academic_standing.review` into the live `permissions` catalog.
3. Build the admin settings UI for defining the school's `academic_standing_rules` values — every value editable, none hardcoded, per the "10 variations" finding in Problem 8.7's root-cause writeup.
4. `npm run build` — clean.
5. On-device: admin defines real standing-rule thresholds for the school, confirmed via SQL Editor read.

**✅ Done Check:** `academic_standing_rules` correctly populated for the real school, confirmed via direct SQL read. **Fully confirmed, 2026-07-16:**

- **A real RLS design mistake was caught before any migration ran, not after:** the first migration draft (from a since-recovered, previously-unrecorded session) referenced `current_staff_id()` for both tables' write policies — a function that does not exist anywhere in this codebase. Rather than trust that draft, the live helper set was queried directly (`pg_proc`/`pg_namespace`) before running anything: only `current_actor_type()`, `current_has_permission(perm_key text)`, `current_is_admin()`, and `current_school_id()` exist. The migration was rewritten before being run — `academic_standing_rules` and `academic_standing_events` both use `[STD]`-scoped reads plus a `current_has_permission('academics.academic_standing.review')` / `current_is_admin()` write gate, mirroring the one already-live precedent for this exact "scope + permission key" shape (the storage-bucket write policy), rather than the `promotion_history` pattern's plain `[STD]`-only shape (`promotion_history` has no permission-gated write, only an unenforced `decided_by` audit column — confirmed by re-reading its actual live policy text before assuming it was the right template). `academic_standing_events` deliberately has no delete policy, matching `promotion_history`'s own audit-trail discipline (create/update only, never delete).
- Migration run against the real DB and independently verified via `pg_policies`: 7 policies live across both tables (4 on `academic_standing_rules` — select/insert/update/delete; 3 on `academic_standing_events` — select/insert/update, no delete), all named and scoped correctly.
- `academics.academic_standing.review` permission key inserted into the live catalog (`insert ... on conflict (key) do nothing`, with the required `module` column — a second real mistake caught before running, the first insert attempt omitted `module`, a `not null` column, which would have failed outright).
- `settingsService.ts`: `AcademicStandingRules` type + `loadAcademicStandingRules(schoolId)`/`upsertAcademicStandingRules(schoolId, rules)`, mirroring `CgpaPolicy`'s exact convention (`{ data: null }` is a normal "not configured" state, not an error; upsert on `school_id`).
- `SettingsModule.tsx`: new `StandingTab`, gated `isHigherInstitutionSchoolType` the same way as Grading/Curriculum/Offerings, given its own tab (not folded into Grading) per explicit owner direction to follow this codebase's existing precedent — Offerings earned its own tab as a distinct object rather than being folded into Curriculum, and Academic Standing is a distinct policy-review concern (triggered off CGPA) rather than part of computing it, so the same split applies. Built as an unfilled single-row form (four optional numeric fields, no smart defaults, Number() coercion deferred to save time), mirroring `GradingTab`'s CGPA-policy form structure exactly. **One real piece of validation added beyond the original card's scope:** the save handler rejects a withdrawal threshold set higher than the probation threshold, since the two would otherwise define a contradictory review order.
- **Typecheck and build genuinely re-verified from scratch, not taken on the strength of an interrupted prior session's own claim of a clean result:** the work resumed from a recovered but unverifiable transcript (a prior session that was lost before being properly recorded). Rather than trust that transcript's account, the actual uploaded files were independently re-checked end to end — grepped directly for the claimed functions/wiring, confirmed genuinely present (and further along than the transcript itself described), then typechecked in isolation with real `@types/react` (not the transcript's abandoned guessed-stub attempt), and finally confirmed against the real project's own `npm run build`, which came back clean.
- **On-device, confirmed by the owner:** real thresholds saved through the new Standing tab, persisted through a UI reload, and independently confirmed via a direct SQL read against `academic_standing_rules` (`probation_cgpa_threshold: 2`, `withdrawal_cgpa_threshold: 1.5`, `max_carryover_courses_before_review: 4`, `max_registration_multiplier: 2`, `updated_at` later than `created_at`, confirming the upsert-on-existing-row path works, not just first insert). One verification-query false alarm along the way ("Success. No rows returned" against a `current_school_id()`-filtered query) was traced to a SQL Editor session-context mismatch, not a data or code problem — resolved by re-querying without the session-dependent filter and reading `school_id` directly.

#### SESSION 62pre — Auto-flag/un-flag carryover on score finalize (owner-directed follow-up, prerequisite for SESSION 62's evaluation flow) — ✅ Complete, 2026-07-16
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7 (fills a real gap between Phase 4 scoring and Phase 2 registration — SESSION 62's evaluation flow *counts* outstanding carryovers, but nothing previously *created* the `course_registrations.status = 'carryover'` flag it and `loadOutstandingCarryover()` (SESSION 57) both depend on)
**Touches:** `migration_session_62pre_carryover_flip.sql` (new `grade_scales.is_passing`/`course_results.is_passing` columns, `apply_carryover_status()` security-definer function), `gpaService.ts` (`GradeBand.is_passing`/`ScoredAttempt.isPassing`), `courseResultsService.ts` (`saveCourseResult()`/`finalizeWeightedCourseResult()` gain an `isPassing` param and call the new RPC; both `ScoredAttempt[]` construction sites updated), `CourseResultsModule.tsx` (`loadHigherEdGradeScale()`'s local band mapper, both finalize call sites, `recomputeAndCacheGpa()`'s pass/fail read), `settingsService.ts` + `SettingsModule.tsx` (`GradeScaleBand.is_passing`, Grading tab gains a per-band toggle)
**Depends on:** SESSION 60/60a/60c (scoring UI + `saveCourseResult()`), SESSION 57 (`loadOutstandingCarryover()`, the existing consumer this only feeds — unchanged)

**The gap found, precisely:** every worked example and test scenario up to this point (SESSION 57's own Scenario B included) set `course_registrations.status = 'carryover'` by hand via the SQL Editor. Nothing in `CourseResultsModule.tsx`, `courseResultsService.ts`, or `courseRegistrationService.ts` ever flipped that status from the app itself when a lecturer finalized a failing score. Researched against real institutional practice (UTS, Melbourne, APU) before designing a fix: the industry pattern is a deterministic status flip on grade finalization, with human review reserved for the *consequences* of a fail (probation, exclusion), not for the fail record itself — confirming this system's existing split (SESSION 62's `academic_standing_events` review queue) already anticipated the right architecture; it just had no trigger feeding it.

**Where the logic lives, and why:** a single new `apply_carryover_status(p_registration_id uuid, p_is_passing boolean)` security-definer function, called from inside `saveCourseResult()` — confirmed as the one real write path both `finalizeScore()` and `finalizeWeightedScore()` call through (`finalizeWeightedCourseResult()` computes a merged score and delegates to `saveCourseResult()`, it doesn't duplicate the write). One implementation, both finalize flows covered, matching this codebase's existing "one implementation, not two" discipline (`gpaService.ts`, `resolveCourseResultComponents()`). A security-definer function was necessary, not optional: `course_registrations`' staff-write RLS policy is gated on `academics.course_registration.manage`, a permission no lecturer role holds (confirmed live) — a plain client-side update from `saveCourseResult()` would match zero rows under RLS and silently do nothing. Follows the exact convention already established by `current_has_permission()`/`current_is_admin()` (§3.2): elevated privilege internally, callable by any authenticated staff session, scoped tightly (one named registration row, explicit school-membership check, only the `status` column touched, never `is_carryover`/`original_registration_id`, which describe the *next* registration cycle and remain SESSION 57's job unchanged). A `withdrawn` row is never touched either direction. Idempotent by design — re-finalizing a corrected score (fail→pass or pass→fail) re-derives status on every call rather than one-way-triggering, so fixing a mistaken fail doesn't leave a phantom carryover behind.

**A real design correction, made before first real use, not after:** the first version of this function inferred "failing" as `grade_point <= 0`. The owner tested this on-device — it worked for a school's `0–49` band configured at `grade_point = 0`, then deliberately reconfigured that same band to `grade_point = 0` again to confirm the flip, and separately asked whether an institution could ever want a band *above* zero to still count as carryover-triggering. Researched directly (UNC Law's published scale, which runs bands below its passing line at nonzero grade points; general findings that institutions calibrate the pass/fail line as an independent policy decision, not inferable from "whichever band sorts lowest") — confirmed `grade_point <= 0` was a narrower rule than real institutions use, exactly the kind of quiet miscalibration this PRD has flagged before (see `grade_scales`' own header note on `is_passing`). Fixed **before** this shipped anywhere: added `is_passing boolean`, explicit per band, to `grade_scales` (backfilled `grade_point > 0` as a starting guess for existing rows, reviewable per-band afterward — see the new Grading tab toggle below) and to `course_results` (persisted once at finalize time, alongside `grade_point`, for the same "a finalized result shouldn't silently drift if the scale is edited later" reason `grade_point` itself is already persisted rather than re-derived; left `null` for any row finalized before this migration, since no honest backfill value exists for those). `apply_carryover_status()` now takes `p_is_passing` as a direct boolean param from the caller, which already resolved the real band via `findGradeBand()`, rather than inferring anything from the numeric grade point itself.

**A second, independent gap the `npm run build` catch surfaced, not part of the original plan:** `courseResultsService.ts` had a second, separate `ScoredAttempt[]` construction site — the admin-view live CGPA computation for `StudentsModule.tsx`'s `includeUnpublished=true` branch — that TypeScript correctly flagged as missing the new required `isPassing` field. Investigating why revealed this site had its own independently-hardcoded `isPassing = (a) => a.gradePoint > 0`, the exact stale convention this whole session was fixing everywhere else. Not merely a type fix: the shared `course_results` select (used by both the admin and guardian query branches) gained `is_passing`, the local `CourseRow` type and its mapping carry it through, and the live computation now reads the real persisted field — with the identical pre-migration null-fallback (`grade_point > 0`) used at the finalize-time site, so the admin-facing live number and the guardian-facing cached number can never disagree over how an old, pre-migration row is treated.

**A real migration-application error, caught and fixed:** re-running the corrected migration on top of an already-applied first version (`p_is_failing` signature) failed with Postgres error `42P13: cannot change name of input parameter "p_is_failing"` — `create or replace function` preserves a function's identity by name **and argument types**, and refuses to silently rename a parameter under an unchanged type signature (`uuid, boolean` in both versions). Fixed by adding `drop function if exists apply_carryover_status(uuid, boolean)` immediately before the `create or replace`, confirmed idempotent for repeat application (`if exists` makes it a no-op once the `p_is_passing` version is already live) and safe within the single-transaction batch a manual SQL Editor run applies this file as.

**Settings UI, not left SQL-only:** `GradeScaleBand` (`settingsService.ts`) gained `is_passing: boolean | null`; `loadHigherEdGradeScale()`/`upsertHigherEdGradeBand()` read/write it (required, not optional, on every save from this form — no half-configured band going forward). `SettingsModule.tsx`'s Grading tab reuses the existing `Toggle` component as-is (no new UI pattern invented), labeled "Counts as passing" with an inline hint distinguishing this from grade-point value, pre-filling correctly on edit (falling back to the same `grade_point > 0` guess only for a legacy row the migration's backfill never set), and showing "· Passing" / "· Fails (carryover)" directly on each band's list row so a full scale is reviewable at a glance without opening every band's edit form individually.

**✅ Done Check — CONFIRMED 2026-07-16.** `npm run build` clean (after both the initial `isPassing` gap and the second admin-view construction site were fixed). Migration applied successfully (after the drop-before-recreate fix). On-device testing confirmed clean by the owner, including re-testing the exact case that motivated the `is_passing` redesign — a band with a positive `grade_point` still correctly flips a registration to `carryover`. **Known, not yet built:** SESSION 62's own evaluation flow (reading outstanding carryovers + CGPA against `academic_standing_rules` to populate `academic_standing_events`) is unaffected and still next — this session only ensures the flag it will count is now set correctly in the first place, matching real institutional pass/fail calibration rather than a fixed zero cutoff.

#### SESSION 62 — Session-end evaluation flow + admin review queue (probation/spillover, always human-reviewed) — ⚠️ Partial, 2026-07-16 (code written and `npm run build` confirmed clean — see row 63 in §11 for the full account; **on-device testing explicitly deferred, not yet run** — the two worked test cases in this card's own Done Check remain open)
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7
**Touches:** new evaluation function (service-layer, triggered on-demand at session-end, not a cron job — no scheduled-job infrastructure exists anywhere in this app per the precedent already confirmed in Session 10a-3), new admin review-queue UI
**Depends on:** SESSION 61 — ✅ complete as of 2026-07-16, `academic_standing_rules` is live and school-configured; unblocked, ready to start.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Build the session-end evaluation flow: for every student, compute CGPA (reading `student_gpa_snapshots`) and outstanding-carryover count, compare against `academic_standing_rules`.
2. Any student below `probation_cgpa_threshold`, or whose registration count exceeds `max_registration_multiplier`, must generate a review-pending `academic_standing_events` row — never a silent automatic status change. A human (admin/registrar) always reviews and confirms.
3. Build the admin-facing review queue: list pending standing events, allow the admin to confirm (or annotate/override with a note) each one.
4. `npm run build` — clean.
5. On-device, two real worked test cases: one student deliberately below the configured probation threshold generates a review-pending probation event; one student with enough accumulated carryovers to exceed the configured residency multiplier generates a review-pending spillover event. Admin reviews and confirms both.

**✅ Done Check:** Both worked test cases produce correct, review-pending (not silently auto-applied) events, and the resulting `academic_standing_events` audit trail is visible and correct for both students after admin confirmation — closes Phase 5's Definition of Done.

### TIER 8 continued — Problem 8.7a: the operational gap (owner walkthrough, 2026-07-17)

**Grouping rationale, stated once here rather than repeated on every card:** the 11 items raised map to three dependency groups, not one flat list. **Group A (SESSIONS 64–65)** fixes the student identity/status model and builds the one real admission workflow everything downstream needs — nothing else can be correctly built while a higher-ed student's "current state" is ambiguous between two disconnected systems. **Group B (SESSIONS 66–69)** is academic operations that each depend on Group A's placement model being the single source of truth, plus — for timetable and self-registration specifically — real semester terms existing first. **Group C (SESSION 70)** is finance: independent of Groups A/B technically, but sequenced last because fee-gating (item 11) needs a real fee-status field to check, which the payment/receipt work in the same group must produce first.

#### SESSION 64 — Reconcile the student status/placement model; make `student_programme_placements` the real source of truth for higher-ed students
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7a, item 2
**Touches:** `students` (possible additive migration — see step 1), `studentsService.ts`, `StudentsModule.tsx`, `settingsService.ts` (existing `student_programme_placements` functions)
**Depends on:** none — this is the root of the whole group, unblocked today

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Confirm exactly what `students.status` (`active`/`inactive`/`alumni`) is meant to mean for a higher-ed student versus a secondary one — do not assume they're the same lifecycle. A secondary student's status and class are independent; a higher-ed student's "active" status arguably *should* derive from having a current `student_programme_placements` row, and "alumni" from graduation, not from separate manual toggles that can drift out of sync with placement history. Get this reconciliation decision from the owner and record it in master-plan §12 before writing any code — this is a real design decision, not a technical one.
2. Audit every place `StudentsModule.tsx`/`studentsService.ts` reads or writes `class_node_id` or `status` and determine, per call site, whether it needs a higher-ed-aware branch (reading `student_programme_placements`/`programme_level_id` instead) or is safely secondary-only and should be explicitly gated out for higher-ed schools — do not silently leave ambiguous call sites unbranched.
3. Surface a student's current programme/level directly in `StudentsModule.tsx`'s own detail view for higher-ed schools (today this only exists inside `InstitutionTab`'s placement action) — a registrar should never need to leave the Students module to see what level a student is in.
4. `npm run build` — clean.
5. On-device: open a real higher-ed student's record; confirm their current level/programme is visible directly on the student record, and confirm changing their placement (via the existing `InstitutionTab` action or wherever step 3 lands it) is reflected immediately in that same view.

**✅ Done Check:** A higher-ed student's current programme/level and status are visible and correct from one place in `StudentsModule.tsx`, with no reliance on cross-referencing `InstitutionTab` separately, confirmed on-device with a real student.

**✅ Complete, 2026-07-17.** §12 decisions recorded before any code (active = derived from placement, alumni = final-level completion deferred to SESSION 69, inactive left an open flagged gap). Every call site audited and branched or explicitly gated, not left ambiguous — including two owner-reported list-page bugs (class-picker/"No class assigned" persisting for higher-ed) and a full Brief-tab research pass (attendance/performance relevance determined from the real schema and courseAttendanceService.ts, not assumed) that replaced two previously-wrong or stubbed stats with real, working ones. Full breakdown in PRD Log row 64. `npm run build` and on-device testing both confirmed clean by the owner.

#### SESSION 65 — Admission screen with matriculation-number generation
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7a, items 1 and 7
**Touches:** `students` (new columns — see step 1), `settingsService.ts` (new `admitStudent()`/matric-generation function), new or extended admin UI (likely `InstitutionTab` or a new `AdmissionTab`)
**Depends on:** SESSION 64 (placement must be the confirmed source of truth before an admission flow writes to it)

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Design the matric-number format as a real, school-configurable pattern — this school's one existing hand-assigned number (`MED/NSC/26/001`, from SESSION 55) suggests a `{programme}/{year}/{sequence}` shape, but confirm with the owner whether this is fixed or needs to vary per school (§7.27.1's own header note already flags numbering conventions as genuinely varying by institution — do not hardcode one format as if it were universal). Get this confirmed and recorded in §12 before migrating.
2. Design the sequence-generation mechanism itself (a per-programme-per-year counter table, or a `max(sequence) + 1` derived read) — reason about race conditions if two students could be admitted concurrently (unlikely at this school's real scale, but state the assumption rather than silently ignoring it).
3. Build one real admission screen: search/select or create a student record, select programme + level + session, generate and assign the matric number, and create the `student_programme_placements` row — all in one atomic flow, not the current two-separate-manual-steps process.
4. `npm run build` — clean.
5. On-device: admit a second real student through the new screen; confirm a correctly formatted, unique matric number is generated and the placement row is created correctly, both confirmed via direct SQL read.

**✅ Done Check:** A real student is admitted end-to-end through one screen — matric number generated (not hand-typed), placement created — confirmed via SQL read of both `students.matric_number` and `student_programme_placements`.

#### SESSION 65a — Correction: admission-applications schema and pipeline engine
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7a, items 1 and 7 (correction, owner walkthrough 2026-07-17, post-SESSION-65-ship)
**Supersedes:** the "search-or-create student" step of SESSION 65's shipped admission screen. That step created a real `students` row at the point of admission — before an applicant had actually been admitted — which is the direct cause of two owner-reported defects: matric numbers generating without a programme code (the created student had no real programme/department linkage yet), and a cramped single-screen UI trying to do search, create, format-config, and generation all at once. SESSION 65's own atomic `admit_student()` logic is **not** superseded — it's reused unchanged by `admit_from_application()` below.
**Depends on:** SESSION 65 (this *is* SESSION 65's own corrected scope, not new ground)
**Design reference:** `ADMISSION_REDESIGN_PROPOSAL.md` — full research trail, schema, and all five owner decisions recorded there and in this session's §12 rows (below)

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Record all five owner decisions into master-plan §12 before migrating (done — see §12 rows for SESSION 65a, this update).
2. Migrate `admission_applications` (§3.1 of the proposal) — the applicant record, independent of `students` until admitted. Real columns for JAMB/O'Level/Direct-Entry data (not one JSON blob — these need to be filterable/reportable), `custom_field_values jsonb` as the genuine extension point, `decision_programme_id`/`decision_level_id` separate from the applied-for `programme_id`/`entry_level_id` (implements the owner's "offer into a different programme/level" requirement), and the retention columns (`rejected_at`/`purge_at`) for §6's NDPA-driven purge.
3. Migrate `admission_field_settings` (§3.2) — one row per school, enable/required per field, plus a `custom_fields` extension array.
4. Migrate `admission_pipelines` + `admission_pipeline_stages` (§4.2, as revised) — pipelines are authored objects a school can create more than one of, matched to applicants via an `applies_to` rule (`entry_mode`/`programme_id`) or a default fallback. Each pipeline's stage rows carry component-level toggles (real, researched components per stage — §4.1 of the proposal), not one on/off switch per stage. **No `sequence` column** — stage order is fixed (Application → Screening → Decision → Clearance) per owner decision §10.5, encoded once in the stage-advance function, not authored per pipeline.
5. Build the stage-advance function: validates that an application can only move to the next *enabled* stage in the fixed order (skipping any stage type with no row for that pipeline), never out of order, never into a stage with zero components (owner decision §10.4 — enforce at the DB level, not just the settings form).
6. Build `admit_from_application(p_application_id uuid)`: creates the real `students` row (the *only* place this happens for a pipeline-enabled school), then calls the existing, unmodified atomic matric-number-generation + placement logic from SESSION 65's `admit_student()`, using `decision_programme_id`/`decision_level_id`. Sets `admission_applications.stage = 'admitted'` and `admitted_student_id`.
7. Add `schools.admission_pipeline_enabled boolean not null default false` (§5) — the master switch. Block `createStudent()`'s direct-add insert path (RLS or function-level check) whenever this is `true` for the school, per the owner's explicit "no direct student add once enabled" requirement. Default `false` means zero behavior change for every school without this infrastructure.
8. Set up the scheduled purge job (§6, owner decision §10.2): `pg_cron`, confirmed available on all Supabase tiers including free — hard-deletes any `admission_applications` row where `purge_at < now()`. Note in the migration's own comment: free-tier projects auto-pause after 7 days idle, silently pausing the cron too; worth a `cron.job_run_details` spot-check post-launch.
9. `npm run build` — clean.
10. On-device: create a real application, walk it through a pipeline with at least Screening and Decision enabled (including a programme/level override on decision), admit it, and confirm via SQL that `students`/`student_programme_placements`/`matric_number` all land correctly and `admission_applications.admitted_student_id` links back. Separately confirm a direct `createStudent()` call is rejected once `admission_pipeline_enabled = true`.

**✅ Done Check:** A real applicant is walked end-to-end — application → screening → decision (with a programme/level override) → clearance → admitted — with a correctly formatted matric number generated only at the point of real admission, confirmed via SQL. A direct student-add attempt is confirmed blocked once the school's pipeline is enabled.

#### SESSION 65b — Correction: rebuild AdmissionModule.tsx as a multi-tab operational screen
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7a, items 1 and 7 (correction, owner walkthrough 2026-07-17)
**Supersedes:** the single-screen `AdmissionModule.tsx` shipped in SESSION 65 (search-or-create-student + matric-format-builder crammed into one view — the owner's own "hard to navigate," "not intuitive," "occupied all the spaces" feedback). The nav entry and route wiring done for SESSION 65's `AdmissionModule.tsx` (`App.tsx`) stay in place; only the module's internal content is rebuilt.
**Depends on:** SESSION 65a (needs `admission_applications`/pipelines to exist and be readable)
**Design reference:** `ADMISSION_REDESIGN_PROPOSAL.md` §7

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Rebuild `AdmissionModule.tsx` as a tab bar computed from the **union of stages actually used across the school's active pipelines** — not a fixed four tabs. A school running an all-off pipeline sees a lean module; a school using every stage across two pipelines sees the full set. This is the direct fix for "the format builder occupied all the spaces."
2. **Applications tab** (always present): list of applicants at `stage = 'application'`, with a "+ New application" form built from `admission_field_settings` (not hardcoded field lists). This tab does not touch `students` at all — replaces "search-or-create student" entirely. Pipeline assignment is automatic (§4.2's matching rule), shown read-only, not staff-picked.
3. **Screening tab** (rendered only if ≥1 active pipeline enables it): list at `stage = 'screening'`, showing only the components that specific applicant's own pipeline enabled.
4. **Decision tab** (rendered only if ≥1 active pipeline enables it, which may be none): offer/reject/defer, programme/level override, decision note — whichever components are on for that applicant's pipeline.
5. **Clearance tab** (rendered only if ≥1 active pipeline enables it): whichever clearance components are on; completing all required ones for an applicant triggers `admit_from_application()`.
6. **Admitted/Rejected view** (always present): outcome list — admitted applicants show the generated matric number (reusing SESSION 65's existing result display verbatim); rejected applicants show their purge countdown (`purge_at`).
7. Remove the matric-number-format builder from this module entirely — it moves to Settings (SESSION 65c). This module no longer references `matric_number_formats` directly at all except read-only, to display the generated number after a real admission.
8. `npm run build` — clean.
9. On-device: confirm the tab bar correctly reflects a school's actual pipeline configuration (add a second pipeline with a different stage selection, confirm the tab bar updates accordingly); walk one real applicant through every tab to admission.

**✅ Done Check:** The admission module shows only the stages a school's pipelines actually use, an application can be created without touching `students`, and a full application-to-admission walkthrough works on-device with the tab bar correctly reflecting pipeline configuration.

#### SESSION 65c — Correction: Admission Settings tab in SettingsModule.tsx
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7a, items 1 and 7 (correction, owner walkthrough 2026-07-17)
**Supersedes:** the matric-format builder previously living inside `AdmissionModule.tsx`/`InstitutionTab` — relocated here, not rebuilt from scratch (SESSION 65's versioned/soft-locked format engine, §12 SESSION 65 correction row, stays exactly as designed).
**Depends on:** SESSION 65a (needs `admission_pipelines`/`admission_field_settings` tables to exist)
**Design reference:** `ADMISSION_REDESIGN_PROPOSAL.md` §8

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Add a new "Admission" tab in `SettingsModule.tsx`, matching this file's existing tab-bar convention.
2. **Master switch:** `admission_pipeline_enabled` toggle, with a real confirmation step on enabling (not a casual toggle) — turning it on changes how every future student is onboarded from that point forward, matching the same "soft-lock in the UI, hard rule in the database" pattern already established for the matric-format lock.
3. **Pipelines:** list-and-edit UI matching this file's own `matric_number_formats`/curriculum add-edit-remove conventions. Add/rename/deactivate a pipeline; set its matching rule (`entry_mode`/`programme_id`, or mark it default fallback). Per pipeline: a checklist of the three stages, each revealing its own component sub-checklist when checked on (owner decision §10.4 — UI must not allow saving a stage with zero components checked).
4. **Application fields:** enable/required checklist for every JAMB/O'Level/Direct-Entry field (`admission_field_settings.field_config`), plus a custom-field builder (`custom_fields`). One shared, school-wide form (owner decision §10.3) — no per-pipeline duplication.

6. **Rejection retention window:** `rejection_retention_days` setting (default 90, editable), feeding the SESSION 65a purge job.
7. `npm run build` — clean.
8. On-device: author a second pipeline with a different stage/component selection than the first, confirm both are correctly matched against new applications per their `applies_to` rules; confirm the master-switch confirmation step actually blocks a casual accidental toggle.

**✅ Done Check:** A school can author at least two distinct pipelines with different stage/component selections through this tab, both correctly applied to matching applications, and the master switch requires explicit confirmation to enable — all confirmed on-device.

#### SESSION 65e — Owner audit: wire up every Admission Settings component, close a real Decision-stage deadlock, make the master switch actually gate the module
**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7a, owner audit of the shipped 65a/65b/65c work (2026-07-18)
**Touches:** `AdmissionModule.tsx`, `admissionService.ts`, new migration `migration_session_65e_decision_gate_and_revert.sql`
**Depends on:** SESSION 65a/65b/65c (all already shipped)

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Audit every component key `SettingsModule.tsx`'s `PIPELINE_STAGE_TYPES` lets a school enable (screening: `document_review`/`jamb_verification`/`olevel_verification`/`interview`/`aptitude_test`; decision: `committee_review`/`programme_confirmation`; clearance: `fee_payment`/`medical_clearance`/`document_submission`) against what `AdmissionModule.tsx` actually renders per stage — close every gap found, not just the ones initially reported.
2. Fix any key-name mismatch found between the two files (Settings authors against one vocabulary; the operational screen must read the same one, not an earlier or invented one).
3. Close the real on-device deadlock: an application that reaches Clearance with no programme/level ever set (neither the original application nor a Decision-stage override) can currently neither advance (correctly refused at `admit_from_application()`) nor go back (no reverse-stage function exists). Add a hard DB block on leaving Decision without a resolvable programme/level, and a new one-stage-at-a-time revert function, surfaced in the UI only when an applicant is genuinely stuck (missing what's needed at Decision, or an unfinished required checklist item at Clearance) — not as a general always-available control.
4. Wire `AdmissionModule.tsx` to the `admission_pipeline_enabled` master switch, which it currently has no awareness of at all — render the module blank with a clear note when off, matching the DB-side direct-add lock this switch already, correctly, enforces on the Students module.
5. `npm run build` — clean.
6. On-device: confirm every previously-invisible component now renders and can be toggled/filled per stage; confirm an application deliberately left without a programme/level is hard-blocked from leaving Decision with a clear error, and can be moved back to Screening instead; confirm switching the master toggle off blanks this module with a note, and back on restores it.

**Explicitly out of scope, logged as SESSION 65f below, not silently folded in:** an admission-window/scheduler concept (blur tabs when the switch is on but no admission period is currently active, show upcoming dates, optionally per-stage windows) — raised in the same audit, but real net-new schema and UI, not a fix to what's already there.

**✅ Done Check:** Every Settings-configured admission component is visible and usable in the operational screen; an application stuck at Clearance with no programme/level can be moved back to Screening and re-progressed correctly; the master switch genuinely gates the whole module — all confirmed on-device.

4. `npm run build` — clean.

#### SESSION 68a — Programme/level on course offerings; cohort-clash detection for the higher-ed timetable
**PRD ref:** none — this card did not exist before this entry. Raised directly by the owner during SESSION 68's own close-out (see `migration_session_69.sql`'s own header comment, written before this correction, for the contemporaneous record of exactly when and how this was raised), after SESSION 68 above was already delivered and confirmed done. Not part of Tier 8 / Problem 8.7a's original item list — a real gap found through building SESSION 68, not a pre-planned card.
**Touches:** `course_offerings` (two new nullable columns, `migration_session_69.sql`), `OfferingsTab` in `SettingsModule.tsx` (new Programme → Level picker, curriculum-filtered course dropdown, list regrouping), `courseOfferingTimetableService.ts` (`findCohortConflicts()`/`cohortConflictMessage()`, exported `rangesOverlap()`), `TimetableModuleHigherEd.tsx` (pre-save conflict check), `courseAttendanceService.ts` (type-parity fix for `loadCourseOfferingsForStaff()`)
**Depends on:** SESSION 68 (this card only makes sense once a real course-offering timetable exists to check clashes against)

**Naming note, so this is never re-confused the way it was the first time:** this work was informally labeled "Session 69" in working files and a since-recovered transcript, purely because it was built immediately after Session 68's own delivery. It is **not** the real, separate, numbered PRD SESSION 69 (level-to-level promotion + student self-service registration, below), which has its own distinct scope, its own security-sensitive RLS policy, and has not been started. This card exists specifically to remove that collision going forward.

**Status: ✅ Confirmed, 2026-07-18 — see PRD Log row 68a for the full account**, including every design decision (why nullable-no-backfill, why programme/level are NOT derived live from `programme_curriculum`, the `(course_id, programme_level_id, term_id)` fan-out-safe scoping rule, why `unknown` is a genuine third severity rather than defaulted to `elective`), the real `courseAttendanceService.ts` type-parity build break found and fixed along the way, and exactly what has and hasn't been independently on-device confirmed by the owner as of this entry.

**What was actually done:**
1. Migration: `course_offerings.programme_id`/`programme_level_id`, both nullable, no backfill — an offering predating this work and a genuinely shared/university-wide offering are both real permanent null states, not a gap.
2. `OfferingsTab`'s form: Programme → Level picker above Course, curriculum-filtered course dropdown with an explicit "show all courses" override, save-time soft warning (confirm-to-proceed) if a course is picked off-curriculum.
3. Offerings list regrouped: flat list → Programme → Level → "Unassigned" (three real states: no programme at all; a programme but an unresolvable level; both fully assigned).
4. Cohort-clash detection: `findCohortConflicts()` (three severities — `core`/`elective`/`unknown`, all soft, never a hard block) wired into `TimetableModuleHigherEd.tsx`'s slot-save path alongside a newly-added pre-save lecturer-clash check, combined into one `confirm()` dialog per save.
5. `courseAttendanceService.ts` type-parity fix so `loadOfferingsForLecturer()` actually typechecks and a lecturer's offering list carries the two new fields.

**Not yet independently on-device confirmed by the owner as of this entry (tracked forward, not silently rounded in):**
- The offerings-list regrouping display itself.
- The cohort-clash confirm dialog actually firing for a real overlapping pair — this is conditional by design (only appears when a save genuinely clashes against an already-loaded entry), so a school with no current scheduling clashes correctly sees nothing different on the timetable page itself, which is not the same as the feature being unverified.

**✅ Done Check (not yet observed on-device, do not treat as met from this card alone):** a real programme/level assignment on a real offering is visible in the regrouped list under the correct Programme → Level bucket; a real pair of overlapping same-cohort offerings triggers the cohort-clash confirm dialog with correctly-resolved severity wording, confirmed on-device, not just by isolated typecheck.

#### SESSION 69pre — Fix two live term-scoping bugs found by SESSION 67's step-3 audit, before SESSION 69 begins
**PRD ref:** none — this card did not exist before this entry. Not part of Tier 8.7a's original item list. Raised by SESSION 67's own card, step 3 ("audit every higher-ed table/query...to confirm they already correctly branch on `term_id`"), which was finally run this session after being left undone since SESSION 67 itself.
**Touches:** `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx` (`RegistrationStep`'s `terms`-loading race), `SettingsModule.tsx`'s `CurriculumTab` (`handleAddEntry()`'s missing term validation)
**Depends on:** SESSION 67 (real terms must exist to audit against — they do, confirmed live)
**Blocks:** SESSION 69 — building student self-service registration on top of a registration save path that's *currently* saving `term_id: null` for every real registration would mean the new self-service flow inherits the exact same bug on day one, silently.

**Status: ✅ Closed, 2026-07-19, at the start of SESSION 69.** Bug 1 was traced against the real uploaded `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx` and found **not reproducible** — `loadTerms()` is awaited inside the same mount effect before `loading` clears, so `terms` is always settled before `handleSave`/`readyToShowCurriculum` are reachable; the race this card describes does not exist in the code as currently written (a genuine "the audit's data-pattern inference and the real code disagree" finding). Bug 2 was confirmed still present and fixed with one line matching `CurriculumTab`'s existing `addError` pattern, confirmed via SQL (`0` new null-`term_id` curriculum entries after a real save). See PRD Log row 69 for the full account.

**Exactly what was found, both against real data (`session67_step3_audit.sql`, scoped to the real school, `887be9b5-a17b-4952-a043-1672db1f049c`) and traced to a root cause in the real code, not inferred from the data alone:**

1. **`course_registrations` — a real, currently-live race condition, not a validation gap.** All 32 real registration rows have `term_id = null`, and all 32 were created on or after the first real term's start date (2026-07-13) — none are pre-terms backfill. `RegistrationStep` (`CourseRegistrationModule.tsx`) initializes `terms` as `[]` and populates it asynchronously via `useEffect`; `readyToShowCurriculum` (`terms.length === 0 || !!selectedTermId`) evaluates `true` on the very first render, before the terms fetch resolves, making the course-selection UI available and save-able before the component knows this school has real terms. `handleSave()`'s `term_id: terms.length > 0 ? selectedTermId : null` then correctly reads `terms.length` as still `0` at that exact moment and silently saves `null`, even though real terms exist and would populate a moment later. Real data supports this trace: several rows share millisecond-identical `created_at` timestamps (multiple courses saved together), consistent with a normal multi-course registration happening right at page load, not a bulk import or unusual edge case.
2. **`programme_curriculum` — a real, currently-live missing-validation gap, a different root cause from bug 1 despite an identical symptom.** Of 13 real entries for the real school (the initial audit query returned 27 unscoped — a scoping mistake, caught and corrected mid-audit, where 14 rows belonging to other schools were being counted), 9 are null and all 9 were created on or after 2026-07-13 — also not backfill. `CurriculumTab`'s (`SettingsModule.tsx`) Semester `<select>` defaults to no selection, and `handleAddEntry()` never validates that a term was actually chosen once `terms.length > 0` before saving — no race condition here, just a straightforwardly missing check.
3. **`student_gpa_snapshots` — checked, genuinely undetermined, not counted as a third bug.** All 10 real rows are null, all on/after 2026-07-13. Consistent with the established "running CGPA is deliberately `term_id = null`" convention (§7.27.4, SESSION 62pre) — but equally consistent with "no per-term GPA computation has run yet for this school." Cannot be resolved from data alone; not fixed or further investigated this session.
4. **`course_offerings` — checked, confirmed NOT a bug.** 9 of 10 real rows null, owner-confirmed as genuine pre-terms backfill (created before SESSION 67's data step). The 1 non-null row is correctly scoped to the real First Semester term.

**Why this wasn't fixed immediately, on the record:** the owner explicitly chose to document rather than fix in the same pass this was found (see PRD Log row 67's second update). This card exists so that decision doesn't quietly disappear — SESSION 69 (self-registration) is a new write path into the *same* `course_registrations` table bug 1 lives in, and building it without first fixing (or at minimum, explicitly re-confirming a deliberate decision not to fix) bug 1 would mean the new feature inherits a known, already-diagnosed defect on day one.

**Exactly what to do, when this card is picked up:**
1. `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx`: fix the race — either gate `handleSave()` itself on `terms.length > 0 && !selectedTermId` returning an explicit error (matching this codebase's own "hard block on a genuine invalid state" precedent, e.g. `loadCheck.overMax` in the same file), or don't render/enable the save action at all until the terms fetch has resolved (a loading-state fix, addressing the race at its actual source rather than only guarding the symptom). Confirm which fits better against this file's own existing loading-state conventions before choosing, rather than defaulting to whichever is less code.
2. `SettingsModule.tsx`'s `CurriculumTab`: add the missing validation to `handleAddEntry()` — require a term selection once `terms.length > 0`, matching the equivalent guard this audit shows is (mostly) already present in `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx`'s intent, just not its execution.
3. Decide what to do with the 32 existing null-term registrations and 9 existing null-term curriculum entries already in the real data — these are now-orphaned rows created after real terms existed but before the fix, a distinct third state from "genuine pre-terms backfill" and "correctly scoped." A silent backfill risks guessing wrong at which term a given row actually belongs to; leaving them null risks the "Unassigned" treatment already established elsewhere in this codebase for exactly this kind of gap (§7.27.2 amendment's own precedent) being the more honest choice — decide explicitly, don't default without deciding.
4. `npm run build` — clean.
5. On-device: re-run `session67_step3_audit.sql`'s blocks 2 and 3 after the fix and confirm zero *new* null-term rows are produced by a normal registration/curriculum-entry save from that point forward.

**✅ Done Check:** A new course registration and a new curriculum entry, each saved through the normal UI with a real term selected, both persist with the correct non-null `term_id` — confirmed via SQL read, not just by the UI appearing to work. SESSION 67's own original Done Check is re-evaluated and can only be marked ✅ once this is true.

**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7a, items 6 and 8

**Depends on:** SESSION 64 (placement as source of truth), SESSION 67 (registration is term-scoped)

**Status: ✅ Confirmed, 2026-07-19 — see PRD Log row 69 for the full account.**

**Mid-session scope amendment, confirmed with the owner (not assumed from this card's own wording alone, since a genuine conflict with a prior recorded decision surfaced):** item 3's "student-facing registration screen" was found, while building it, to directly contradict SESSION 57's own explicit, documented decision that course registration is admin-run only, not student self-service (`CourseRegistrationModule.tsx`'s header comment). Resolved as **both, side by side** — the original admin-gated flow is untouched; a new student self-service path was added as a tab inside `GuardianPortal.tsx` instead, reusing the same `RegistrationStep` logic rather than a second implementation. See `session69_masterplan_amendment_admin_and_self_service.md` and §7.27.2's SESSION 69 amendment below for the full record.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Design the higher-ed promotion mechanism: does it mirror `promotion_rules`/`promotion_history` exactly (a parallel table keyed on `programme_level_id`) or extend the existing tables to support both anchors? Get this reviewed into §7.27 before migrating — this is the same class of decision SESSION 61 made for `academic_standing_events` mirroring `promotion_history`'s audit shape, and should be reasoned through the same way, not assumed identical.
2. Design and add the missing student self-write RLS policy on `course_registrations`, scoped tightly (a student may only insert/update their own `student_id`'s rows, only for course offerings open for registration, only within their own current placement's programme/level/term) — this is a real security surface, review it as carefully as SESSION 62pre's `apply_carryover_status()` security-definer work was reviewed, not a rubber-stamp addition.

Build the student-facing registration screen (likely reusing `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx`'s existing curriculum-resolution logic, `loadCurriculum()`, but called from the student's own portal context rather than an admin's) — respecting the same core/elective/carryover/credit-load-cap rules SESSION 57 already built for the admin-facing flow, not a second reimplementation of that logic.
4. Build the promotion run: admin triggers a level-advancement pass at session end, mirroring the auto-promote-above-cutoff / manual-review-below-cutoff pattern already designed (but not yet built) for secondary school in the paused SESSION 50.
5. `npm run build` — clean.
6. On-device, two real cases: a real student registers themselves for a real course through their own portal login (confirmed the write actually happens as that student, not as an admin on their behalf); a real promotion run advances at least one real student a level, confirmed via SQL read of the resulting placement/promotion-history row.

**✅ Done Check:** A real student, logged in as themselves, registers for a real course with no admin involvement; a real promotion run correctly advances a real student's level — both confirmed via direct SQL read, not just UI appearance.

**PRD ref:** Tier 8 / Problem 8.7a, items 9, 10, and 11
**Touches:** new schema (a fees/invoices/payments concept — needs design; confirm whether this school's existing `payments`/finance tables, built for the secondary-school fee model, can be extended or need a higher-ed-specific parallel, the same "extend vs. parallel" question every other higher-ed table has already had to answer), Remita integration (new Edge Function, following the same pattern `create-staff-account`/other Edge Functions already establish), receipt generation (likely a PDF, following `printStudentIdCards.ts`'s or the transcript PDF's established pattern rather than inventing a new print/export mechanism), new access-gating checks on registration/transcript endpoints
**Depends on:** SESSION 64 (a fee obligation needs to attach to a real student/placement), SESSION 69 (registration is one of the activities item 11 needs to gate — building the gate before the thing it gates exists would be premature)

1. Confirm with the owner exactly what a "fee" is for this school in the higher-ed context — per session? per semester? per course? Flat or programme-dependent? This is a real design decision requiring research into Remita's actual integration model (this needs a dedicated web search into Remita's real API/webhook shape before assuming a design — do not guess at its integration pattern the way `max_registration_multiplier`'s formula was research-grounded rather than assumed).

Build the Remita integration: likely a new Edge Function to initiate a transaction and a webhook/verification endpoint to confirm payment server-side (never trust a client-side "payment succeeded" signal alone — this is a real fraud-surface consideration, treat it with the same rigor as any other RLS/security design in this PRD).

6. `npm run build` — clean.
7. On-device, real test transaction: a real (sandbox/test-mode) Remita payment completes, is verified server-side, a receipt is generated and viewable, and a student's registration or transcript access is confirmed blocked before payment and unblocked immediately after.

#### SESSION 71 — Fix student-self portal RLS gap (Problem 8.9, new — found while attempting SESSION 70's own on-device Done Check)
**PRD ref:** New problem, surfaced 2026-07-19 while attempting to test SESSION 70's Remita "Pay Now" flow on-device as a real student — blocks that Done Check, so sequenced before returning to it.
**Touches:** RLS policies only (`students`, `attendance_records`, `fee_assignments`, `fee_discounts`, `payments`, `scores`) — no application code, no schema shape changes. `GuardianPortal.tsx`/`portalService.ts` themselves need no changes; they were always correctly written against the assumption that RLS would scope reads to the caller, an assumption that was true for guardians and staff but never true for student-self.
**Depends on:** none technically; sequenced ahead of finishing SESSION 70 because SESSION 70's own Done Check requires a real student to load the portal at all.

**Root cause, confirmed via direct SQL this session, not guessed:** every table the student portal reads directly has a real RLS policy for `'guardian'` and `'staff'` but none for `'student'` reading their own row — `pg_policies` read against `students`, `attendance_records`, `fee_assignments`, `fee_discounts`, `payments`, and `scores` (which the `subject_results` view reads from) confirmed this precisely; `course_registrations`/`student_programme_placements` (built later, specifically for higher-ed self-service) already have the correct pattern, confirming this is a leftover gap from the original guardian-first portal design, not a new regression. `subject_results` was checked specifically for a potential RLS-disabled data leak (a `false` `relrowsecurity` reading could mean either "blocked" or "wide open") — confirmed to be a plain view (not a base table, so `relrowsecurity=false` is expected/normal for it) reading from `scores`, and `scores` has the same missing-student-policy gap, not a leak. Full evidence trail recorded in master plan §12.

**Exactly what to do:**
1. Run `session71_migration.sql` — adds `students_self_select`, `attendance_records_self_select`, `fee_assignments_self_select` (includes the `programme_level` branch, since a higher-ed student needs it), `fee_discounts_self_select`, `payments_self_select` (no status filter — a student must see their own `pending` Remita rows too, for SESSION 70's "Awaiting confirmation" banner), and `scores_self_select` (approved-only, mirroring the guardian policy's own gate).
2. Run the migration's own verification query, paste results back, confirm all six new policies are actually present before treating this as done.
3. On-device: log in as the real student (`Isa Hafizu` or any of the other four confirmed real student identities), confirm the portal loads past `bootState === 'error'` into the real summary screen — not just that the error disappears, but that real data (attendance, fees, results if available) actually renders.
4. Specifically confirm the `subject_results` view path works for a real student session, not just a direct `scores` query — the view's `security_invoker` setting was found unset (`reloptions` is `null`) during this session's investigation, which could mean the new `scores_self_select` policy doesn't actually apply when read through the view, depending on the Postgres/Supabase version's default view-ownership behavior. If the Results tab still fails after the migration while `attendance_records`/`fees` succeed, this is the first thing to check — the fix would be `alter view subject_results set (security_invoker = true);` (Postgres 15+/Supabase-current only).
5. `npm run build` — clean (expected to be a no-op here, since no application code changes, but confirm rather than assume).

**✅ Done Check:** A real student, logged in as themselves (not a guardian), opens the portal and it loads successfully — Summary, Attendance, Fees, and Results/Transcript tabs all render real data rather than the error screen — confirmed on-device, not just "the error is gone."

| 5 | Fix onboarding-loop-on-reload bug | ✅ Complete | 2026-06-30 | **Root cause (confirmed via on-device repro + direct SQL, not assumed):** application code was never the actual defect. `OnboardingWizard.tsx`'s original `handleFinish` discarded `completeOnboarding()`'s return value and called `onComplete()` unconditionally — this masked a pre-existing RLS gap on `schools`: the table had only `SELECT` policies (`public can read school routing info`, `school members read own school`) plus the platform-admin `ALL` policy — **no school-admin UPDATE policy existed**, unlike every sibling table (`class_nodes`, `roles`, `sessions`, `staff_roles`, `subjects`, `role_permissions`, `school_public_config` all correctly have a `school admins manage ...` ALL/UPDATE policy scoped to `current_school_id()`). Because Postgres RLS silently returns zero-rows-affected with `error: null` (not a thrown error) when no policy permits the write, `completeOnboarding()` always reported `{ok:true}` even though `onboarding_complete` never actually flipped — confirmed directly: `update schools set onboarding_complete = true` via SQL Editor (bypasses RLS) succeeded immediately, proving the column/table were fine and the gap was specifically RLS. **Fixes applied:** (1) `OnboardingWizard.tsx` — parent `handleFinish` now checks `completeOnboarding()`'s result; only calls `onComplete()` on confirmed success, surfaces a real error and allows retry otherwise; `Step8Staff`'s local `handleFinish` now awaits and resets its `finishing` state correctly instead of leaving the button stuck. (2) `App.tsx` — `onComplete` now re-fetches the school row from the DB instead of optimistically patching local state, so local state can never drift from ground truth. (3) **New RLS policy** (the actual root-cause fix): `create policy "school admins manage own school" on schools for update using (id = current_school_id()) with check (id = current_school_id());` — matches the exact pattern/naming convention of every sibling table's working policy. **On-device Done Check observed:** admin completes wizard, finishes cleanly, reload lands on `home` → `AdminDashboard` reliably (`activeTab` defaults to `'home'` on every fresh mount, confirmed not persisted/stale). Teacher identity (SESSION 3's seeded account) also completes/reloads cleanly, landing on the Tier-4-pending `PlaceholderModule` "Teacher Dashboard — Coming in Phase 6", which is correct/expected since `TeacherDashboard.tsx` is SESSION 14 scope, not a defect. **Flagged for follow-up, not blocking:** every sibling table's working policy (`class_nodes`, `roles`, `sessions`, etc.) checks only `school_id = current_school_id()`, not an admin-role check — meaning any staff member of a school, not just admins, currently has RLS-level write access to these tables; Tier 3's permission system is intended to be the actual gate with RLS as the school-boundary backstop only (§3.2), so this is expected by design, not a new gap, but noting it explicitly per verification-rule discipline rather than silently assuming it's fine. |
| 5b | (follow-up, same day) Re-verify Step 1 (`saveSchoolIdentity`) write now that the `schools` RLS policy exists | ✅ Complete | 2026-06-30 | Re-tested on-device: admin set a theme color + welcome message on Step 1, advanced cleanly with no error. Confirmed via SQL: `theme_color` persisted correctly (`#2563eb`). `logo_url` correctly `null` — Step 1's UI has no logo-upload control, so this is expected, not a defect. Same RLS fix from Session 5 covers this write path since both go through `schools.update(...)`. **Separate bug found and fixed during this same follow-up (not an RLS issue — a different root cause entirely):** Step 8's "Add staff member" was failing with the generic "Could not create staff account" message on every attempt. Edge Function logs (`create-staff-account`) showed a consistent rejection: `PGRST116 — Results contain 13 rows, application/vnd.pgrst.object+json requires 1 row`, from this query inside `supabase/functions/create-staff-account/index.ts`:<br>`callerClient.from("app_identities").select("school_id, actor_type").maybeSingle()`<br>— this query had **no filter at all**, relying on `.maybeSingle()` to coincidentally return exactly one row. RLS scopes `app_identities` reads to everyone in the caller's school, not just the caller's own row, so any school with more than one identity row (i.e. any real school) caused this to throw and reject every caller unconditionally — explains why this matches §11's 2026-06-25 note flagging `create-staff-account` as a prior "claimed-done-but-not-deployed" risk; this time it was deployed, just broken. **A second, more serious latent bug of the same class was found and fixed in the same pass:** the admin-role check immediately after (`callerClient.from("staff_roles").select("roles(is_admin_role)")`) also had no filter — it checked "does ANY staff member at this school hold an admin role" rather than "does THIS caller hold one," meaning **any non-admin teacher could have created new staff accounts** at any school that has at least one admin (i.e. every real school) — a genuine authorization gap, not just a crash, that hadn't yet been triggered/noticed. **Fix:** added `callerClient.auth.getUser()` to explicitly resolve and validate the caller's own auth user id (the function never verified the token resolved to a real user before this), then filtered both queries correctly — `app_identities` by `auth_user_id`, `staff_roles` by the caller's own `staff_id` (via `app_identities.actor_record_id`). (3) Owner reported "Record payment" was undiscoverable, buried behind first expanding "Payment history" (two clicks to reach a primary action) — confirmed via direct code read; moved the trigger and form to sit directly on the payslip row, independent of history expansion, which remains a separate collapsed-by-default disclosure. (4) Owner reported the payroll run only offered a single bulk "Mark paid" button with no per-staff control — reconciled two previously-overlapping payment concepts (`markPayrollRunPaid`'s blind `paid_at` stamp vs. `recordPayslipPayment`'s real per-staff partial-payment tracking) per owner's explicit decision: removed the bulk-stamp behavior entirely; `markPayrollRunPaid()` now only flips the run's status, and only once every payslip in it already independently shows `payment_status = 'paid'` via `recordPayslipPayment()`, otherwise returns a specific count of what's still outstanding. `payslips.paid_at` column is now dead (nothing writes it) — `Payslip.paid_at` in the type layer was redefined to mean "most recent `payslip_payments.paid_at`," derived at read time in `loadPayslipsForRun()`, not the stale column. **Prior closure history (2026-07-08), carried forward for full context, not superseded:**

**Closed by explicit owner sign-off on scope actually delivered — not by this row's own original Done Check being observed.** That Done Check (two schools set to two different theme colors, visibly and consistently different across all ten modules, side-by-side screenshot review, on-device) was never attempted this arc; the owner reviewed what was actually built, ran `npm run build` clean on the final state, and explicitly said "I'm satisfied with this session's scope. Close it." — recorded here as exactly that, not conflated with the stricter on-device visual confirmation this row was originally written to require. **Full scope actually delivered, beyond what the two earlier passes in this same row's Notes column cover:** a systematic sweep of all ten CSS prefix blocks (`dash-`, `tt-`, `att-`, `res-`, `stu-`, `fin-`, `hr-`, `lib-`, `ann-`, `port-`, plus `acctprefs-`) for brand-color contrast bugs, in both directions. **Direction 1 — brand color as background, fixed white text:** found 24 real sites (`.tt-toggle-btn--active`, `.tt-retry-btn`, `.tt-edit-btn`/`.tt-edit-start-btn`, `.fin-add-btn`/`.fin-save-btn`/`.fin-record-payment-btn`, `.hr-staff-row-avatar`/`.hr-add-btn`/`.hr-run-approve-btn`/`.hr-save-btn`/`.hr-detail-avatar`, `.lib-tab-btn--active`/`.lib-retry-btn`/`.lib-btn--primary`/`.lib-toggle-btn--active`/`.lib-tag-chip--active`/`.lib-resource-open-btn`, `.ann-compose-btn`/`.ann-save-btn`, `.port-sched-today-chip`, `.acctprefs-save-btn`) where `background: var(--color-brand-600, ...)` paired with a hardcoded `color: #fff`/`#ffffff` — the exact same bug already fixed once for `LoginPage.tsx` earlier this row, just never propagated to the stylesheet's own component rules. All 24 fixed to `color: var(--color-brand-on, #ffffff)`, preserving the old white as fallback. Verified via a clean 24-hunk diff (nothing else touched) and a fresh PostCSS parse (2253 nodes, same as before the fix). Semantic-status colors (present/absent/suspended/warning/approved chips etc.) and white text on fixed non-brand tokens (`--color-primary`, `--color-danger`) were checked and correctly left alone — verified these are role-appropriate, not a missed instance of the same bug. **Direction 2 — brand color as text/border, checked but found already safe, not fixed further:** 51 sites use `--color-brand-600` (or 700/800 shades) as text color, all sitting on white or light brand-tint backgrounds (`--color-surface`, `--color-brand-50`/`--color-brand-subtle`) — confirmed these are already protected by `deriveBrandScale()`'s existing legibility guard, since that guard darkens `--color-brand-600` against pure white *before* exposing it to any consuming rule, and every background here is white or lighter. Border/focus-ring uses of `--color-brand-600` were checked against WCAG's looser 3:1 non-text minimum by reproducing the exact contrast math in an isolated script across 7 realistic test colors (including pale ones that trigger the darkening guard) — confirmed the guard's 4.5:1 text threshold structurally satisfies the looser 3:1 border threshold in every case, so no separate border-contrast gap exists. Zero cases found of fixed non-brand hex text sitting on a brand-scale background. **One structural finding surfaced, not resolved — flagged for the owner, not decided unilaterally:** `.tt-edit-btn`/`.tt-edit-start-btn` exist as an exact byte-for-byte duplicate rule block at two separate locations in `index.css` (~line 2794 and ~3117) — the color bug was fixed at both copies since it existed in both, but whether to deduplicate the block itself is an open, undecided question. **`npm run build` confirmed clean by owner** on this final state, 2026-07-08 ("Npm built clean"). **Genuinely still open, carried forward rather than silently dropped:** (1) the row's own on-device Done Check (two schools/two colors/all ten modules/side-by-side) — never performed; (2) the `.tt-edit-btn` duplicate block — undecided; (3) the reference app's fuller token vocabulary (parchment neutral scale, fixed gold accent, shadow/radius/motion scale, `[data-theme="dark"]` block) — entirely unbuilt, this row only fixed the existing blue-based token set's internal consistency and contrast safety, it did not port the richer reference architecture the row's original instructions describe. |
| 24 | Rebuild login page + timetable grid to match reference design | ✅ Complete — code delivered, `npm run build` confirmed clean by owner, on-device Done Check confirmed clean by owner | 2026-07-08 | **Dark mode confirmed out of scope** — no `theme_mode` concept exists anywhere in the schema, only `theme_color`; light-theme values only. **Login page rebuilt** against the reference app's `.att-login-card` pattern: compact brand strip (logo/name only) above a centered white card (`.login-card`, `--radius-xl`, `--shadow-md`, max-width 400px) with icon+title+description header, segmented tab switcher, labeled inputs, full-width primary button, inline error panel. Tab icons are real inline SVGs (viewBox 0 0 24 24, stroke=currentColor, matching the reference app's own `ICONS` convention), scoped to just these two icons — not a pre-emption of Session 25's icon-system port. **Password-visibility toggle added to the staff login form** (owner request, this session) — eye/eye-off SVG button inside the password field, same simple show/hide pattern already precedented in `TeacherDashboard.tsx`'s password-change form; not added to the login-code field, since a login code isn't a password. **Timetable grid rebuilt** against the reference app's `.tbl-wrap`/`.cell` pattern, keeping the existing CSS-grid (div-based) structure rather than switching to `<table>` markup (owner's explicit choice). `.tt-grid-wrapper` is now a rounded, shadowed container; day-header row is a solid brand-color fill with real per-shade contrast text (see bugfix below); filled lesson cells have the reference's hover/focus lift. **"Now" column highlight reserved, not wired** — `.tt-col--now-col`/`.tt-cell--now-col` classes exist in CSS but are not yet referenced by any JSX condition; Session 26's live period widget supplies the state this needs. **Gold-accent decision:** no gold token exists (Session 23 never ported the reference's fuller vocabulary) — owner chose to reuse the brand color for all "now"/highlight treatments rather than introduce a new fixed accent token. **Four real pre-existing/newly-introduced bugs found and fixed this session:** (1) `.form-input:focus` was hardcoding a stale green fallback that never tracked the school's real brand color — now uses `color-mix()` against `--color-brand-600`, app-wide fix, not login-only. (2) `.btn--primary` was hardcoding `color: white` on a `--color-brand-600` background — same white-on-pale-brand-color defect class Session 23 fixed at 24 sites, missed here since `.btn` sits outside the ten prefixed blocks that audit covered; now uses `--color-brand-on`. (3) The day-header's solid `--color-brand-800`/`-700` fill was reusing `--color-brand-on` (computed only against the working 600) for its text color — for gray/mid-tone or legibility-corrected pale brand picks, this can put black text on an 800-shade background at ~2.4:1 contrast, well under WCAG AA. Fixed by adding real `on700`/`on800` fields to `deriveBrandScale()` in `themeService.ts` (computed independently per shade, not inherited from `on`), verified against a 5,000-random-hex-color scan with zero AA failures. (4) The day-header row and its sticky corner cell were not actually sticking on vertical scroll — root cause was `.tt-grid` and `.tt-col--periods` both carrying `overflow: hidden`, which made them the nearest scrolling ancestor for `position: sticky` instead of the real scroll container, `.tt-grid-wrapper`; both `overflow: hidden` declarations were removed, and `.tt-grid-wrapper`'s existing `overflow: auto` now correctly owns both scrolling and corner-radius clipping. **Break-row height mismatch fixed** (Option B, owner-selected over a full CSS-Grid DOM restructure): `.tt-cell--break` and `.tt-cell--break-fill` now share a deterministic, non-wrapping height, since the period-label column's break cell (label + time range, two lines) could previously overflow past the day-column's break-fill cell (one line, "Break" only), misaligning every row below it. **The real fix — converting the grid from independent per-day flex columns to true CSS Grid so row heights are always shared — is flagged as a named follow-up, not silently dropped**, since it's a structural DOM rewrite (~150-200 lines) that deserves its own isolated pass, not a same-session bundle. **Shared `Modal.tsx` (Problem 6.5) pulled forward into this session, owner-approved** — nominally scheduled for Session 27 (itself gated on Session 26), built now because the timetable click-to-popup feature (owner request, this session) needed a real modal pattern rather than a one-off. Matches the reference app's `.modal-ov`/`.modal`/`.modal-x` pattern: backdrop blur, centered card, `popIn` 0.18s animation, focus store-on-open/restore-on-close (focus-trap-lite, not a full Tab-cycle trap — same subset the reference app itself implements), close on backdrop click or Escape. Thin shell only — content is passed as children, this session's timetable popup is its first real consumer. **Timetable read-only tap-to-view wired**: outside edit mode, tapping a filled lesson cell now opens a detail popup (subject/teacher/class/day/period/time), sourced entirely from `ttData.entries` already in memory — no new query. Edit-mode's own tap semantics are untouched (the two paths are mutually exclusive on `editMode`). **Conflict detail added to the popup, including which specific class the clash is with** (owner follow-up request, extended to also handle same-teacher-different-subject collisions): when the tapped entry is in `conflictIds`, a second in-memory lookup finds the colliding entry by matching `staff_id` (not the denormalized `staff_name` string) + `day_of_week` + `period_id` against a different `class_node_id` — deliberately independent of the already-computed `conflictIds` Set to avoid a declaration-order coupling in the component. The popup then names the actual colliding class and subject (e.g. "X is also teaching Y to Z at this same time"), with a generic double-booked fallback message if the specific counterpart entry somehow isn't found. **Also done this session, not originally scoped but done while already deep in the CSS file at owner's request:** deduplicated a ~295-line byte-for-byte duplicate `tt-*` CSS block (flagged, undecided, in Session 23's log) — confirmed via full diff that the first occurrence was the strict subset before deleting it, preserving the second copy's one genuinely new addition (`.tt-print-btn` + its `@media print` rule). **Verification performed:** brace/paren balance and `esbuild` parse-clean on every changed file, checked after every edit, not just once at the end; full JSX-class-to-CSS-rule gap check on both files, zero gaps outside the deliberately-reserved `now-col` hooks; a 5,000-sample random-hex-color scan specifically for the `on700`/`on800` contrast fix; **owner has run `npm run build` directly and confirmed it is clean; owner has also confirmed the on-device Done Check (side-by-side visual comparison against the reference app) is clean.** Both §0.2 requirements are now satisfied — this row is fully closed, not conditionally closed. **Files delivered:** `src/index.css` (login section rebuilt; `.form-input:focus`/`.btn--primary`/day-header-contrast/sticky-header bugfixes; timetable grid section restyled; break-row height fix; shared Modal CSS added; duplicate block removed), `src/routes/LoginPage.tsx` (card layout, inline SVG tab icons, password-visibility toggle), `src/components/Modal.tsx` (new, shared, Problem 6.5), `src/services/themeService.ts` (`on700`/`on800` added to `deriveBrandScale()` and `applyBrandTheme()`), `src/routes/TimetableModule.tsx` (read-only tap-to-view popup wired, conflict-detail lookup with named colliding class matched on `staff_id`, header doc comment updated to describe the new behavior). |
