Emoji-to-icon conversion is functionally complete and ready for on-device visual validation. |\n
| 26 | Build live "current period, all classes" widget above timetable | ✅ Complete — `npm run build` confirmed clean by owner, on-device Done Check confirmed clean by owner | 2026-07-09 | **New pure function `getCurrentPeriodState(data, classNodes, now?)` added to `src/services/timetableService.ts`**, deliberately built on the same day/time-window checks as the existing `getCurrentPeriod()` (used by `TeacherDashboard.tsx`'s "today's lesson" header) so the two can never disagree about what period it currently is — this widget calls the new function rather than re-deriving period boundaries independently. Returns a `LivePeriodState` discriminated on `status`: `before_school` / `in_period` / `break` / `after_school` / `non_school_day`, plus `currentPeriod`, `secondsRemaining`, `progressFraction`, a `liveGrid` (every class's entry for the current period, sourced from already-loaded `TimetableData`/`ClassNode[]`, no new query), and an `upcoming` list of remaining periods today. **New file `src/components/LivePeriodWidget.tsx`**: collapsed banner (live dot, status text, live countdown, expand toggle) that ticks every second off `Date.now()` against already-loaded data — no network calls per §4.1 — and, when expanded (only possible during `in_period`/`break`), shows the per-class live grid plus the upcoming-periods list. Respects `prefers-reduced-motion` globally via `index.css` (dot pulse and expand/collapse both fall back to non-animated states there), so the component itself doesn't branch on it. **Wired into `src/routes/TimetableModule.tsx`**: rendered above the grid wrapper (hidden in edit mode); the widget's resolved state is surfaced via an `onStateChange` callback into a `currentPeriodState` value held alongside the existing `liveState`, and that one shared state is threaded into `TimetableGrid` as `nowColState` to drive the previously-reserved `.tt-col--now-col`/`.tt-cell--now-col` classes (Session 24 left these reserved but unwired) — one shared source of truth for "what period is live right now," not a second independent derivation that could drift from the widget's own display. **CSS**: new `.lpw-*` block added to `src/index.css` (banner/dot/countdown/progress-bar/expanded-panel/grid/upcoming-list), modeled on the existing `.dash-schedule-*` precedent from `TeacherDashboard.tsx` rather than introducing a divergent visual pattern; the previously-reserved now-col rules were left in place and are now actually referenced. New `chevron-down` icon added to `src/components/icons/index.tsx` for the expand toggle (rotated 180° via CSS for collapse, rather than adding a near-duplicate `chevron-up` SVG). **Two bugs found and fixed this session, after initial build:** (1) three JSX text nodes in `LivePeriodWidget.tsx` were using literal `\uXXXX` escape sequences (em dash, middle dot) typed directly into bare JSX text rather than inside a JS string literal — JSX does not interpret `\uXXXX` escapes outside of actual JS strings, so they rendered as visible backslash-u codes instead of the intended characters; fixed by replacing with the real unicode characters directly. (2) the expanded panel could open below the fold with content taller than the remaining viewport and no way to reach it — root cause was twofold: no scroll cue on expand, and (after adding one) the browser's native `scrollIntoView` sliding the widget's top underneath the app's `position: sticky` header (`.app-header`, which `scrollIntoView` has no concept of), clipping the banner and first grid row; fixed with a `rootRef` + effect calling `scrollIntoView({ behavior: 'smooth', block: 'start' })` on expand, `scroll-margin-top` on `.lpw-root` sized to the sticky header's height so the browser stops short of it instead of tucking the widget behind it, and `max-height`/`overflow-y: auto` plus bottom padding/`scroll-padding-block` on `.lpw-expanded` so very short viewports get a working internal scroll with breathing room at the last row instead of a flush, clipped-looking cutoff. **Verification performed:** brace/paren balance and parse-clean checked after every edit; the widget correctly handles all five states (before/in-period/break/after/non-school-day) rather than only "ongoing"; ticking observed across multiple real minutes, not a single glance; **owner has run `npm run build` directly and confirmed it is clean; owner has also confirmed the on-device Done Check is clean**, including the two post-build bugfixes above. Both §0.2 requirements are satisfied — this row is fully closed. **Files delivered:** `src/services/timetableService.ts` (`getCurrentPeriodState()` + `LiveClassEntry`/`LivePeriodState` types added), `src/components/LivePeriodWidget.tsx` (new), `src/components/icons/index.tsx` (`chevron-down` added), `src/routes/TimetableModule.tsx` (widget imported and rendered, shared `currentPeriodState` computed and threaded into `TimetableGrid` as `nowColState`, now-col classes actually applied, header doc comment updated), `src/index.css` (`.lpw-*` block added; now-col reserved-block comment updated to note it's wired; `scroll-margin-top`/expanded-panel scroll fixes). |

| 29 | Build term/session progress card with holiday-aware auto-extend/revert | ✅ Complete | 2026-07-10 | **Resumed a third time, closed out.** Prior passes (2026-07-07, 2026-07-09, earlier 2026-07-10) built the visual spec (today-pin, holiday notches, break-impact summary), corrected the extension-eligibility signal against the real `attendanceService.ts` (`qualifiesForExtension`), and wired `<TermProgressCard />` into `AdminDashboard.tsx`/`TeacherDashboard.tsx` and confirmed `GuardianPortal.tsx` compatible via `tsc --strict` — see prior entries and §11 for that detail, unrepeated here. **This pass's own finding and fix:** owner reported the term-progress card's footer ("Day 100 of 179") and the attendance summary card ("72 school days") showed disagreeing numbers for the same term, with no unit on either to explain the gap. Root cause: `loadTermProgress()`'s `totalDays`/`elapsedDays`/`remainingDays` were calendar-day counts (`daysBetweenInclusive()` over the raw date span — weekends and holidays included), while the attendance card's count came from `getSchoolDayMap()` — a genuinely different, and per Problem 6.7's own PRD wording ("elapsed/total/remaining school-week counts... not a hardcoded 2"), the wrong denominator for this card to begin with. **Fix:** `portalService.ts` renamed the ambiguous fields to explicit `totalCalendarDays`/`elapsedCalendarDays`/`remainingCalendarDays`, added real `totalSchoolDays`/`elapsedSchoolDays`/`remainingSchoolDays` (via the same `getSchoolDayMap()` the attendance card itself uses, so the two can never drift apart again), and added `schoolDaysPerWeek` (real `school_week_days` count, not hardcoded). `percentElapsed`/`positionPercent` deliberately kept calendar-based (so the bar fills at a constant real-time rate and doesn't jump unevenly around holiday clusters). `TermProgressCard.tsx` gained a 3-way **School days / All days / Weeks** toggle (defaults to School days, per owner discussion — the most directly useful "real teaching time left" figure) rather than silently picking one number and hiding the other; weeks derived client-side as `schoolDays / schoolDaysPerWeek`, not a hardcoded ÷5/÷7. **Separately, same day: a real `npm run build` failure was found and fixed in `settingsService.ts`** (unrelated file, Problem 6.6/row 28's own territory, not new scope for this problem) — `loadStaffPreferences()`'s `.select(STAFF_PREFERENCES_COLUMNS)` passed a `string`-typed variable rather than a literal, which broke Supabase's typed-client column-shape inference and produced a `TS2352` cast error blocking the build entirely. Fixed by inlining the literal column list (matching every other `.select()` call's own convention in that file) and removing the now-unused constant, which would otherwise have failed `noUnusedLocals` in its place. This fix was a build-level blocker affecting the whole app, not a Problem 6.7 code change — logged here because it's what unblocked this row's own `npm run build` gate; see row 28 for that file's still-open alarm-firing criterion, untouched by this fix. **Verification this pass:** brace/paren/bracket balance, `esbuild` parse, and a 0-gap class diff on `portalService.ts`/`TermProgressCard.tsx`/`index.css` (3 new toggle classes, 0 removed); a real `tsc --strict` typecheck of `settingsService.ts` against a shim of its one import confirmed the `TS2352` fully resolved with zero errors touching `StaffPreferences`/`loadStaffPreferences`. **Both of this problem's own outstanding gates are now confirmed, not assumed: `npm run build` reported clean by the owner, and the on-device Done Check performed and confirmed** — insert a school-day holiday inside the current term, notch/remaining-count/extended-end-date update without reload; delete it, card reverts; toggle confirmed showing the correct, attendance-card-matching school-day count. **Files changed this pass:** `src/services/portalService.ts`, `src/components/TermProgressCard.tsx`, `src/index.css` (Problem 6.7); `src/services/settingsService.ts` (unrelated build-blocker fix, logged under row 28's own file history too). Every call failed at the database with a hard Postgres `42703` error, silently caught into the function's own error branch — not an RLS issue, not an empty-data issue, an actually-invalid query that had (per the master plan) never been on-device tested before this session. **Fix:** rebuilt `loadClassNodes()` to fetch the real columns (`parent_id`, `sort_order`) and compute hierarchy client-side via a parent-walk — grouping rows by `parent_id`, sorting each sibling group by `sort_order` (meaningful only within a group, not as a flat cross-tree order), then walking from roots down to produce a correctly nested, depth-annotated flat list. This is the same map-to-parent-walk approach the master plan documents for `timetableService.ts`'s `buildClassTree()` — reused, not reinvented. `ClassNodeOption.level` replaced with a computed `depth` field; the one consumer (the class `<select>`'s indentation in `AnnouncementsModule.tsx`) updated to match. Verified via an isolated strict `tsc` extraction of just the new walk/grouping logic — zero errors. **Verification performed throughout, matching prior rows' discipline:** `esbuild` clean parses on every touched file after every edit; brace/paren/bracket balance checked at each checkpoint; full bidirectional classname↔CSS diffs for both `stu-*` (202 JSX classNames, 1 known template-literal false positive, 0 real gaps) and `ann-*` (86/86 exact match before this session's edits, 4 new classNames added and closed, 0 real gaps after); line-by-line diffs against the owner's uploads confirming every change is additive, nothing unrelated touched. **Both of this row's own Definition-of-Done gates now genuinely confirmed, not assumed: owner reported `npm run build` clean, and performed the on-device Done Check — confirmed clean**, including the class-picker fix. **Files changed this session:** `src/routes/StudentsModule.tsx`, `src/services/printStudentIdCards.ts` (new), `src/routes/AnnouncementsModule.tsx`, `src/services/announcementsService.ts`, `src/App.tsx` (`schoolName`/`schoolLogoUrl` threading), `src/index.css`. | 47 | Design session: Scheme of Work system (Problem 8.6) | ✅ Design complete, schema not yet migrated | 2026-07-12 | Owner-requested net-new scope, researched before proposing structure (not invented cold): confirmed the standard Nigerian scheme-of-work shape — term-long, per-subject-per-class plan broken into weekly rows (topic/objectives/activities/resources), submitted by the subject teacher and reviewed by HOD/admin at the start of each term, content pulled from the WAEC/NECO or NERDC syllabus — and the operational pattern comparable SIS platforms use (SOW broken into weeks, coverage tracked against it, visible to admin, not just filed once and forgotten). Two-table schema (`schemes_of_work` shell anchored to `teaching_assignment_id`, reusing Problem 8.1's identity-resolution fix with no new bug surface; `scheme_of_work_weeks` carrying per-week content and `coverage_status`) documented in full under Problem 8.6 and master plan §7.15a. Three design decisions locked with the owner: **(1)** submit → approve/return workflow required, not self-publish — matches the researched norm, with `returned` as a revise-and-resubmit path (required note), never a silent terminal rejection. **(2)** weekly coverage tracking in scope for first launch, not deferred — per-week status, not a single blanket flag, since that granularity is what makes the feature a live pacing tool rather than a static filed document. **(3)** deliberately decoupled from Problem 8.4's Assessment Window tables (content pacing vs. scoring/weighting are separate concerns, no FK relationship) and, despite being independently buildable in parallel, sequenced **after** 8.4's Sessions 45–50 close by explicit owner resourcing decision rather than run alongside it. | 48 | Design session: Higher-institution academic model — universities, polytechnics, colleges of education (Problem 8.7); Tier 8 re-sequenced | ✅ Design complete, schema not yet migrated | 2026-07-12 | Owner-flagged, then confirmed the real first enrolling school is a higher institution, not secondary — re-sequences Tier 8's remaining build priority (8.4/8.6 paused, not dropped, per §0.1's updated Tier 8 note). Researched before proposing structure, not invented cold: confirmed Nigerian NUC/NBTE/NCCE academic norms differ meaningfully by institution type (5.0 vs. 4.0 CGPA scales, NCCE's fixed 40:60 CA:Exam split, at least two distinct CGPA rollup methods and two retake-treatment models in real use, and a vendor-confirmed "10 variations" finding on probation policy alone) — directly informing the decision to make every academic-standing rule, grading scale, and CGPA formula school-configurable rather than hardcoded. Five-phase schema (Institutional Hierarchy → Course Catalogue & Registration → Per-Course Attendance → Scoring/GPA/CGPA → Carryover/Spillover/Academic Standing) documented in full under Problem 8.7, with `course_registrations` established as the new central anchor object every later phase reads from — the same "one anchor, everything reads from it" discipline already used for Problem 8.1's identity fix. Five Phase-0 decisions requiring explicit owner sign-off before Session 54 begins: institution-category branch at onboarding; GPA rollup method + retake treatment (flagged as the single highest-stakes value in the whole problem); programme levels as administrative metadata, not a live daily container (deliberately **not** reusing `class_nodes`, avoiding a repeat of the Problem 6.1 "inert flag becomes a landmine" pattern); no separate carryover-roster screen; soft, never-hard-locked per-category gating. §3.16 flagged for amendment — `school_type` becomes load-bearing for the first time, a locked-architecture change that must be recorded in §12, not discovered later in a diff. New permission keys (`academics.course_registration.manage`/`.view_own`, `academics.curriculum.manage`, `academics.institution_structure.manage`, `academics.cgpa_policy.manage`, `academics.academic_standing.review`) and a full §8/§10 roadmap entry (Sessions 54–63) added same session. **Not yet done, at the time of this entry:** master-plan schema acceptance (later formalized as §7.27 — §7.16 was this session's placeholder before the actual master plan was consulted; see row 51), Phase 0 sign-off, migration, or any UI — design artifact only, per this PRD's own rule that a checkbox is never ticked ahead of a real build+on-device confirmation. | 68a | SESSION 68a (owner-initiated follow-up, raised at SESSION 68's own close-out — not part of SESSION 68's original card, and not the same thing as the real, separate, still-unstarted SESSION 69 card below, despite having been informally labeled "session 69" in working files for a time): `programme_id`/`programme_level_id` added to `course_offerings` (§7.27.2 amendment, both nullable — see master-plan addition record below for the full rejected-alternatives reasoning), `OfferingsTab`'s form gains a Programme → Level picker with a curriculum-filtered course dropdown and an explicit "show all courses" override, the offerings list itself regrouped from a flat list into Programme → Level → "Unassigned", and cohort-clash detection (`findCohortConflicts()`/`cohortConflictMessage()`, three severities — core/elective/unknown, all soft confirm-to-proceed warnings, never a hard block) wired into `TimetableModuleHigherEd.tsx`'s slot-save path alongside the pre-existing lecturer-clash check. **The gap that motivated this work:** SESSION 68's lecturer-conflict check alone only catches "one person can't be two places" — it says nothing about two required classes for the same cohort of students landing at the same time, a real scheduling failure mode a course-offering timetable needs to catch and Session 68's original card never scoped. **A real, then-broken build error found and fixed as part of closing this out, not left for the owner to discover:** `courseOfferingTimetableService.ts`'s `loadOfferingsForLecturer()` passes `courseAttendanceService.ts`'s own local `CourseOffering` type through as this file's re-exported `CourseOffering` (= `settingsService.ts`'s shape); once that shape required the two new fields, the assignment stopped being structurally valid — reproduced with a real isolated `tsc` error (`TS2322`) before fixing, not assumed from reading the code alone. Fixed by adding both fields to `courseAttendanceService.ts`'s local interface, its query select, and its row mapping. **Design decisions, in order:** (1) migration two nullable columns, no backfill — an offering predating this session and a genuinely shared/university-wide offering with no single owning cohort are both real, permanent null states, not a gap to close later; (2) considered and rejected deriving programme/level live via `programme_curriculum` at read time instead of adding columns — rejected because it doesn't resolve the one-to-many ambiguity for a course shared across programmes, and because a mid-session curriculum redesign would silently reshuffle which group an already-taught offering appears under, which is wrong (an offering's cohort is fixed at creation/teaching time, not a live reflection of today's curriculum) — a deliberate, recorded departure from this problem's own general "derive, don't duplicate" preference, flagged as such rather than applied by default; (3) every curriculum read scoped to `(course_id, programme_level_id, term_id)` together, never `course_id` alone, since joining on `course_id` alone fans out when a course appears under more than one programme/level/term — the exact bug already found once in `courseRegistrationService.ts`'s `loadCurriculum()` (SESSION 57), restated here as a standing rule for this table family rather than re-derived from scratch; (4) cohort-clash severity: both-core → `core`, either-elective → `elective`, either-side not on the curriculum at all → a genuinely distinct third `unknown` state, not defaulted to `elective` — defaulting would assert a false fact (known-elective) instead of the true one (impact undetermined), and this also surfaces real curriculum-mapping gaps to the admin at the moment they're actually relevant; (5) all three severities are soft, confirm-to-proceed warnings, deliberately not differentiated by blocking behavior, only by wording, so the admin has one consistent interaction to learn; (6) the pre-save check (lecturer clash + cohort clash, one combined `confirm()` dialog, not two stacked) required building a synthetic not-yet-saved entry to check against already-loaded ones, since neither `findLecturerConflicts()` nor `findCohortConflicts()` previously accepted anything but already-saved rows — `rangesOverlap()` was exported from `courseOfferingTimetableService.ts` (previously private) rather than re-derived a second time in the component, matching this file's own "one definition" discipline. | ✅ Confirmed | 2026-07-18 | Every file typechecked clean in isolation against real stubs, each stub's signature confirmed directly against the real file rather than guessed (including catching and fixing two of the assistant's own initially-wrong stubs — a loose `isSchoolDay` return shape and a missing `loadCourseOfferings`/`Input` import — before trusting a clean result). Brace/paren/bracket balance confirmed on every touched file against its original uploaded state. Zero classname↔CSS gaps confirmed bidirectionally for the new `set-offerings-*` grouping family. Owner ran the migration successfully and confirmed on-device that the Offerings tab form shows the new Programme/Level pickers as expected (an earlier "no changes visible" report was traced to a stale deploy/cache on the owner's end, not a code defect — confirmed once the owner re-checked). **Not yet independently on-device confirmed by the owner as of this entry:** the offerings-list regrouping display, and the cohort-clash confirm dialog actually firing for a real overlapping pair (the dialog is conditional — it only appears when a save genuinely clashes with an already-loaded entry, so a school with no current clashes will correctly see nothing different on the timetable page itself). Migrated and confirmed live. **Item 2 — student self-write RLS on `course_registrations`:** real schema gap found and closed first — `course_offerings` had no "open for registration" concept anywhere; researched real registrar/SIS practice (per-term add/drop windows, not per-offering flags) before designing, then added nullable `registration_opens_at`/`registration_closes_at` to `terms` (`session69_registration_window_migration.sql`) rather than inventing a per-offering boolean. Write policy scoped to: own `student_id` only, own current placement's programme/session/term via `student_programme_placements` (`is_current = true`), and the term's registration window open (or unset = unenforced). Confirmed live via a real `pg_policies` read; the live positive/negative test was deferred to "once the UI exists" (owner's own call), completed later this session — see below. **Item 3 — student-facing registration screen, including a real design wrong turn caught and corrected before shipping:** first attempt built a standalone `StudentSelfRegistrationPortal` with its own `loadCurrentStudentId()` identity resolution in `courseRegistrationService.ts` — found, once `GuardianPortal.tsx`/`guardianService.ts`/`portalService.ts` were actually supplied, to duplicate (and do *worse* than) `loadPortalIdentity()`, which the real portal shell already calls correctly with proper `school_id` scoping the first attempt lacked. **Fully reverted** (`courseRegistrationService.ts` confirmed byte-identical to the original upload afterward) and rebuilt correctly as a `'registration'` tab inside `GuardianPortal.tsx` itself, gated `isHigherEd && isStudentSelf` (never shown to a guardian — the client-side mirror of the RLS policy's own `current_actor_type() = 'student'` check), reusing `RegistrationStep` (newly exported from `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx`, otherwise byte-for-byte unchanged) rather than a second reimplementation of its curriculum/credit-load/carryover logic. **A real, undocumented conflict surfaced and resolved mid-build, not silently overridden:** this reuse required first discovering that `registerStudent()` is not an additive insert but a full replace-diff (loads existing registrations, silently withdraws anything not in the new set) — safe only because `RegistrationStep`'s own UI always pre-loads and pre-checks existing state before building the save set; a leaner from-scratch student view would have silently reproduced a real data-loss bug. Separately, building this also surfaced that SESSION 57 had explicitly documented registration as **admin-only, not self-service** — a direct conflict with this card's own instruction, caught by the assistant before shipping rather than assumed compatible; owner resolved as **both, side by side** (admin-gated flow untouched; student self-service additive), recorded in `session69_masterplan_amendment_admin_and_self_service.md` and the §7.27.2 SESSION 69 amendment. `npm run build` clean confirmed by the owner for this item. **Item 4 — the promotion run, `run_programme_promotion()`/`record_manual_promotion_decision()`:** SESSION 50's real secondary-school precedent (auto-promote at/above cutoff, explicit review list below, never silent retention, one history row per decision) translated onto the real higher-ed tables, not reinvented — with one real design fork confirmed rather than assumed: SESSION 50's cross-student sequencing gate ("destination class must be cleared first") does **not** apply here, owner-confirmed, since `student_programme_placements_one_current_uniq` already enforces one current placement per student with no shared-container dependency between students the way `class_node_id` has. CGPA-only eligibility for this version (owner's explicit choice; `max_carryover_courses` is read from the rule but not yet enforced, flagged as a distinct later follow-up). **Three real bugs found and fixed across four SQL revisions, the last two hit only on real data, not the dry-run:** (1) `fix2` — bare `outcome` reference inside `RETURN QUERY`, ambiguous against the function's own `returns table` OUT parameter of the same name (`42702`); (2) `fix3` — the identical bug class recurred on a *different* OUT-parameter name (`student_id`, inside a `RETURNING` clause) because the first fix patched only the reported symptom instead of systematically checking all six OUT-parameter names against every line — the second attempt this time enumerated all six before shipping; (3) `fix4`/`fix5` — a genuine logic bug, not a naming collision: the original function computed `evaluated` and both its dependent writes (retire old placement, insert new one) as three data-modifying CTEs in one `WITH` clause, assuming textual order was execution order. A real `23505` unique-constraint violation (`student_programme_placements_one_current_uniq`) on real data proved this false; researched directly against Postgres's own documentation and a committer's (Tom Lane) own account before re-fixing rather than guessing a second time — confirmed multiple data-modifying CTEs in one `WITH` share one snapshot and cannot see each other's writes, so a `JOIN`-based dependency (the first attempted fix) does not force real ordering either. Corrected by abandoning the multi-CTE-write approach entirely: `evaluated` materializes into a real temporary table (`promotion_evaluation`), and the `UPDATE`/`INSERT`/`INSERT` sequence runs as genuinely separate, sequential `plpgsql` statements — confirmed via the same research pass that ordinary sequential statements in a function body **do** see each other's committed effects, unlike CTEs. Re-run clean on real data after this fix (Idris Ishaq: `no_rule_configured` → `auto_promoted` once a real rule existed, confirmed via a real, non-rolled-back promotion). **A fourth real bug, found only after the owner ran a real manual review through the shipped UI, not caught by any prior test:** `record_manual_promotion_decision()`'s `manual_override` path was a silent no-op — audit row written, but the branch that flips the placement and records a destination level only ever matched `'promoted'`/`'graduated'`, so `manual_override` always wrote `to_programme_level_id: null` and never moved the student, functionally indistinguishable from `'repeated'` — confirmed directly against real `programme_promotion_history` data the owner queried (`manual_override` rows uniformly `to_level: null`, `reason: 'Manual review: manual_override'`, matching neither the destination level nor the reason text actually entered in the UI). Owner-confirmed correct semantics: `manual_override` should behave exactly like `promoted` (flip placement, insert new one at the chosen level), distinguished from `promoted` only in the audit trail's `decision` column. Fixed at **three layers**, found and fixed in sequence, not all at once: the DB function (`fix5`, `p_decision in ('promoted', 'manual_override', 'graduated')`), `settingsService.ts`'s `recordManualPromotionDecision()` (same `'promoted'`-only hardcoding on both the validation check and the payload), and `SettingsModule.tsx`'s `handleReviewDecision()` (the identical hardcoding a third time, at both its own validation check and payload). **A fifth bug, purely visual, found from an on-device screenshot after the logic fix:** the "Repeat this level"/"Manual override" buttons rendered with no visible label — root-caused to `set-save-btn` (a filled brand-colored class pairing background+text color) with an inline `background: transparent` override that stripped only the background, leaving light-on-nothing text; fixed by switching to `set-cancel-btn`, the file's own already-correct secondary-button class every other Cancel/secondary action already uses, rather than patching the color. **A sixth, purely mechanical regression, caught before delivery:** the button-visibility fix was first delivered as a standalone file but never merged back into the base copy being continued from; the very next edit (the three-layer `manual_override` fix) was applied on top of the stale, pre-button-fix base, silently reintroducing the blank-button bug — caught by re-diffing against the actual current working file rather than assumed still-present from memory, and both fixes delivered together in the same file this time. `settingsService.ts`'s pre-existing `StandingTab` "Override → Good standing" button was found to share the identical `background: transparent` defect (not introduced this session) and was explicitly flagged to the owner as out-of-scope rather than silently fixed or silently ignored. Also flagged, not fixed: a Nusaiba Idris/Fatimah Ja'afar-class duplicate-name/blank-matric-number pattern visible in the real promotion-history query output was not investigated this session — out of scope, noted for awareness only. | ✅ Confirmed | 2026-07-19 | Every SQL revision hand-traced against real Postgres documentation/committer statements before re-shipping, not guessed a second or third time (`RETURNING`/`WITH`-clause snapshot semantics both independently verified via web search before the corresponding fix). Every code file's diff checked against the actual prior upload at each step (not assumed from memory), including one explicit full revert (`courseRegistrationService.ts`/`CourseRegistrationModule.tsx`'s abandoned standalone-portal attempt) confirmed byte-identical to original afterward. Brace/paren/bracket balance confirmed after every edit across `settingsService.ts`, `SettingsModule.tsx`, `CourseRegistrationModule.tsx`, `GuardianPortal.tsx`, and every SQL revision, against each file's own known pre-existing baseline (not a fresh zero each time). Isolated `tsc` typechecks run after every edit, every remaining error confirmed to belong to the same pre-existing environmental-noise classes (`TS2307`/`TS7006`/`TS7026`/`TS2874`/`TS2741`, missing-module and implicit-any from the sandbox's stubbed `../lib/supabase` and untyped Supabase client) already present at the same or equivalent lines in the owner's own unedited uploads — zero new real error classes introduced by any of this session's edits. **Both of SESSION 69's own Done Check halves independently confirmed by the owner, not assumed from a clean build alone:** the owner reported `npm run build` clean multiple times across the session (each real code change re-confirmed, not a single blanket claim), and, in this session's final message, reported the `manual_override` fix effected and **"on-device testing works perfectly"** — covering the registration tab (student self-service, RLS-enforced, admin path untouched and confirmed still separately live) and the full promotion-review flow (Promote / Repeat this level / Manual override, all three buttons now visibly labeled and functionally correct, confirmed against real `programme_promotion_history` rows with real, non-null destination levels and real reason text). SESSION 69pre's own Done Check (a new registration and a new curriculum entry both persisting non-null `term_id` through the normal UI) was independently confirmed via SQL earlier in the session, closing SESSION 67's own long-outstanding Done Check as a side effect — SESSION 67 (PRD Log rows 67/67b) can now be revisited and rounded up. |
| 70 | SESSION 70: Remita payment gateway integration, receipt generation, fee-based access gating — **fee-model foundation, Vault credential design, and two real wiring bugs closed this pass; the Remita Edge Function itself, receipt generation, and fee-status gating have not been built yet.** Researched Remita's real integration shape (RRR-based invoice flow, hash/OAuth2 auth generations, separate polling-based status endpoint) and real Nigerian university fee precedent (flat per-session/semester, never per-course) before designing anything. **Fee model:** `fee_assignments.target_type` gains `'programme_level'`, `programme_id`+`programme_level_id` added together (matching `course_offerings`/`programme_curriculum`'s own confirmed pairing convention); per-student variation via existing `fee_discounts`/`installment_plans`, no new override column. `payment_methods_enabled.method` gains `'remita'`. **Credential storage:** Supabase Vault, chosen over application-level envelope encryption and an external KMS after an explicit, honestly-stated threat-model comparison (nothing stops a compromised Edge Function either way) — `get_remita_credentials(school_id)`, a service-role-gated `security definer` function, is the only path to a decrypted secret. Full reasoning for both decisions, plus the payment-trigger research (student self-service only, matching every real institutional precedent found), recorded in master plan §12. **Two real wiring bugs found and fixed:** (1) `App.tsx`'s `FinanceModule` call site was missing `schoolType` (every sibling module already receives it), so `FinanceModule.tsx`'s own `isHigherEd` was always `false` — the cause of an earlier "npm built cleanly but the UI saw no changes" report; (2) a second, independent bug found only by comparing directly against `App.tsx`: `FinanceModule.tsx`'s own local `isHigherInstitutionSchoolType()` checked a drifted string set (`'college'` instead of `'college_of_education'`/`'other'`) that would have kept silently misclassifying the real enrolled school (`school_type = 'other'`) even after bug (1) was fixed. Both corrected; not yet re-checked whether `ResultsModule.tsx`/`CourseRegistrationModule.tsx`/`CourseAttendanceModule.tsx` carry the same locally-copied drift, since those files weren't available this session — flagged, not assumed fine. **Secondary-isms swapped inside Finance only** (owner's explicit "Reading A" scope, not a platform-wide audit): term→semester copy in the Assignments tab and assignment form, PTA-Levy example text → Tuition/Acceptance-Fee examples in the Fee Heads tab. **One live, independent bug also fixed:** `FinanceModule.tsx`'s assignment-save was sending both `class_node_id` and `student_id` regardless of `target_type`, causing 2 real bad rows in production — fixed at the save layer plus a one-time data-fix update for the 2 existing rows. **`payments.paid_at` semantics bug found and fixed while designing the Remita insert path:** was `not null default now()`, wrong for a pending, not-yet-paid RRR — made nullable, `Payment` type corrected to `string | null`, `loadStudentPayments()`'s ordering switched from `paid_at` to `created_at` (nulls-first-in-descending would have wrongly surfaced pending payments above recent completed ones), and the payment-row date render guarded to show "Pending" instead of the `new Date(null)` 1970 epoch date. Full migration SQL (`session70_migration.sql`) written and **run against the real database, confirmed via all four verification queries the owner ran and pasted back:** `fee_assignments_target_type_check` includes `'programme_level'`; `payment_methods_enabled_method_check` includes `'remita'`; `payments.paid_at` is nullable with no default; `get_remita_credentials(uuid)` correctly denies `authenticated` and allows only `service_role`. | ⚠️ Partial | 2026-07-19 | `npm run build` confirmed clean and on-device confirmed for the fee-model/copy/wiring-bug work specifically (Balances, Assignments, Fee Heads tabs all confirmed correct for the real higher-ed school after the `schoolType`/string-set fixes). Schema/Vault migration confirmed live via direct SQL read, not merely assumed run. Per this log's own rule, this row stays ⚠️ Partial, not ✅ — SESSION 70's own named Done Check (a real sandbox/test-mode Remita payment, verified server-side, a receipt generated and viewable, and registration/transcript access confirmed blocked-then-unblocked) has not been attempted.
